### the api seam

F803 | mobile-app-core | eas.json:10 vs src/services/api.ts:11,242 | **EVERY EAS BUILD POINTS AT THE WRONG BASE URL.** `build.base.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL = "https://api-production-a56f.up.railway.app"` — no `/api/v1`. The api client treats that value as the COMPLETE base (its own default is `http://localhost:3000/api/v1`, and the health probe strips `/api/v1` off the end to find `/health`), and the NestJS app sets `setGlobalPrefix('api/v1')` (apps/api/src/main.ts:181). So a development/preview/production EAS build calls `https://…/search`, `https://…/users/me` and 404s on everything. It has not bitten because the day-to-day sim reads `.env.local`, which carries the suffix. The base-URL truth lives in four places (eas.json, .env.example, .env.local, the DEFAULT_API_URL constant) and three of them agree. | one place: put the `/api/v1` suffix INSIDE the client (`baseURL = ${origin}/api/v1`) and let the env carry only an origin; then a missing suffix is impossible | FIXED (D38) — eas.json's `build.base.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` now carries the `/api/v1` global prefix, so every EAS profile (development/development-device/preview/production) calls a real base instead of 404ing on the origin. The agreement between the four homes of that URL is now ASSERTED, not trusted: services/api-base-url.spec.ts checks every EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL in eas.json, the .env.example template, and the client's DEFAULT_API_URL fallback. RED-PROVED: restoring the suffix-less value fails 'every EAS build profile points at a complete base URL' | read main.ts prefix + all four env homes

F804 | mobile-app-core | src/services/api.ts:304-383 | **THE ONE CLIENT HAS NO 401 SEAM.** The response interceptor handles 403+ENTITLEMENT_REQUIRED and >=500, and nothing else. A 401 (expired/revoked Clerk session, or `getAuthToken` returning null because the token resolver threw — :265-271 swallows that into `logger.warn` + null) falls through to the generic failure modal, and the app keeps making unauthenticated requests forever with no sign-out and no re-auth prompt. The AuthProvider's resolver is the only refresh path and it has no retry. LADDER — compensates-for: nothing; the case was simply never written. bedrock: Clerk owns refresh, but the CLIENT owns "the refresh failed, now what". rederive: one `on401` branch beside the 403 branch — clear the query cache, announce through a session-lapse store the way `entitlementLapseStore` does for entitlement, and let the route coordinator show sign-in. red-team: "Clerk auto-refreshes so 401 can't happen" — it can (revoked session, deleted user, clock skew, the resolver's own catch), and today the failure mode is an app that silently degrades to anonymous. | FIXED (D38) — ONE explicit 401 seam. `resolveApiFailureAction` (services/api-failure-policy.ts) closes the outcome set — session_lapse / entitlement_lapse / service_issue / caller_owned — and the response interceptor now dispatches on it instead of testing two ad-hoc conditions and letting 401 fall through. A 401 announces through `useSessionLapseStore` (mirroring entitlementLapseStore), tags the error `isSessionLapse`, and AppRouteCoordinator makes the SERVER's verdict win over Clerk's cached `isSignedIn`: authStatus becomes 'signed_out', the react-query cache is cleared, and the route is sign-in. Silent-anonymous is no longer representable. The lapse clears on a real session change (signed out, or a new user id). MUTATION-PROVED: api-failure-policy.spec.ts, 8 pass, incl. 'THE TRANSITION: a 401 flips the app into an explicit signed-out state'; deleting the `status === 401` case fails 2 cases including the store transition | read the whole interceptor + AuthProvider token resolver

F805 | mobile-app-core | src/services/api.ts:281-284,315 + src/services/users.ts:88-91 | CALLER-MUST-REMEMBER: `suppressSystemStatus` / `suppressErrorLog` are read off `error.config` as EXTRA keys smuggled through the axios request config. Nothing types the call site into remembering them, and the consequence of forgetting is invisible in code review — a background poll that 500s raises the app-wide "Service temporarily unavailable" banner. `usersService.getMe` remembers; most callers don't. | make it explicit at the seam: `api.get(url, quiet())` or a second exported client (`backgroundApi`) whose interceptor never reports | PROPOSED | grep suppressSystemStatus across src

F806 | mobile-app-core | src/services/api.ts:396-413 | The banner-recovery health probe is registered by a MODULE-SCOPE `useSystemStatusStore.subscribe` inside the api module. It works, and the design note is honest, but it means importing the api client starts a subscription with a `setInterval` as a side effect of module evaluation — untestable in the hermetic jest project and impossible to stop. IDEAL-ADJACENT, not ideal: the behavior is right, the ownership is wrong. | move the probe to a mounted listener component beside NetworkStatusListener; the store stays the seam | PROPOSED | read the subscription + systemStatusStore

### build config that cannot do what it says

F807 | mobile-app-core | metro.config.js:24-41 | **DEAD CONFIG POINTING AT FILES THAT DO NOT EXIST.** `config.resolver.alias = { 'react-native$': …/src/shims/reactNativeProxy.js, 'react-native/Libraries/Utilities/codegenNativeComponent': …/src/shims/codegenNativeComponentShim.js, '@rnmapbox/maps': …, 'react-native-svg': … }`. Neither shim file exists (`src/shims/` contains only `codegen-native-component.ts` and `registerCodegenNativeComponent.ts`). The bundle nonetheless builds — because **Metro has no `resolver.alias` option**: it is absent from `metro-config/src/types.js.flow` and from `@expo/metro-config`'s build output (grepped both). So the block is inert; if `alias` ever became real, the app would fail to bundle on the first two entries. A reader today reasonably believes react-native is being proxied through a shim. Related: `.eslintignore:1` ignores `src/polyfills/react-native-codegen.js` — the file is `.ts`. | delete the alias block (or, if the mapbox/svg dedupe is genuinely needed, express it with `resolver.resolveRequest` / `extraNodeModules`, which Metro does support) | FIXED (D38) — the whole `config.resolver.alias` block is DELETED. Re-verified before deleting: `alias` appears nowhere in metro-config/src/types.js.flow, metro-resolver, or @expo/metro-config's build output, so all four entries were inert (and two pointed at shim files that do not exist). A comment records the verified fact and names the levers Metro DOES support (resolver.resolveRequest / extraNodeModules) if the mapbox/svg dedupe ever proves necessary. Also fixed the related stale path: .eslintignore's `src/polyfills/react-native-codegen.js` → `.ts`. PROOF: tsc + the full jest suite green after removal; the alias option does not exist, so nothing could depend on it | grepped metro-config + @expo/metro-config for `alias`; listed src/shims

F808 | mobile-app-core | .eslintrc.js (whole file) + root .eslintrc.js:12 | **NO `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` ANYWHERE IN THE CHAIN.** A 37k-line React Native app with hand-maintained dependency arrays in every runtime hook and zero exhaustive-deps enforcement. The cost is already visible: `src/screens/EntitlementLapseHost.tsx:28` carries a bare `// eslint-disable-line` (no rule name — it disables EVERY rule on that line) to silence a rule that is not even installed, and `src/screens/onboarding/OnboardingTeaser.tsx:62-63` writes a comment explaining that no disable is needed because the plugin is absent. Two files reasoning about a linter that isn't there. LADDER — compensates-for: nothing; it was never added. rederive: this repo's whole methodology is "make the failure a lint error that names the file" (see the ActivityIndicator and Alert.alert bans in this same config, both written for exactly that reason). exhaustive-deps is the same move for the largest defect class in the app. red-team: turning it on will produce hundreds of warnings — so land it as `warn` with `reportOnlyOnChangedFiles` in CI, or scope it to `src/**/runtime/**` first. | add eslint-plugin-react-hooks; delete the two ghost disables | PROPOSED | grepped both .eslintrc.js files

### errors that reach nobody

F810 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/Onboarding.tsx:451-463 | **SILENT LOSS OF THE ONE THING ONBOARDING EXISTS TO COLLECT.** When the user is signed in and `usersService.completeOnboarding` throws, the catch logs a warn, CLEARS the service-issue banner (`clearServiceIssue('global')` — actively hiding the evidence), and marks onboarding complete LOCALLY. The persisted status is now `completed`, so the flow never runs again and the payload — every answer, plus the username claim — is never retried or replayed. The user believes they finished; the server has nothing. There is no outbox and no reconciliation on next launch. LADDER — compensates-for: a real fear, that a network blip strands a user on the last onboarding screen. bedrock: the answers are the product's cold-start signal; losing them silently is worse than a retry. rederive: complete locally AND enqueue the payload for replay (the answers already live in the persisted zustand draft — keep the draft until the server confirms, and re-send on next authenticated launch). What becomes impossible: a completed-locally user whose answers never landed. | keep the draft until server-confirmed; replay on launch | FIXED (D38) — the answers are DURABLE DATA (owner, 2026-08-03) and can no longer be discarded. The catch no longer clears the service-issue banner and no longer marks onboarding complete: it records the exact rejected payload in a PERSISTED outbox (`onboardingStore.pendingServerCompletion`, in `partialize` so it survives a cold start), surfaces the failure through `completionError` (the CTA stays live, so pressing it again retries), and returns false. AppRouteCoordinator replays the payload on the next authenticated launch via `decideOnboardingCompletionReplay`; only a CONFIRMED server write empties the outbox, and it NEVER expires — a payload we cannot land today is still the only copy of the user's answers. RED-PROVED: onboarding-completion-replay.spec.ts drives the REAL persisted store through a REAL failing call, 8 pass; restoring the old catch (completeOnboardingLocally + return true) fails 4, including 'RED — a failing server call does NOT mark onboarding complete' and 'the payload survives VERBATIM, username claim included' | read completeAndEnterApp + onboardingStore.completeOnboardingLocally

F811 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/CameraCaptureHost.tsx:80-84 | `takePictureAsync` failure is swallowed with `catch {}` and the comment "stay live, the user can retry" — but nothing tells the user anything happened. They tap the shutter and the app does nothing, twice, three times. The app has a standard failure surface (`announceFailureIfOnline`) that this bypasses. | announce, or set an inline error state | PROPOSED | read the handler + the modal store's API

F812 | mobile-app-core | src/screens/EntitlementLapseHost.tsx:23-25 | `void access.refresh().then(…)` with NO `.catch`. The probe fires precisely when the server just returned 403 — the likeliest next outcome is that `/users/me` fails too, producing an unhandled promise rejection inside the paywall takeover. | `.catch(() => {})` is not the fix; the honest one is to leave the wall up and log through the crash-reporting seam | PROPOSED | read the effect

F813 | mobile-app-core | src/providers/AuthProvider.tsx:36-38,48-50 + src/services/purchases.ts:93-99 + src/services/push-permission.ts:85-87 + src/screens/onboarding/OnboardingTeaser.tsx:51 | Five independent `console.warn`/`logger.warn` swallow sites in the AUTH+MONEY path, none of which reach `captureHandledError` — the crash-reporting seam App.tsx wires for the mutation chokepoint. A token cache that stops persisting, or an RC configure that fails, is invisible in production. The seam exists and is used in exactly one place. | route these through `captureHandledError` with a `seam:` tag, the way the mutation chokepoint does | PROPOSED | read crash-reporting's exports + every warn site in the auth path

### argued ideal

F819 | mobile-app-core | src/services/purchases.ts (whole file) | **IDEAL, argued.** Build it again today and this is the shape. Every identity mutation chains through ONE promise queue (`enqueueIdentity`), so an interleaved logOut/logIn cannot leave RevenueCat pointing at the wrong user while the app believes otherwise; `purchasePackage` REFUSES unless RC's configured identity equals the caller's signed-in Clerk id, which closes the "user charged, purchase unmappable" hole at its only door; access truth is stated to live in the server ledger and the file refuses to gate on `CustomerInfo`; and a release build carrying a `test_` key disables purchases loudly rather than shipping the Test Store to App Review. The one thing each guard protects is nameable, and each can show RED. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read alongside PurchasesProvider + useAccess

F820 | mobile-app-core | src/store/systemStatusStore.ts + src/providers/NetworkStatusListener.tsx | **IDEAL, argued.** ONE online truth with a single writer: NetInfo writes the store, the store MIRRORS into react-query's `onlineManager` (and is the only writer of it), so banner, search pause/resume, react-query refetch and the dev failure-matrix lever cannot disagree. `subscribeToReconnect` makes the offline→online EDGE a shared primitive instead of five hand-rolled detectors. The `devOfflineOverride` is `__DEV__`-fenced in the setter itself, not at the call site. The foreground re-`fetch` is justified by a named NetInfo failure mode. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read both plus every consumer of isOffline

F821 | mobile-app-core | src/services/device-key.ts | **IDEAL, argued.** A best-effort keychain install id with an explicit "NEVER throws, never blocks a request" contract that the code actually holds; no `Math.random` fallback, because a fabricated weak id would poison the clustering signal it exists to feed (the no-fake-values law, applied one level below where anyone would think to apply it); an in-flight promise so concurrent requests mint once; and a comment explaining why `expo-crypto` is deliberately NOT require()'d (Metro resolves require statically, so a guarded require of an absent module still breaks the bundle). Even the decision to cache a null answer is argued rather than assumed. | — | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read with the api request interceptor

F822 | mobile-app-core | src/store/viewport-subject-store.ts:148-166 | **IDEAL, argued** (and a pattern worth copying): `useViewportSubjectVerdict` exists beside `useViewportSubjectState` because subscribing to the whole state re-rendered every place-name mouth on each settle. ### the services layer (F830–F849)

EXECUTED: `npx jest --silent` from apps/mobile → **51 suites / 480 tests pass, exit 0** — and a worker DIED mid-run (F830).
Every `api.*` path in search/polls/photos/user-lists/messaging was cross-checked against the NestJS controllers:
**all match** (search.controller 7, history 4, polls 15, photos 10, user-lists 21 + public + share, messaging 10). No 404s.
No spec anywhere exercises `services/{search,polls,photos,user-lists,messaging}.ts` or `useSearchRequests.ts`.

F830 | mobile-app-core | src/overlays/panels/save-list-model.spec.ts:12-18 + src/store/saved-membership-store.ts:82,93 | **THE SUITE PRINTS GREEN WHILE A WORKER DIES.** The spec partially mocks `services/user-lists` with three methods; the membership store's 50ms `setTimeout` flush calls `batchMemberships`, which the mock lacks, so the timer fires after teardown and Node prints `TypeError: userListsService.batchMemberships is not a function` — and jest still reports `51 passed`, exit 0. This is CLAUDE.md's own always-green disease inside the instrument that is supposed to catch it: a genuine unhandled rejection is invisible to CI. | add `batchMemberships` to the mock; export `resetSavedMembershipQueue()` and call it in `afterEach`; make an unhandled rejection fail the run | FIXED (D38) — the always-green disease is out of the instrument itself, in three parts. (1) THE LEAK: `save-list-model.spec.ts` mocks `batchMemberships` (the method its import graph reaches, not just the ones it calls) and calls the new `resetSavedMembershipQueue()` in afterEach, which cancels the 50ms flush timer. (2) THE OTHER LEAK, found while fixing this one and NOT in the finding: the transition engine's join-liveness watchdog was never disarmed when a txn left 'joining', so every transition left a live 600ms timer — that was what force-killed the worker in the full run. `advance`/`supersede` now disarm it, `resetTransitionTxnHolderForTest` disarms every armed watchdog (a txn a spec created directly was never in the holder), and the spec calls it in afterEach. (3) THE GATE: `yarn test` now runs `scripts/jest-honest.js`, which streams jest's output and FAILS the run on 'A worker process has failed to exit gracefully', 'A worker process has crashed', 'Cannot log after tests are done', and the force-exit notices. RED-PROVED: with the watchdog leak present, jest exits 0 while the runner exits 1 naming the marker (captured run: 53 suites / 486 tests 'passed', 12 post-teardown logs, jest exit 0, runner exit 1). SURPRISE WORTH RECORDING: in-band (single-file) the crash DOES exit 1, but with 2+ suites the worker death is swallowed ENTIRELY — no message at all, exit 0. The always-green window is wider than the finding described. NOW: 58 suites / 526 tests, exit 0, no markers | reproduced in the jest run

**The always-green class (this territory's highest-value hunt).**

F850 | mobile-app-core | src/perf/ui-frame-sampler.ts:60-79 + PerfScenarioCoordinator.tsx:231-236,946-954 | **A MISSING NATIVE SAMPLER IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A PERFECT 60fps RUN.** `startUiFrameSampler` returns a no-op stop when the module is absent; the only notice is triple-gated `__DEV__ && EXPO_PUBLIC_PERF_UI_SAMPLER_MISSING_LOG==='1' && !hasLoggedOnce` — so on the RELEASE lane, the exact lane the coordinator's own comment says "the 60fps assertion must be measurable on", that log is unreachable BY CONSTRUCTION. Name the defect that makes this instrument show RED: there isn't one. A missing module, a renamed `UI_FRAME_SAMPLER_MODULE_NAME`, a native `start` that no-ops — all produce zero output, which reads as zero bad windows. Compounding it, `scenario_sampling_started` logs `activeConfig.uiFrameSampler.enabled` — a URL PARAMETER, i.e. intent — while the failed-attach no-op is pushed into `stopCallbacks` indistinguishably. | emit `ui_sampler_attached: true|false, reason` unconditionally on the window channel; carry `uiSamplerAttached` in the scenario summary; log the START RETURN, never the config | FIXED (D38) — `startUiFrameSampler` returns an ATTACHMENT (`{attached, reason, detail, platform, stop}`) instead of a bare stop function, so the caller is handed the verdict and cannot ignore it (the type change broke the coordinator at compile time, which is the point). Three named failure reasons: native_module_missing, native_module_malformed, native_start_threw (the native `start` is now wrapped). The coordinator attaches BEFORE logging `scenario_sampling_started` and reports the START RETURN — `uiFrameSamplerAttached` + `uiFrameSamplerAttachFailure` — beside the renamed intent fields (`*Requested`); it also emits an `attach` event on the UiFrameSampler channel itself. The triple-gated **DEV**/env/once log, unreachable on the Release lane by construction, is DELETED. MUTATION-PROVED: perf-sampler-honesty.spec.ts drives a missing module, a malformed module and a throwing start; making the missing path claim `attached:true` fails 3 cases | read both samplers + the coordinator's start path

F851 | mobile-app-core | src/perf/js-frame-sampler.ts:158-162 + js-task-latency-sampler.ts:132,150-152 | **THE WORST STALL IS THE ONE BOTH SAMPLERS DISCARD.** A frame gap above `MAX_TRACKED_FRAME_MS` (5000) is DROPPED and the window flushed; a lag above `MAX_TRACKED_LAG_MS` falls to `flushWindow` without incrementing `stallCount`. A 6-second JS block — the most severe defect these exist to catch — produces FEWER stall events than a 200ms one. The clamps were written to reject bogus deltas from backgrounding and they swallow the real thing instead. | emit `{event:'stall', clamped:true, frameMs}` above the ceiling and let the consumer decide if it was a suspension | FIXED (D38) — both samplers now REPORT the over-ceiling gap instead of discarding it, tagged `clamped: true` so a consumer that knows about backgrounding can attribute it; the sample stays out of avg/p95/max (it would swamp them) but increments stallCount/stallLongest, so the window is emitted rather than silently flushed. A nonsensical delta (<=0, NaN) is still unreported — that is genuinely not a measurement. MUTATION-PROVED: perf-sampler-honesty.spec.ts, incl. 'THE DEFECT THIS EXISTS TO CATCH: a 6s freeze is not quieter than a 200ms one'; restoring the bare clamp branch fails 3 cases | read both clamp paths

The comment at the `shouldLogWindow` site names the worlds 'no output' covers and gives the device recipe: raise `logOnlyBelowFps` above the device refresh rate (e.g. 200) and every window must produce a [SearchPerf][JsFrameSampler] line; silence at 200 proves the sampler is not running. ### shared components, constants, cards, skeletons, reorder (F880–F899)

85 files read (the two `src/generated/*` sprite tables skimmed — machine-written).

F880 | mobile-app-core | src/components/app-modal-store.ts, share-modal-store.ts, option-selector-store.ts, score-info-store.ts, collaborator-modal-store.ts, list-edit-store.ts + their six hosts | **THE BIGGEST STRUCTURAL ITEM IN THIS TERRITORY, AND IT HAS ALREADY COST THREE BUGS.** Six hand-rolled copies of the same imperative singleton store (`let current = null`, `const listeners = new Set()`, `emit`, `show/close`, `getSnapshot`, `subscribe` — ~35 lines apiece), plus six copy-pasted `useSurfaceHost` ref dances in the hosts (AppModalHost:31-35, ShareModalHost:370-374, OptionSelectorHost:20-24, ScoreInfoHost:20-24, CollaboratorModalHost:239-243, ListEditHost:172-176). They have DRIFTED in exactly the way duplicated primitives do: `dismissAppModal`/`dismissShareModal` take an optional IDENTITY argument to defeat the close-races-a-newer-open bug (app-modal-store.ts:78-87, the fix documented at AppModalHost.tsx:67-70) — `closeScoreInfo`, `closeCollaboratorModal` and `closeListEdit` DO NOT, so each still carries the bug that was found and fixed twice; `closeOptionSelector` invented a third, key-matched variant. LADDER — compensates-for: nothing; the second store copied the first before the identity fix existed. rederive: one `createSingletonSurfaceStore<T>()` returning `{show, close(identity?), getSnapshot, subscribe, useValue}` with the identity-scoped close BUILT IN, plus a `useSurfaceHost(store)` hook owning the last-payload-through-exit-animation. What becomes impossible: a surface that forgets the race fix. ~200 lines deleted, three latent races closed. red-team: "score-info is scene-scoped in search, so it's genuinely different" — that is a second INSTANCE of the store, not a second KIND; the factory serves it. | one factory + one host hook | FIXED (D38) — ONE `createSingletonSurfaceStore<TPayload, TIdentity>()` (components/singleton-surface-store.ts) with the identity-scoped close BUILT IN, plus `useSingletonSurfaceHost(store)` owning the last-payload-through-exit-animation and a close scoped to the payload the render drew. All six stores are instances (app-modal, share-modal, option-selector, score-info, collaborator-modal, list-edit) and all six hosts use the hook; ~200 lines of copied `let current / listeners / emit / show / close / getSnapshot / subscribe` and six copy-pasted ref dances are gone. The three stores that never got the race fix (closeScoreInfo, closeCollaboratorModal, closeListEdit) now have it, and the option selector's third, key-matched variant is expressed as the factory's `identityOf` parameter rather than a second implementation. A surface that forgets the race fix is unrepresentable. SURPRISE: making `closeListEdit` take an identity immediately caught a live bug the types had been hiding — ListEditHost passed it straight to `onPress`, so a GestureResponderEvent was arriving where the identity goes; under the new close that would silently refuse to close, and it is now `() => closeListEdit(payload)`. RED-PROVED: singleton-surface-store.spec.ts, 8 pass; deleting the identity guard fails 'THE RACE: a deferred close cannot kill the surface that replaced it' and the custom-identity case | diffed all six stores and all six hosts

NEUTRAL_SCORE_COLOR is reserved for null/NaN ONLY — the very case this coercion destroys. F759 | api-identity-money | apps/api/src/modules/billing/billing.service.ts, billing.controller.ts, dto/create-checkout-session.dto.ts, prisma/schema.prisma (CheckoutSession + checkout_session_status), migrations/20260803130000_restore_stripe_checkout_rail, config/configuration.ts (stripe.\*) | THE STRIPE WEB CHECKOUT RAIL WAS MISSING FOR THE PLAN THAT NOW DEPENDS ON IT. Deleted 2026-07-09 in c2861853 under the then-true rationale "models a rail that doesn't exist"; owner ruled 2026-08-01/08-03 that web checkout IS the plan (Strava dual-button paywall, ~97% net vs ~85% via StoreKit, Apple-commission-free out-of-app under the April 2025 Epic contempt ruling). Without it the primary paywall button had no URL to open. | RESTORED where the old shape was still right (hosted Checkout session creation, the attempt-log table, the portal session, `checkout.session.completed`). RE-DERIVED onto today's tree where it was not: (1) the DTO no longer accepts client `successUrl`/`cancelUrl` — an open redirect wearing a feature's clothes; all three redirect URLs plus the price id come from config with NO defaults (no-fake-estimates: a guessed price sells the wrong thing, a guessed redirect strands a payer), and an unconfigured rail 503s naming the missing env vars. (2) SINGLE-PRODUCT LAW is now enforced on BOTH doors: a `priceId` that is not the configured premium price is a 400 naming both ids, and a subscription arriving with an `entitlement_code` outside the closed ledger vocabulary throws (event recorded FAILED + replayable) instead of minting a paid grant under a code `accessVerdict()` never asks about — the same D1-residual hole `RevenueCatEntitlementMap` refuses at boot, closed on the Stripe side. (3) ENTITLEMENTS UNIFY THROUGH THE ACCESS-GRANT LEDGER, not the restored table: `checkout.session.completed` retrieves the subscription and funnels into the same `syncSubscriptionGrant` the subscription events use, idempotent per `stripe:<subId>`, so whichever of the two events Stripe delivers first is sufficient and the second is a no-op. The table is the ATTEMPT log only. (4) `subscription_data.metadata` is stamped at session creation — Stripe does NOT copy Checkout Session metadata onto the subscription, so without it every web subscription event would log "without matching user" and grant nothing. (5) `ensureStripeCustomer` came back into BillingService rather than UserService: it is billing's business and nothing else ever called it. (6) The recreated table is timestamptz throughout and FK RESTRICT, per the laws that postdate its deletion. | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: `npx jest billing entitlements` 86/86 green (12 new in billing-checkout.spec.ts, stripe SDK mocked — zero live vendor calls); `yarn build` clean; migration applied locally and `prisma migrate diff` shows ZERO checkout drift. **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (same day, owner ruling): TWO PRICES OF THE ONE PRODUCT, and the single-product law got STRONGER, not looser.** The owner ruled that the web paywall shows the SAME structure as the in-app one (Strava pattern: same offers, different payment location) — $7.99/mo and $39.99/yr with an annual-only ~1-week free trial. So `stripe.premiumPriceId` (STRIPE_PREMIUM_PRICE_ID) became the closed pair `stripe.prices.{monthly,annual}` (STRIPE_MONTHLY_PRICE_ID / STRIPE_ANNUAL_PRICE_ID), and the DTO's `priceId` field was DELETED in favour of `{ plan: 'monthly' | 'annual' }`. Note what this does to the law: the original shape enforced 'premium is the only product' by comparing a client-supplied opaque Stripe id against the configured one — a refusal. A two-word vocabulary makes a price id outside the configured pair UNREPRESENTABLE, because the caller cannot name a price at all; the api alone maps word -> price. One PRODUCT, two PRICES of it; `entitlement_code` metadata is identical on both plans, so both grant the same thing `accessVerdict()` asks about. `plan` is REQUIRED, never defaulted — a paywall showing two prices against a body that silently means 'monthly' is a charge the user did not pick. The trial lives in the SESSION (`subscription_data.trial_period_days = ANNUAL_TRIAL_PERIOD_DAYS = 7`, annual only, spread in rather than set-to-undefined) not on the Stripe price, so the offer the copy promises and the offer the code builds are the same object. `requireCheckoutConfig()` requires BOTH prices regardless of the requested plan: a half-configured rail must 503 on the FIRST request, not on the unlucky user who picks the other button. PROOF: `npx jest billing --silent` 53/53 green; `yarn build` clean; zero live vendor calls (stripe SDK still mocked wholesale). MUTATION-PROVEN RED, three recipes: (a) `dto.plan === 'annual'` -> `'monthly'` (trial on the wrong plan) = 2 red; (b) `config.prices[dto.plan]` -> `config.prices.monthly` (plan ignored) = 1 red; (c) reopening the DTO to `@IsOptional() @IsString() plan` = 4 red; all restored, 53/53 green. ORIGINAL-PASS PROOF: replacing the single-product price check with `if (false)` and the replayed-event short-circuit with `if (false)` turns exactly 2 tests red ("REFUSES a second product", "idempotent on REPLAY — zero extra grants"); restored, 12/12 green. NOT DONE (out of scope, reported): no Stripe key was used, nothing deployed, mobile client not wired.

F706/F707 RESOLUTION (2026-08-03) | OWNER-RULED: RAIL RETIRED. "The map is in a good state, that's locked in, and we no longer need to debug it the way we did when we created that rail." Deleted: visual-baselines/ (4 PNGs + README), scripts/visual-regression.js + .test.js, both package.json entries, the stale @run-by cross-reference. perf-scenario-visual-contracts.js is NOT part of the rail (perf-verb contracts, owner-KEEP seed) and survives. The eye is the map's regression oracle now, by explicit owner choice. | OWNER-DECISION → DELETED

F760 | site + api-identity-money | apps/site/** (new workspace: src/config.ts, src/clerk-frontend-api.ts, src/premium-page.ts, src/router.ts, src/server.ts, src/pages/{index,privacy,terms,support}.html, test/router.test.ts, Dockerfile, README.md, package.json, tsconfig.json), railway.site.json, apps/api/src/main.ts (CORS) | THE PUBLIC WEBSITE HAD NO SOURCE. craveapp.ai was a Railway bun "function" whose ENTIRE source was a base64 blob inside the service's `startCommand` — four HTML constants and an if-chain, outside the repo, outside git, outside review, un-diffable and un-testable. D39 ruled it must carry web payments (Strava dual-button, ~97% net); that shape cannot hold auth or checkout at all. SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT FOUND WHILE WIRING IT: prod CORS was a flat `origin: false`. Correct while the only client was the native app (not a browser, sends no Origin) — but it makes the browser's POST /billing/checkout-session unreachable, so F759's restored rail had a URL nobody could ask for. The web rail was DEAD ON ARRIVAL in prod and nothing said so. | REDERIVED as apps/site, a zero-dependency node:http server (runs unchanged on Bun; node:http chosen over Bun.serve so the shipped server is the one the tests exercise — bun is not installed on the dev machine, and a test that cannot run is not a test). (1) The four pages are PRESERVED, captured from the live site, with exactly one edit: Cloudflare had rewritten every support@craveapp.ai link into `__cf_email__` obfuscation that renders only if a CDN script loads — those are plain mailto: links again, and a test asserts no cdn-cgi/**cf_email** residue survives. (2) /premium: Clerk sign-in for the SAME instance as the app, then a JWT-TEMPLATE bearer to `${API_ORIGIN}/api/v1/billing/checkout-session` with an EMPTY body, then follow the returned hosted-Stripe url. Empty body is the point: the site names no price (single-product law is the api's to enforce) and no redirect URL (the DTO stopped accepting client successUrl/cancelUrl — an open redirect wearing a feature's clothes); /premium/success and /premium/cancelled exist to BE the configured STRIPE*CHECKOUT*\*\_URL targets, not to be passed as them. Tests assert /priceId/ and /successUrl|cancelUrl/ NEVER appear in the served page. (3) The Clerk frontend-API host is DERIVED from the publishable key (which is base64 of that host) rather than being a fourth env var — a hand-typed host that disagrees with the key signs visitors into the wrong Clerk instance and the api then rejects every token; a malformed key throws naming the reason instead of guessing, including a hostname-shape check so a corrupt key cannot inject a path into the script src. (4) NO SECRET LIVES HERE: no Stripe key, no Clerk secret key, no DB URL. Env contract is three non-secret vars (API_ORIGIN, CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY, PORT=8080) plus CLERK_JWT_TEMPLATE. (5) An unconfigured checkout is an OUTAGE and must look like one: /premium answers 503 naming the missing vars, while the four static pages keep serving — a billing misconfiguration must never take the Privacy Policy offline. (6) api CORS now reads WEB_ORIGIN (comma-separated exact origins) and, UNSET, leaves prod EXACTLY as strict as before — an allowlist, not a loosening, and a config-less deploy changes nothing. credentials stays false (bearer header, never a cookie). | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: 12/12 `node --test` green in apps/site; root `yarn build` green (4/4 turbo tasks, site included). MUTATION-PROVEN RED: changing /premium's unconfigured status 503->200 and dropping the trailing-slash normalization turns exactly 2 tests red ("/premium is 503 (not a broken 200) when unconfigured", "trailing slashes and case do not 302 a real page away"); restored, 12/12 green. ZERO VENDOR CALLS in tests (the fixture publishable key is base64 of "fake-clerk.example.com$"). NOT DONE (out of scope, reported): nothing staged, committed, or deployed; Railway untouched; the base64-startCommand function is still the live site until the owner executes the cutover in apps/site/README.md. **AMENDED 2026-08-03 (owner ruling, paired with F759's amendment): /premium NOW SHOWS BOTH OFFERS.\*\* The page shipped with a single 'Continue to secure checkout' button posting an empty body — which, once the api grew two prices, would have quietly steered every web buyer onto whichever price the api happened to pick as the default. The Strava pattern is the SAME offers in a different payment location, not a reduced menu, so /premium renders two plan cards in the existing dark/light visual language: $7.99/mo (Cancel anytime) and $39.99/yr (1 week free, then $39.99/year), each button posting `{"plan":"…"}` built from its own `data-plan`. The 'never names a price' property SURVIVES and is now structural: the page cannot express a Stripe price id, only a plan word. HONEST COPY IS TESTED, not just present — the annual note must say what happens AFTER the trial ('1 week free, then $39.99/year'); '1 week free' alone is the half-truth that produces chargebacks — and a test asserts the trial is never promised on monthly. The dollar amounts and the trial length are COPY, not configuration: they must match the Stripe prices and ANNUAL_TRIAL_PERIOD_DAYS, and the README says so, because only the api can charge — a mismatch is a LIE ON THE PAGE, not a wrong charge, which is worse rather than better. Both buttons disable together while either is in flight (one impatient double-click must not open two payment pages for one decision). Still zero dependencies, zero secrets, zero vendor calls. PROOF: `yarn workspace site build` + `test` 13/13 green. MUTATION-PROVEN RED: (d) hard-coding the POST body to `{"plan":"monthly"}` (ignoring the button) = 1 red; (e) deleting the annual offer card = 2 red; restored, 13/13 green. OPEN DEPENDENCY: CLERK_JWT_TEMPLATE defaults to 'mobile' because that is the ONLY template known to mint an audience CLERK_JWT_AUDIENCE accepts — a dedicated 'web' template requires adding its aud to the api first, or every checkout 401s.

Two laws it encodes: unknown KEYS are preserved verbatim on write and merely reported (`unknownQuestionIds`) — the document is the user's testimony and the decoder has opinions at READ only; unknown OPTION ids are dropped at read and COUNTED (`droppedOptionIds`), so a drop can be seen. The seven remaining CLOSED option vocabularies (attribution, dining-frequency, budget, decide-how, dietary-needs, spice, dining-goals, notification cadence) moved to shared in the same pass and mobile builds them through `labelledOptions`/`detailedOptions` — a renamed id without a label is now a compile error on both sides. | FIXED (2026-08-03) | PROOF: api yarn build clean; 6 decoder cases in apps/api/src/modules/identity/onboarding-answer-decoder.spec.ts; MUTATION-PROVEN RED end to end — changing the builder-spec fixture's stored option id from an unknown `'mexicano'` to the real `'mexican'` turns "an answer document whose option ids are NOT in the shared vocabulary builds nothing" red (1 failed / 8 skipped), restored green. F762 | api-identity | apps/api/src/modules/identity/user.service.ts updateOnboarding, dto/update-user-onboarding.dto.ts, prisma UserOnboardingResponse + users.onboarding_question_set_version | THE COMPLETION WRITE BELIEVED THE CLIENT AND KEPT NO HISTORY. One raw UPDATE: it stored whatever `onboardingVersion` int arrived (the server had no opinion of its own), replaced the whole answer document with no record of what it replaced, and `@IsOptional @IsObject` was the entire validation. | REDERIVED: (1) append-only `user_onboarding_responses` (userId, answeredWithVersion, questionSetVersion, answers jsonb, source, recordedAt) is the RECORD and `users.onboarding_responses` is its latest-row PROJECTION, written in the SAME transaction — one write path, so the projection cannot disagree with the row behind it. Migration backfills one row per answering user (`source='backfill'`, question set 1 — not a guess: it is the set those keys belong to). (2) The server stamps its OWN `ONBOARDING_QUESTION_SET_VERSION` beside the client-declared one, so a mismatch is a fact on the row instead of an assumption nobody can check. (3) `source` ('completion' | 'replay' | 'edit' | 'backfill') keeps "answered at the end of the flow" distinguishable from "landed later from the outbox" forever. (4) The DTO validates THROUGH the decoder — proving the document is DECODABLE, not filtering it (rejecting unknown keys would make every question-set rollout a coordinated deploy). RED recipes stated in the spec header (move the UPDATE outside $transaction; store dto.onboardingVersion in both version slots).

F764 | api-signals | apps/api/src/modules/signals/user-taste-profile.builder.ts (new) + signal-demand-aggregate.service.ts, prisma UserTasteProfile | THE BEHAVIORAL HALF HAD NO READ MODEL, SO A RECIPE WROTE ITS OWN LEDGER SQL. `curated-list-builder.engagedSubjectIds` queried raw `signals` with its own kind list and NO echo exclusion — a fourth dialect of the act-grain law `act-identity.ts` exists to state once, quietly disagreeing with the other three. | REDERIVED as `user_taste_profile`, a derived read model built from `signal_demand_daily` THROUGH `dailyActsCteSql` (never raw signals; the builder cannot be half-adopted). F766 | mobile-onboarding | apps/mobile/src/screens/Onboarding.tsx completeAndEnterApp, navigation/runtime/AppRouteCoordinator.tsx | THE ANONYMOUS COMPLETER'S ANSWERS WERE DESTROYED BY THE SUCCESS PATH. That call RESETS THE DRAFT, so a waitlist / pre-auth completer's answers were gone from the device as well as never sent — and the "already finished on this device" mirror that fires after they later sign in posts `answers: {}`, so signing in landed an EMPTY document. The one thing onboarding exists to collect was lost on the happy path. | REDERIVED, no new machinery: "not signed in yet" is the same SHAPE as "the server said no" — a payload we cannot land TODAY — so it takes the same lane. The anonymous branch now queues the full payload into the F810 persisted outbox BEFORE completing locally (order is load-bearing: the local completion wipes the draft), and `decideOnboardingCompletionReplay` needed NO change — it already holds forever and lands on the first authenticated launch. TWO GUARDS ADDED IN THE SAME DIRECTION: (a) the mirror effect is silenced while a payload is queued, so it can never overwrite real answers with `{}`; (b) server-side, an EMPTY answer document is treated as the absence of an answer — it writes no history row and leaves the projection untouched. Clearing answers is not an action any surface offers, so it is not a request the endpoint can express. F767 | api-identity + api-signals | apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma UserOnboardingResponse + UserTasteProfile, apps/api/src/modules/identity/account-deletion.service.ts, scripts/rig/scrub-staging-user-data.sql | HONEST GAP, DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN — two NEW homes for user data landed in this pass and NEITHER joins the deletion path or the staging PII scrub. This is D40 red-team escalation #2, which Phase 2 sent to the owner, so implementing it here would have been implementing an escalated item. Recording it so the gap is a decision and not an oversight: `user_onboarding_responses` retains the full answer document after `AccountDeletionService` nulls `users.onboarding_responses` (i.e. deletion currently removes the projection and keeps the record), and `user_taste_profile` is an inferred-preference record (dietary needs, spice tolerance and budget band are arguably sensitive) that nothing deletes or rebuilds-empty on account deletion. Escalation #1 (account deletion never severs `signal_actors.user_id`) is the substrate both sit on and is likewise untouched. | OWNER RULED (D40 rulings #1+#2, 2026-08-03) and IMPLEMENTED. Deletion is ANONYMITY, not data destruction, wherever the data is anonymous demand evidence: `AccountDeletionService` now (a) SEVERS `signal_actors.user_id` (and `device_key` with it, so the next sign-in on that device cannot re-adopt the actor) — the actor row and every signal survive as anonymous demand evidence, completing the story signals.service.ts has documented all along; (b) DELETES `user_onboarding_responses` (the person's own answers — the record now follows the projection deletion already did) and `user_taste_profile` (inferred dietary/spice/budget), read off the actor BEFORE severance since the profile is actor-keyed. Scrub EXECUTED locally inside BEGIN...ROLLBACK: NOTICEs 'scrubbed user_onboarding_responses' + 'scrubbed user_taste_profile', verifier passed, 0 rows both, ROLLBACK confirmed (users 17 / polls 17930 intact). Never run against staging or prod. yarn build clean; full suite 160 suites / 1404 tests green.

F901 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:400-420 | `amendTransitionTxnJoinInputs` doc promises a guard the code does not implement: "Legal only before any input has landed (staged/committed/joining with a FULL PENDING SET); later amendment is a loud violation." The code checks ONLY `phase !== 'staged' && phase !== 'committed'` — it never checks that the pending set is full. Line 416 then does `live.pendingJoinInputs = new Set(nextInputs)` UNCONDITIONALLY, so an already-landed input is RESURRECTED into pending. Failure scenario: BottomSheetSceneStackHost.tsx:1491 amends on a genuine roles change; if a `paint` offer already landed in the 'committed' window (e.g. the warm evidence path at :1499 on a prior frame), the amend un-lands it and the join now waits for a paint that will never re-fire on a warm leg -> 600ms watchdog degrade every switch. Ladder: compensated-for = the host being the only place that knows cold-vs-warm; bedrock = the host declares the join, once, at arm; rederive = make the amendment a first-class 'arm' edge (staged|committed -> armed) that is legal exactly once and preserves already-landed marks, instead of a mutable rewrite of readonly-ish plan data via a cast. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | amendTransitionTxnJoinInputs now computes landed = declared \ pending and rebuilds pendingJoinInputs EXCLUDING it (no resurrection), and barks `late_join_amendment` naming the landed inputs; a second amend of the same txn barks too (joinAmended). Proof: transition-transaction.spec.ts "an amendment NEVER resurrects a landed input" + "a SECOND amendment ... barks". RED-proven: restoring `new Set(nextInputs)` fails 1 test

F902 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:255-258 vs apps/mobile/src/overlays/BottomSheetSceneStackHost.tsx:1486-1500 | CALLER-MUST-REMEMBER + SILENT NO-OP. `sealTransitionTxnJoin` returns silently when `phase !== 'committed'`. The host's arm site explicitly admits phase may be 'staged' (`liveTxnForArm?.phase === 'staged' || === 'committed'`) and then calls amend + `sealLiveTransitionTxnJoin()`. In the 'staged' case the seal is a NO-OP, and the warm-evidence `offerTransitionJoinInput('paint')` two lines later is ALSO a no-op (offer requires phase joining|committed, :429-435). Both the seal and the warm paint evidence are silently dropped; the txn later seals at idle with a pending 'paint' that a warm leg never re-fires -> guaranteed 600ms watchdog degrade. Neither drop barks. Ladder: bedrock = an arm either takes effect or is loud; rederive = seal should either be legal from 'staged' (staged -> joining is a sensible edge) or report a violation, and offer should bark when it drops an input the live plan declared. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | sealTransitionTxnJoin from staged is REMEMBERED (txn.joinSealRequested) and replayed by commitTransitionTxn, so the host arm always takes effect; offerTransitionJoinInput now CONSUMES in staged (the warm-paint evidence path) and BARKS `dropped_join_offer` when a declared, still-pending input is dropped by phase. Proof: the 6-test "arm window (F901/F902)" describe. RED-proven 3 ways: removing the staged-remember branch fails 3 tests; removing staged from offer fails 1

F903 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:238-240 + 298, 388, 418, 438, 447 | TRUTH IN 2 PLACES (notification). `advance()` already notifies every listener when the txn is live (:238-240). Five call sites ALSO call `listeners.forEach(...)` immediately after an advance-bearing call: the watchdog (:298), `withLiveTransitionTxn` (:388), `amendTransitionTxnJoinInputs` (:418), `offerTransitionJoinInput` (:438), `sealLiveTransitionTxnJoin` (:447). Every phase-changing path notifies TWICE. Not a correctness bug today (listeners are idempotent readers), but it is exactly the "who notifies" ambiguity that makes a future non-idempotent subscriber (the scene-stack runtime's deferred-flush subscriber at app-route-scene-stack-runtime.ts:2088) fire twice. Ladder: bedrock = one notifier; rederive = `advance()` and `stage()` are the ONLY notifiers; delete the five trailing forEach calls. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | ONE NOTIFICATION PER OPERATION. All notifies route through `notifySubscribers()`; the multi-notify entry points (stage, offer, sealLive) wrap their bodies in `inOneNotification`, which suppresses inner notifies and emits exactly one at the end — so nothing is dropped (amend, an intermediate join landing and a seal remembered in the 'staged' window still wake subscribers) and nothing fires twice. The watchdog's trailing forEach deleted outright (advance already notified). PARTIAL CORRECTION TO THE FINDING: amend and offer are NOT phase-changing, so their trailing notifies were the ONLY ones on those paths — deleting them as the finding proposed would have dropped notifications; the wrapper is what makes 'one notifier' true without loss. `withLiveTransitionTxn` (the 5th site) DELETED: repo-wide it had no callers outside one spec assertion. New spec 'notifies subscribers EXACTLY ONCE per operation', RED recipe stated in-file. | read

F904 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:341-348 | `supersedeTransitionTxn` deliberately does NOT notify listeners; only its caller `stageTransitionTxn` notifies, AFTER minting the replacement. Consequence: a subscriber waiting for "the txn I armed for has terminated" (app-route-scene-stack-runtime.ts:2088-2105) is woken with a BRAND-NEW live txn in phase 'staged' — none of its terminal conditions match — so it does NOT unsubscribe and does NOT flush. It self-heals on the new txn's revealed edge, but the subscriber is watching WHICHEVER txn is live, not the one it armed for, which is the un-keyed-singleton smell (Q-5) the engine's own header claims to have killed. Ladder: rederive = subscribers register interest in a txnId, and the holder delivers `(txnId, phase)` to the listener rather than making every listener re-read a global. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | Both halves. `supersedeTransitionTxn` now notifies, and `subscribeTransitionTxn` records the txn that was LIVE AT SUBSCRIBE TIME and hands it to the listener — so a subscriber watches the txn it armed for instead of re-reading the global holder. The one consumer (app-route-scene-stack-runtime's deferred body-surface flush) reads `armedTxn` and terminates on the supersede edge instead of self-healing one reveal later. New spec asserts 'superseded' is observed and 'staged' never is (RED recipe stated). | read both

F905 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts:278 | MAGIC NUMBER: `const JOIN_LIVENESS_MS = 600;`. The 20-line comment above it explains WHY a watchdog exists and what a fire means, but never what 600 is derived from. It is not a fact, not a stated owner choice, and not a derivation. It is the single number that decides whether a slow real switch is "broken" or "fine" — and per-plan override `joinLivenessMs` exists precisely because 600 was wrong for fade-paced revises ("~2s measured"). Ladder: the override IS the evidence the default is unattributed; rederive = derive the default from the measured p99 machine-paced join (the [L4STAMP] joinWaitMs instrument already logs exactly this distribution) and state the measurement inline, or make it an explicit owner choice with a date. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — INSTRUMENTED, value untouched per the ruling. The [L4STAMP] line now emits `windowMs`, `windowOverridden` and `headroom` alongside `joinWaitMs`, so the p99 of MACHINE-paced joins (windowOverridden=false, i.e. excluding search's 10000ms fade-paced plans) is a grep away — that p99 is the number that would attribute 600. JOIN_LIVENESS_MS carries a header stating it is UNATTRIBUTED and must not be changed by feel. | read; contrast with the honest derivation of DEFAULT_TRANSITION_SPRING_CONFIG at transition-lane-player.ts:38-46

F912 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/sceneScrollStateRegistry.ts:129-138 | UNBOUNDED REGISTRY (the leak class, registry variant). `const states = new Map<string, SceneScrollState>()` is module-global and has NO removal path — `getState` creates on miss and nothing ever deletes. The key is a CONTENT identity, not a bounded scene-key space: RestaurantPanel.tsx:853 passes `` `restaurant:${restaurantId}` ``. So every distinct restaurant/list/DM the user opens in a session permanently adds a record (savedOffset + pendingRestore + scrollHandle slot + publishedOffsets array + boundaryFacts). Small per entry, unbounded in count, and it survives every scene pop. Ladder: compensates-for = session-persistent scroll restore, a real requirement; bedrock = a scroll memory is a CACHE with a policy; rederive = bound it the way the residency law already bounds units — an LRU capped at `RESIDENT_UNIT_RETENTION_LIMIT`-scale, or drop a record when its route entry is popped and its identity is not stack-pinned (the scene-entry-mounts resolver already computes exactly that retention set). | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The record store is a bounded LRU: touch-on-access, cap 48, eviction skips PINNED records (live scroll handle / in-stack header publication / staged one-shot restore — all released by their own teardown, so pinning cannot wedge it). The cap is documented as a MEMORY knob, not a UX knob. New spec sceneScrollStateRegistry.spec.ts (3 tests) PROVEN RED by raising the cap to 4800: 'Expected 0, Received 123'. | read; caller identity confirmed at RestaurantPanel.tsx:853, useOverlaySheetListRuntime.ts:28

F913 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/overlayTransientDismissorRuntime.ts:14-22 | SWALLOWED ERROR. `dismissTransientOverlays` wraps each handler in `try { handler() } catch { console.warn(...) }` and continues. A dismissor that throws leaves its overlay ON SCREEN while every caller of `dismissTransientOverlays` proceeds as if the screen were clear — and `console.warn` is stripped in Release, so the failure is invisible on the only lane that matters. Ladder: compensates-for = one bad dismissor blocking the rest of the list, a real concern; bedrock = a dismiss either happened or it is loud; rederive = keep the per-handler isolation but make the failure honest — `logger.error` (the app's real sink, Release-visible), and return the failed count so a caller that NEEDS a clear screen can act on it rather than assume. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | Per-handler isolation kept; the failure now reaches `captureHandledError` (the Release-visible Sentry sink) plus a dev console.error, instead of a `console.warn` stripped in Release. NOT DONE, deliberately and recorded in-file: returning the failed COUNT — ~15 consumer contracts in screens/Search declare the verb `() => void` and that is a concurrent lane. | read

F914 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/scene-load-failure-policy.ts:50-61 | LATCH THAT NEVER RE-ARMS. `announcedRef` is documented as "one announcement per error EDGE" but it is only reset when `isError` goes FALSE (:55). On the ROOT-scene path the retry fires on the next presentation (:70-77) WITHOUT resetting the latch, so if that retry also fails while `isError` never dipped false, the user gets NO second modal — the app silently shows a skeleton forever. Failure scenario: offline-ish flaky network, user returns to Lists twice; the second failure is swallowed. Ladder: bedrock = every failure edge is announced once; rederive = reset the latch where the retry is fired (the retry IS the start of a new edge), not only on a false `isError`. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | EVERY RETURN IS AN EDGE. The presentation subscription no longer unsubscribes on the first re-presentation: while the root scene stays in its error state, each return ANNOUNCES the failure already known and THEN re-runs the load. That ordering is what keeps it honest — the modal reports the attempt that finished, never a guess about the one starting — and it tears down by construction when `isError` goes false. (Considered and rejected: the finding's literal 'reset the latch at retry', which re-announces while the retry is still in flight and would put a false failure modal over a page that then loads fine.) | read

Either delete the disjunct (and let the posture-seat registry be the sole classifier, which is what the paragraph above promises: "a future scene classifies itself by construction"), or delete the "search never gates" sentence and explain why search is special-cased outside the registry. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The `sceneKey === 'search'` disjunct deleted; `resolveSheetPostureSeat` is the sole classifier, which is what the paragraph above it promises. | read; the two statements in the same file contradict

F916 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/AppRouteCoordinator.tsx:294-321 | STALE-CLOSURE HAZARD. The signed-in profile-hydration effect calls `queryClient.setQueryData(accessQueryKey(clerkUserId), profile.access)` at :302, but the dep array (:321) is `[authStatus, hydrateCompletionFromServer]` — it omits BOTH `clerkUserId` and `queryClient`. On a user switch where `authStatus` stays 'signed_in' throughout (Clerk `setActive` to a different session — which this very file drives at :163), the effect does not re-run, and if it is mid-flight its `.then` writes the NEW user's access payload under the OLD user's query key. The access cache is the paywall routing axis (:391), so a mis-keyed write routes the wrong user. Ladder: bedrock = a cache write is keyed by the identity it was fetched for; rederive = key the effect on `clerkUserId` (re-fetch on user change is correct anyway) and capture the id at request time so the `.then` writes under the id it asked for, not the id that happens to be current. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | Both halves: `clerkUserId` is a dependency (a user switch re-fetches), and the id is CAPTURED at request time so the `.then` writes the access payload under the id it asked for. `queryClient` joined the deps. | read; `git log -1 245332d17` is the new baseline for this file

F917 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/AppRouteCoordinator.tsx:428-429 | STICKY-READY LATCH, undocumented. `exposedIsReady = isReady || stableRouteState !== null` — once ANY route state has ever been published, the coordinator reports ready FOREVER, even when `isReady` later goes false (session recovery, a lapse re-entering 'loading'). During such a window `exposedRouteState` serves the LAST route state, so the app keeps rendering the previous destination while auth is genuinely unresolved. That is very likely the intended anti-flicker behavior — but nothing in the file says so, and `areRouteStatesEqual` (:42-56, JSON.stringify-based) plus the whole `stableRouteState` mirror exist only to serve it. Ladder: rederive = name the concept — publish `{ routeState, isResolving }` so a consumer that must NOT act on stale routing (a sign-out) can tell "still showing the last known destination" from "this is current", instead of that distinction living in an unnamed `||`. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The concept is named: the coordinator publishes `isResolving` alongside `isReady`/`routeState`, documented as 'what you are being handed is the LAST KNOWN destination, not a current one'. The stickiness is unchanged (it is the anti-flicker behaviour) but a consumer that must not act on stale routing can now tell the difference instead of the distinction living in an unnamed `||`. | read

F918 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/app-route-scene-stack-runtime.ts:1157-1194 | IDEAL-VERIFIED (argued, not assumed). `AppRouteSceneStackController.dispose()` is the shape to build today: it unsubscribes both subscription collections, clears all 12 caches/registries, clears the deferred body-surface timeout AND its txn subscription, and clears every `dataLaneTimers` entry. Contrast with the leak class this repo keeps paying for (F830's undisarmed watchdog, F912's unbounded scroll registry): this controller can be created and destroyed in a loop without retaining a timer, a listener, or a snapshot. Notable: it disarms the ENGINE subscription it armed (:1181-1182) — the exact discipline the engine holder itself only offers under a test-only reset. | - | read :1157-1194 against every `new Set`/`new Map`/`setTimeout` declared on the class

F919 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/useArmedOutsideDismiss.ts (whole file) | IDEAL-VERIFIED. 66 lines that encode one interaction law (touch-down is a no-op; dismiss on first move or on lift-without-move) once, for every modal surface in the app, with the WHY stated in the header ("press, then reconsider" must be cancellable). No magic numbers, no per-call-site options that could drift, `manualActivation` + `cancelsTouchesInView(false)` documented in terms of the behavior they buy. The `onDismissRef` bridge is the correct fix for the real problem it names (rebuilding the gesture every render). This is what a shared interaction primitive should look like. | - | read

F920 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/premount-violation-probe.ts:20,92 | MAGIC NUMBER in a RED instrument. `PREMOUNT_COMMIT_GRACE_MS = 48` is the entire threshold between "the law is satisfied" and a logged violation. The header derives the LAW carefully (a commit may flip visibility, never build) and cites the measurement that motivated it ("the warm 61ms JS commit the red-team names") — but 48 itself is unattributed. 48 is suspiciously ~3 frames at 60Hz; if that is the derivation it should say so, and if the app targets 120Hz it is 6 frames. Ladder: bedrock = a threshold is a fact or an owner choice; rederive = state it as `3 * FRAME_MS` with the frame budget named, or as the measured p99 of a compliant pre-mount. The instrument is otherwise well-built (Release-safe sink, explicitly RED-provable). | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — INSTRUMENTED. The compliant population was being swallowed by an early return; post-flip first commits UNDER the grace now emit `[PREMOUNT] compliant … sinceAckMs= graceMs=` in **DEV**, which is the p99 that would attribute 48. Both log lines carry graceMs. The constant carries a header refusing the `3 * FRAME_MS` restatement as an invented derivation (48ms is 3 frames at 60Hz and 6 at 120Hz — i.e. it silently means different things per device). | read

F938 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/app-route-scene-switch-controller.ts:241-242 | MAGIC NUMBER whose own comment implies the correct value. `// Ack records older than this many switches are pruned — supersede only ever consults the immediately-previous frame's switchId.` followed by `const PRESENTATION_ACK_RETENTION = 8;`. Not harmful, but it is exactly the shape of a number nobody can later reason about. F939 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/shell-residency-registry.ts:100-120 vs apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/ADDING_A_SCENE.md:16-24 | THE ONE REGISTRY THAT ESCAPED THE REPO'S OWN EXHAUSTIVENESS LAW. ADDING_A_SCENE.md states the law plainly — "Let the compiler find the rest. The `Record<OverlayKey>` registries and the completeness assertions turn a forgotten key into a build error that NAMES the key. This exhaustiveness exists specifically because a missing CHILD_SHARED_SHEET_SCENES entry once silently broke nav." The residency registry breaks it three ways: `RESIDENCY_MANAGED_SCENES` is a plain array, not a `Record<OverlayKey>`, so a new scene is silently unmanaged (F908); `residentUnitIdentityOf` ends in `default: return 'scene';`, so a new multi-entry scene silently gets ONE shared singleton resident unit instead of per-identity units — the exact "silently broke nav" class, one layer down; and the doc's section 4 lists the policies that intentionally degrade gracefully, and residency is not among them. Ladder: bedrock = the doc's own law; rederive = make `residentUnitIdentityOf` an exhaustive `Record<ResidencyManagedSceneKey, (entry) => string | null>` so a new managed scene must declare its identity, and derive the key type from the array (F908). | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | `residentUnitIdentityOf`'s `switch ... default: return 'scene'` replaced by an exhaustive `Record<ResidencyManagedSceneKey, ResidentUnitIdentityResolver>` (key type derived from the membership array, F908), so joining residency FORCES a scene to declare its unit identity and the compile error names the key. Singleton/ephemeral rows read as declarations (`SINGLETON_UNIT` / `EPHEMERAL_UNIT`). Mutation recipe stated in-file. Unmanaged scenes (polls/restaurant) keep the legacy singleton via an explicit branch. | read both; doc lines 16-24 quoted

F940 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime + apps/mobile/src/overlays (root, excl. panels) | IDEAL-VERIFIED, measured across the territory: THE ERROR-SWALLOWING CLASS IS ESSENTIALLY ABSENT HERE. A repo-wide grep for empty catches and bare `catch (x) {` across all ~155 navigation/runtime files plus the overlays root returned exactly THREE catches: AppRouteCoordinator.tsx:165 (session recovery — logs, resets the resumed-id so a retry is possible, returns an honest `false`), MainLaunchCoordinator.tsx:585 (location watch — logs and degrades to no-location, which is the correct product behavior), and overlayTransientDismissorRuntime.ts:18 (the one genuine offender — F913). Likewise magic numbers: a targeted scan of the four largest controllers (scene-switch, sheet-host-authority, transition-policy-runtime, motion-executor — ~4,800 lines) surfaced exactly ONE unattributed constant (F938); every other literal is a token counter or a stack-length comparison. For a 69k-line surface this is an unusually disciplined result and worth defending. | - | `grep -rn "catch\s*{\s*}\|catch (.*) {$"` over the territory; `grep -nE "= [0-9]+" ` over the four controllers

F941 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/ADDING_A_SCENE.md (192 lines) + apps/mobile/src/overlays/PERFORMANCE_PATTERNS.md (368 lines) | IDEAL-VERIFIED. Two in-repo guides that do what a stranger actually needs: ADDING_A_SCENE.md gives a principled rule rather than a menu ("surfaceKind 'list' for ANY scrollable body ... default to this"), names the historical bug each rule exists to prevent ("the polls feed was wrongly 'mounted' — that's why its cards didn't tap"), and is HONEST about which policies are compile-forced versus which degrade gracefully (section 4) instead of pretending everything is derived. That last distinction is the thing most such docs get wrong. It is also the document that made F939 findable — a doc stating a law clearly enough to catch the registry that violates it is doing its job. | - | read
F942 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/RootNavigator.tsx:49-51 | BLOCK THAT CANNOT CHANGE ANYTHING (mutation-tested to a proof, not a hunch). `if (!isReadyToRender) { return routeState.destination === 'main' ? <AppShellMainNavigator /> : null; }`. Proof: (1) line 41 already returned unless `isReady && routeState`; (2) MainLaunchCoordinator.tsx:461 binds `isRouteReady` to that SAME `useAppRouteCoordinator().isReady`; (3) MainLaunchCoordinator.tsx:1027-1031 defines `isReadyToRender = escape || (isRouteReady && routeState != null && (destination === 'main' ? startup && mainLaunchReady : TRUE))`. So for every NON-main destination `isReadyToRender` is necessarily already true at line 49 — the `: null` arm is unreachable. And the `'main'` arm returns `<AppShellMainNavigator />`, which is byte-identical to what `case 'main'` at :76 returns. Delete lines 49-51 entirely: identical behavior on every path. Ladder: compensates-for = an older split where the splash lived above the shell and main had to render early; bedrock = the splash gate now lives INSIDE the main shell (shouldHideSplash, MainLaunchCoordinator.tsx:1032-1044); rederive = drop the block, and `isReadyToRender` stops being consumed by RootNavigator at all — which is the honest statement, since it is a main-launch concept that no longer routes anything. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The 3-line block deleted and `useMainLaunchCoordinator` dropped from RootNavigator entirely — the derivation chain is written out in-file as the reason. `isReadyToRender` is now honestly a main-launch concept that routes nothing. | read all three files; derivation chain verified line by line
