F1000 | mobile-search | runtime/scheduler/runtime-work-scheduler.ts:148-153 + frame-budget-governor.ts:17-22 | THE ANTI-STARVATION MECHANISM IS STRUCTURALLY DEAD. `canStarvationOverride` = `starvationOverrideEligible && !isCriticalPressure() && !STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES.has(lane)`. `starvationOverrideEligible` can only become true for a lane with a FINITE `MAX_DEFERRED_FRAMES_BY_LANE` entry — and that map has exactly three keys {selection*feedback, phase_b_materialization, overlay_shell_transition}, ALL THREE of which are members of STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES. Every other lane gets POSITIVE_INFINITY and is never eligible. Therefore `canStarvationOverride` is a compile-time-constant FALSE, and the `deferredFrameCount` bookkeeping (L157) feeds nothing. A guard that cannot fail. Failure enabled: a deferred phase_b/overlay task can be starved indefinitely under sustained frame pressure with no escape hatch, while the code reads as though one exists. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting both "heavy lanes must not hog a frame" and "heavy lanes must not starve"; bedrock = those are the SAME set of lanes, so the two policies were written against each other; rederive = pick one owner for heavy-lane admission — either the governor's one-heavy-lane-per-frame rule (which already bounds hogging) OR a deferral cap, not both, and make the cap apply to the lanes it was written for; red-team = the dead override may be load-bearing-by-absence (enabling it could admit a heavy task during critical pressure and regress the very jank the governor was built to kill), so this must be re-derived as a decision, not "fixed" by deleting a set membership. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The override DIED rather than being switched on: MAX_DEFERRED_FRAMES_BY_LANE, STARVATION_OVERRIDE_DISABLED_LANES (the byte-identical HEAVY_LANES duplicate), canStarvationOverride and the deferredFrameCount field+bookkeeping are all deleted. ONE OWNER for heavy-lane admission — the governor one-heavy-lane-per-frame rule. Enabling the override would have been an unmeasured behavior change under exactly the pressure the governor exists to relieve (no-fake-estimates law), so the honest move was deletion plus a comment naming the fairness that actually exists: head-retention (a deferred task is unshifted and the frame ENDS). PROOF: runtime-work-scheduler.spec.ts, new; RED PROVEN by changing the unshift to push (1 failure)
F1001 | mobile-search | runtime/scheduler/frame-budget-governor.ts:54,56,64 | SECOND DEAD GUARD IN THE SAME FILE. `canRun` returns false at L56 when `projectedSpentMs > HARD_BUDGET_MS` (12). L64 then returns `projectedSpentMs <= availableBudgetMs` where `availableBudgetMs = TARGET_FRAME_MS - RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` = 16.67-4 = 12.67. Any value reaching L64 is already <= 12, hence always <= 12.67 — L64 is tautologically TRUE. So `TARGET_FRAME_MS`, `RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` and `availableBudgetMs` are inert: three constants and a computation whose sole consumer can never be false. Ladder: compensates-for = two budget models (a frame-headroom model and a hard/soft-budget model) landed on top of each other; bedrock = there is ONE budget question ("may this task run in the remaining frame?"); rederive = delete the headroom model and keep hard/soft, or derive HARD_BUDGET_MS FROM `TARGET_FRAME_MS - RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS` so the constants are related by construction instead of by coincidence; red-team = the 12 vs 12.67 gap is only 0.67ms, so behavior is unchanged either way — this is a legibility/honesty fix, not a perf fix, and should not be sold as one. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | TARGET_FRAME_MS, RESERVED_HEADROOM_MS, availableBudgetMs and the tautological final return are deleted; canRun is now one budget model (hard 12 / soft 8, first-task exemption). isCriticalPressure() went with the override that was its only caller. Behavior identical by construction (the deleted check could never be false). PROOF: 5 governor cases in runtime-work-scheduler.spec.ts; RED PROVEN by moving HARD_BUDGET_MS to 20 (2 failures)
F1002 | mobile-search | runtime/map/map-query-budget.ts (whole file, 168 lines) | A WRITE-ONLY INSTRUMENT ON THE HYDRATION HOT PATH. `MapQueryBudget.snapshot()` has ZERO callers repo-wide (verified: grep for `mapQueryBudget.snapshot`, `MapQueryBudgetSnapshot`, `indexQueryDurationP95`, `runtimeAttributionTopContributors`, `fullCatalogScanCount` across apps/ + packages/ returns nothing outside the file itself). `resetRun()` also has zero callers. Meanwhile `recordRuntimeAttributionDurationMs` IS called on live paths (use-search-results-hydration-key-apply-runtime.ts:20-21, use-search-results-sectioned-projection-telemetry-runtime.ts:49, use-search-results-hydration-rows-release-emission-runtime.ts:35, search-runtime-profiler-attribution-runtime.ts:32). So every results hydration commit pays two Map get/set operations to accumulate numbers nobody ever reads, into totals nobody ever resets. Additionally `recordIndexQueryDurationMs`, `recordReadModelBuildSliceDurationMs`, `recordFullCatalogScan` and `incrementRuntimeCounter` have zero callers, so four snapshot fields (`indexQueryDurationP95`, `readModelBuildSliceP95`, `fullCatalogScanCount`, `runtimeCounters`) are structurally always 0/empty. This is the purest always-green instrument in the territory: it cannot show RED because nothing can observe it. Ladder: compensates-for = a measurement campaign whose reader (a debug surface) was never built or was deleted; bedrock = an instrument exists to be read, and per CLAUDE.md a metric that can only ever show green is lying; rederive = either wire a reader (the search-perf-debug surface) and add a per-probe `resetRun()` so the P95/mean semantics are honest, or delete the class and its five call sites; red-team = the nav-switch attribution instrument two files over (runtime/shared/search-nav-switch-runtime-attribution.ts) is the CORRECT shape — env-gated, bounded ring, zero-cost when off, genuinely readable — so the fix is to converge on that shape rather than invent a third. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — DELETED | BANKING-LAW RE-GREP DONE, wider than the original: mapQueryBudget/MapQueryBudget/map-query-budget/every snapshot field name across apps+packages+maestro+scripts+plans. Result: ZERO readers anywhere — no perf verb, no maestro flow, no debug surface. plans/perf-baselines/perf-shortcut-live-baseline.json DOES contain a recorded snapshot payload, i.e. a reader existed once, but its emitter is gone from the tree; the baseline is a historical artifact, not a live consumer. So the whole class was write-only, not just snapshot(): the one live method (recordRuntimeAttributionDurationMs) accumulated into totals only snapshot() could read. DELETED: map-query-budget.ts (168 lines) + search-runtime-profiler-attribution-runtime.ts (the recorder wrapper) + InstrumentationMapQueryBudget + all five call sites + the threading through 16 files (session core lane, route-scene contract, read-model selectors, the instrumentation/profiler runtimes, SearchMapWithMarkerEngine, search-map.tsx). Two adjacent lies died with it: search-map.tsx recordTimedRuntimeAttribution wrappers (and the now-unused getNowMs), and a rows-release span reporting `getNowMs() - getNowMs()` as a duration — the field is gone rather than shipping a permanent ~0. The correct shape to converge on if this instrument is ever rebuilt remains F1016
F1003 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus.ts:436-458 | THE BUS'S RESULTS-ATTRIBUTION INSTRUMENT IS BLIND TO ITS OWN TARGET CASE. `recordDiagnostic` early-returns at L436 when `durationMs < 0.25 && changedKeys.size <= 1`. The `logPerfScenarioStackAttribution` call for `results`/`resultsRequestKey` writes (L440-458) sits BELOW that filter. A fast single-key `results` publish — the most common results write there is — is therefore never attributed. The noise filter was placed above the signal it was meant to capture. Ladder: compensates-for = ring-buffer noise from trivial publishes; bedrock = "is this publish worth recording in the ring" and "does this publish need stack attribution" are two different questions with two different thresholds; rederive = hoist the `shouldCaptureStack` decision above the noise filter, keep the filter for the ring only; red-team = stack capture is expensive (it builds a stack trace), so hoisting it makes every single-key results publish pay that cost — the honest shape is to gate stack capture on the perf-scenario flag (as logPerfScenarioStackAttribution already does internally) rather than on duration, so the filter and the attribution stop fighting. | ESCALATED | read; the filter predicate and the attribution predicate are independent by construction
F1004 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus.ts:356-390 | NO LISTENER ERROR ISOLATION IN THE CENTRAL BUS. `notify()` invokes listeners inside a bare `this.listeners.forEach`. One listener that throws aborts the whole loop: every listener registered after it is silently never notified for that change, the `recordDiagnostic` call at L381 never runs, and the bus's `version` has already been bumped — so the surface is now desynced from a state the bus believes it published. Failure enabled: a single bad subscriber (e.g. a controller reading a torn-down ref) silently freezes an arbitrary subset of the search surface, with no error surfaced and no diagnostic recorded — an intermittent "half the screen stopped updating" with a clean log. Ladder: compensates-for = nothing; this is an omission, not a compensation; bedrock = a fan-out must not let one subscriber's failure determine another subscriber's delivery; rederive = wrap each `listener()` in try/catch, record the throw into the diagnostics ring (so it can show RED), and continue the loop; red-team = swallowing listener errors can hide real bugs, so the catch must RECORD (ring + logger.warn) rather than ignore — a silent catch here would trade one invisible failure for another. Also note Map.forEach visits entries ADDED during iteration, so a listener that subscribes during notify is notified for a change it did not observe from before subscribing (minor, but same loop). | ESCALATED | read; no try/catch anywhere in notify/publish/batch
F1005 | mobile-search | runtime/resolver/search-world-resolver.ts:360-361 | CANCELLATION IS CLASSIFIED BY SNIFFING AN ERROR MESSAGE STRING. `const isCanceled = reason.includes('canceled') || reason.includes('runSearch returned no response');` decides whether a failed resolution raises a user-visible failure level (retry chip + failed empty state) or is treated as expected lifecycle. Failure enabled, both directions: (a) any upstream reword, axios/AbortController message change, or localized message silently reclassifies a normal session-exit cancellation as a real failure — the user gets a failure banner on an ordinary back-navigation; (b) a genuine network error whose message happens to contain the substring "canceled" is silently swallowed and never reported. The comment claims this is "classified HERE, once" — which is true and good — but the classifier itself is the weak link. Ladder: compensates-for = the fetch layer not distinguishing cancellation from failure in its type; bedrock = cancellation is a distinct OUTCOME of a resolution, not a flavour of error text; rederive = have `fetchWorldForTuple` reject with a typed `SearchFetchCanceledError` (or resolve a discriminated `{kind:'canceled'}`), and check `instanceof`/`kind` here; red-team = the second substring ('runSearch returned no response') encodes a REAL second condition (an empty response treated as a cancellation), so a naive `instanceof AbortError` swap would lose it — the typed outcome must cover both cases, which is an argument for a discriminated result rather than an error subclass. | ESCALATED | read; both substrings are load-bearing today
F1006 | mobile-search | runtime/resolver/search-world-resolver.ts:403 | A CURRENCY GUARD THAT CANNOT FAIL, NEXT TO THE REAL ONE. `resolveNextPage` calls `core.land({generation, worldKey: appendKey}, () => true)` — the currency predicate is the literal `true`, and the returned disposition is DISCARDED. The real currency check is then done separately at L404 (`if (isTupleStillDesired(tuple))`). Compare the page-one path at L315-318, where the same `core.land` call passes the real predicate and BRANCHES on the disposition. So the append path has the same question answered twice, once vacuously and once for real, in two different mechanisms. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting `core.land` to always clear the in-flight append key regardless of currency (a legitimate need — the key must be released or load-more wedges forever); bedrock = "release the in-flight key" and "may this result present?" are two responsibilities the resolver core currently fuses into one call; rederive = give the core an explicit `release(key)` separate from `land(key, isCurrent)`, so the append path releases unconditionally and asks the currency question once, in one place; red-team = splitting the core's API touches the page-one path too and the core has a spec (search-world-resolver-core.spec.ts) that encodes the fused semantics — this is a real refactor, not a one-liner, and the current code is CORRECT, just doubly-stated. | ESCALATED (legibility; no live defect) | read + compared against the page-one path at L315

F1008 | mobile-search | runtime/map/camera-intent-arbiter.ts:103-120,207-218 | `allowDuringGesture` IS HONORED BY commit() BUT NOT BY THE COMPLETION TRACKER, SO THE DEFERRED CAMERA-STATE SYNC IS SILENTLY DROPPED. `commit()` admits an intent during a gesture when `allowDuringGesture === true` (L124) and arms `pendingProgrammaticCameraCompletionId` plus, for `deferControlledCameraStateUntilCompletion` callers, `pendingControlledCameraStateSync` (L165-175). But `setGestureActive(true)` unconditionally cancels and CLEARS both (L109-118), and map-interaction-controller.ts:270 calls `setGestureActive(isUserViewportGestureActive)` on EVERY native viewport-changed tick — many times per second during a pan. So an allowDuringGesture intent issued mid-gesture is un-tracked within roughly one frame: its completion is reported as 'cancelled' though the animation was explicitly permitted, and its deferred controlled-camera-state sync (center/zoom/bearing/pitch/padding) NEVER FLUSHES — the JS-side controlled camera state stays stale. Reachable: `allowDuringGesture: true` has six live call sites (profile-native-command-runtime.ts:45, use-search-session-origin-camera-runtime.ts:73, use-search-root-map-bootstrap-runtime.ts:80, use-search-runtime-instrumentation-runtime.ts:174/223/250, navigation/runtime/use-app-route-scene-camera-motion-target-runtime.ts:111). ATTRIBUTION CORRECTION, recorded honestly: my first hypothesis was a PERMANENT wedge (pending id stuck forever, `syncObservedCameraViewport` dead for the session). That was WRONG — I red-teamed it against map-interaction-controller.ts:270/435/575/582 and found the repeated setGestureActive(true) self-heals the stuck-id case within a frame. The surviving defect is narrower and real: the dropped deferred sync + a lying 'cancelled' status. Ladder: compensates-for = wanting a user gesture to always win over a programmatic camera; bedrock = `allowDuringGesture` is the caller declaring THIS intent coexists with the gesture, and that declaration must be carried through the whole lifecycle, not just the admission check; rederive = store `allowDuringGesture` on the pending record and have setGestureActive skip cancellation for pendings that carry it; red-team = the gestureActive clause in `consumeProgrammaticCameraCompletion` (L210) is ALSO redundant for its stated purpose — setGestureActive(true) already clears the pending id, so the id-match at L211 alone rejects stale completions — meaning the safest minimal change is to drop L210's gestureActive clause and gate the cancellation, but this is the finished-map's JS seam and must be validated by eye (a profile-open camera move overlapping a pan) before any edit. | ESCALATED (owner validation required — JS map seam) | read + reachability grep + red-teamed against the gesture controller
F1009 | mobile-search | runtime/map/camera-intent-arbiter.ts:177-189 vs 191-203 | TRUTH IN TWO PLACES: the camera writer sequence is duplicated verbatim. Lines 177-189 (the `commandCameraViewport` succeeded, non-deferred arm) and 191-203 (the fell-through arm) are byte-identical 13-line blocks: setMapCameraAnimation → padding sync → setMapCenter → setMapZoom → conditional bearing → conditional pitch. A third near-copy of the same sequence lives at L232-247 (`flushControlledCameraStateSync`). Any change to write ORDER (which matters — animation mode must be set before center/zoom or the move animates when it should snap) must be made in two-to-three places. Ladder: compensates-for = an early-return structure that made the shared tail awkward to hoist; bedrock = there is ONE way to write a camera to the map; rederive = extract `applyCameraWriters({center, zoom, bearing, pitch, padding, shouldSyncPadding, animation})` and call it from all three sites, leaving only the deferred arm as a genuine early return; red-team = the three sites differ subtly (the flush path forces `mode:'none'` and uses `hasOwnProperty` on a constructed object where the property ALWAYS exists — L242/245 are themselves guards that cannot fail), so the extraction must preserve the "was this key present on the caller's intent" semantics rather than the flush path's always-true version, or bearing/pitch resets change behavior. | ESCALATED | read; three copies, two of them byte-identical
F1010 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/ (42 files, ~874 lines) | DEAD FILES: 42 of 534 non-spec files in runtime/shared have ZERO importers repo-wide. F1011 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-runtime-bus-context.ts (whole file) | A DEAD SECOND REACT CONTEXT FOR THE SAME BUS — dead code that is also a LANDMINE. This file creates its own `React.createContext<SearchRuntimeBus|null>(null)` and exports `SearchRuntimeBusProvider` + `useSearchRuntimeBus`. The LIVE context is a different object created in search-runtime-bus.ts:495 (`SearchRuntimeBusContext`) with its own `useSearchBus`. Both are zero-importers today, but if a future reader picks `SearchRuntimeBusProvider` from this file while consumers use `useSearchBus` from the other, the two contexts never meet and every consumer throws 'must be used within a SearchRuntimeBusContext.Provider' — a confusing failure whose cause is two files that look interchangeable. Ladder: compensates-for = an extraction of the context out of the bus module that was started and abandoned; bedrock = one bus, one context, one provider, one hook; rederive = delete this file (the live context already lives with the bus and the co-location is fine); red-team = the extraction instinct was not wrong — a context in its own module avoids importing the whole bus class for a type-only consumer — but if that is wanted, MOVE the live one rather than leaving a decoy, and delete either way. Trivial, zero-blast, covered by F1010's bulk delete. | ESCALATED (trivial; NOT landed) | read both files; two distinct createContext calls
F1012 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/ (structural) | THE CENTRAL ARCHITECTURAL FACT OF THIS TERRITORY, measured rather than asserted: runtime/shared holds 534 non-spec files with a MEDIAN of 55 lines (p25=30, p75=91, min=5, max=1475); 187 files are under 40 lines; 67 export no runtime value at all (type-only). 324 of 534 (61%) have exactly ONE consumer in the entire repo, and 42 have none (F1010). Three independent full-read passes over disjoint sub-clusters converged on the same verdict from different evidence: 48 of 61 files in the overlay cluster, 35 of 176 in the search-root cluster, and ~6 in the results-presentation cluster are PURE PASS-THROUGHS — a hook whose body forwards its arguments to one child hook, spreads two children with no logic, or wraps a single already-stable value in a useMemo. Named families: `*-args.ts`(foreground-editing, 7 files, a four-level spread tree producing one flat object),`_-ports.ts`(submit, 13 files, of which 3 are load-bearing),`_-patch-runtime.ts`(hydration, 8 files, four levels deep to assemble a six-field object, one leaf existing solely to return the literal`false`), `_-lane.ts`/`_-control-lanes.ts`(11 single-field useMemo wrappers across 3 files). Ladder: compensates-for = a per-file size budget applied to composition code, plus a belief that each seam is a memoization boundary; bedrock = a seam earns its file only if it owns something — state, a lifetime, a subscription, or a memo the caller could not already have; rederive = keep the seams that own LIFETIME (the`_-authority-runtime`family, the map ref-latch bridges, the stage boundaries — these are genuinely excellent) and collapse the four ceremonial families, removing roughly 30-40 files and ~700-1000 lines without moving a single behavioral decision; red-team = THIS IS THE MOST DANGEROUS PROPOSAL IN THE PASS and must not be run as a mechanical sweep. Three concrete reasons: (a) the decomposition is load-bearing for the CLAUDE.md law that scene body-spec hooks never fire effects — collapsing a body-spec hook into a committed one, or vice versa, silently changes whether effects run, and the boundary is not visible from the file name; (b) the pass-through hooks preserve hook ORDER, which F1013 shows is the real wiring contract — merging two hooks changes call order; (c) at least one ceremonial family actively CONCEALED a live defect (the hydration patch chain hid a missing subscription), which argues the collapse is valuable but also that these files are load-bearing as the place bugs currently hide, so the collapse must be done cluster-by-cluster with tsc+jest green at each step, not repo-wide.  PHASE-3 (2026-08-03): guard built FIRST, then ONE cluster collapsed. The hook-order guard lives in the react-hook-harness (spec-support/react-hook-harness.ts): every hook primitive now records its KIND, and mountHook throws on any change of hook COUNT or ORDER across renders, naming the position and both kinds — proven RED twice in spec-support/react-hook-order-guard.spec.ts (a second-render reorder, and a conditionally-called child hook, the exact shape a careless inline produces). Cluster collapsed: the foreground-editing `*-args` family — a perfect 3-level tree (runtime-args -> {state-args, action-args}; action-args -> {clear, autocomplete, presentation, search-ui}) with EXACTLY ONE consumer per file and zero external importers. Six files deleted (~328 lines), the seventh (use-search-root-foreground-editing-runtime-args.ts) keeps the public seam and now assembles the object in ONE useMemo whose dep array is the UNION of the six leaf dep arrays — provably the same invalidation trigger set, since intermediate memos cannot make the root recompute less often. 7 useMemo calls -> 1. Pinned by a new equivalence spec asserting every field by source path, memo identity stability across a hit and a miss, and a stable hook sequence. tsc clean; 572 tests (was 566). | ONE-CLUSTER-COLLAPSED (F1012 remains OPEN for the *-ports, *-patch-runtime and *-lane families) | measured (wc/grep census) + three independent full-read passes + phase-3 collapse of one cluster under the new guard
F1013 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-root-runtime-stage-runtime.ts:36-217, use-search-root-runtime-control-stage-runtime.ts:61-231, use-search-root-control-foundation-authority-runtime.ts:48-75 | CALLER-MUST-REMEMBER: THE WIRING IS THE HOOK CALL ORDER, AND NOTHING ENFORCES IT. Fourteen stages in the top orchestrator and twelve in the control stage consume each other by field path;`clearRestoreAuthorityRuntime`must be called LAST because it takes the other four authorities as inputs. A future edit that hoists one hook call — a natural "group the authorities together" tidy — produces`undefined` reads four levels deep (`controlAuthorityRuntime.foundationAuthorityRuntime.profileBridgeAuthorityRuntime.profileBridge`) that TypeScript cannot catch, because every intermediate is typed non-optional. Ladder: compensates-for = React's linear-hook-order requirement, which is a REAL constraint, not an invented one; bedrock = dataflow is a DAG and hook order is one valid topological sort of it; rederive = there is nothing better to build here — this IS the honest encoding of a DAG in hooks, and inverting it into a context/provider tree would hide the order behind indirection while adding render layers, which is strictly worse; the cheap and correct win is a one-line ordering comment at each of the three stage boundaries naming what depends on what, so the constraint is documented rather than archaeological. PHASE-3 (2026-08-03): the constraint is now MACHINE-CHECKABLE, not just documentable — the react-hook-harness records hook kind + order and throws on drift, so any composition with a spec gets the ordering contract enforced. Note the standing gap this closes only partly: apps/mobile/.eslintrc.js has NO react-hooks plugin (F808), so neither rules-of-hooks nor exhaustive-deps runs in lint; the harness guard is the only automated hook-order check in the mobile app, and it only covers compositions that have a spec. | IDEAL-VERIFIED (shape is right; guard now exists — the three ordering comments are still owed) | read all three stage files end-to-end

F957 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/scene-chrome-geometry.ts + app-overlay-route-types.ts:354-413 + app-overlay-route-stack-algebra.ts + app-overlay-route-params-equality.ts + app-route-presentation-frame-contract.ts + docked-scene-target.ts + edit-session-liveness-contract.ts + app-route-sheet-motion-descriptor-table.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, eight modules, argued. (1) `scene-chrome-geometry.ts` is the MODEL ANSWER for the magic-number class: every term in `computeSceneChromeHeight` is a named declared constant, the header explains why the value is COMPUTED rather than measured (it killed a cache plus a guess-then-correct pipeline), it says why the result is deliberately NOT rounded (RN's pixel grid), and it names a dev-time computed-vs-measured bark that can show RED. (2) app-overlay-route-types.ts:354-413 — `OverlayKeysWithMetadataFlag` + `AssertNoOverlayKeys<Exclude<...>>` proves a metadata flag and its declaration-ordered array are the same set IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, with a build error that NAMES the offending key. This is the pattern every hand-list in F945/F946/F947 should become. (3) `app-overlay-route-stack-algebra.ts` — pure reducers, no I/O; `previousOverlayRoute` is DERIVED (:175-176) rather than stored, with the bug that motivated it named; `resolveSessionDismissPlan` (:345-370) puts the shape decision in the algebra and leaves choreography to executors. (4) `app-overlay-route-params-equality.ts` — `satisfies { [K in OverlayKey]: Comparator<K> }` replaced shape-sniffing with compile-exhaustive per-key dispatch, and its spec (:284-289) contains the RED case ("two entries of different keys with identical param shapes are UNEQUAL") annotated with proof that it fails against the old implementation. A test that documents which bug it would have caught is the gold standard. (5) `app-route-presentation-frame-contract.ts` — one value, one writer; the header names the five differently-timed derivations it replaced and the race they caused, and divergence is ENUMERATED as legal/illegal rather than left emergent. (6) `docked-scene-target.ts` — a leaf module whose header states a constraint no type system can express ("hosting the values there produced a real boot-order TDZ that tsc and jest cannot see") at the exact place it binds, with a spec that turns a half-revert RED. (7) `edit-session-liveness-contract.ts:38-58` — the counted registry done right: per-scene refcount, idempotent release, entry DELETED at zero, notify only on 0<->1 edges, spec exercises double-release. Four sibling registries use the same identity-checked unregister; this directory is genuinely disciplined about registry disarm. (8) `app-route-sheet-motion-descriptor-table.ts` + spec — the snap decision as DATA with a stated precedence, `__DEV__` ambiguity asserts at module init, `topLevelSwitch` rows DERIVED from the policy registry's `postureSeat` rather than hand-listed, and a parity oracle whose header instructs how to update it on an intentional tune — rare and correct: it distinguishes "pins accidental divergence" from "freezes the design". | - | read

The whole family assembles TWO composite values (`SearchRouteSceneLayoutState`, `SearchRouteSheetVisualSelectionSnapshot`) from two source authorities. Ladder: compensates-for = a real past problem (one giant recompute re-notifying everyone); bedrock = a controller exists per INDEPENDENTLY-CHANGING fact; rederive = collapse to one controller per source authority with derived selectors, keeping the two composite outputs byte-identical. The '35-45+ FPS' bare-number goal is deliberately not carried over, with the reason stated. CRAVE.md's reading-list reference updated. | repo-wide grep of both symbols
F962 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/ADDING_A_SCENE.md:179-192 vs docked-scene-target.ts:36 | THE STRANGER-FACING DOC IS STALE ON THE ONE CONSTANT IT TELLS YOU TO TRUST — and its central claim is disproved by F953. (3) The "no lane/runtime code names the scene directly" guarantee is FALSE — F953 found three live sites that hardcode `'polls'` where the home seat is meant (app-search-route-command-runtime.ts:43-47, use-app-route-shared-sheet-runtime.ts:101/125/175, app-route-shared-sheet-presentation-controller.ts:22/118), plus a test comment still asserting "Home's carrier scene is 'polls'". F963 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/edit-mode-session-core.ts:18 | CORRECTNESS BY UNSTATED COINCIDENCE. `return baseline == null || baseline.join(' ') !== live.order.join(' ');` compares two string-id arrays by joining them with a space. This is only correct because no id may contain a space — a fact the file never states and nothing enforces. If any id ever gains a space (a slug, a composite key, a server-generated label), `['a b','c']` and `['a','b c']` become indistinguishable and the dirty check silently returns false, discarding a real edit as a no-op cancel. The module is otherwise exemplary — it is reanimated-free precisely so the dirty semantic is jest-provable, and its comment names the previous wrong semantic (`history.length > 1`) and the red-team nit that killed it. Ladder: rederive = compare by `length` plus `every((id, i) => id === other[i])` — strictly correct, cheaper, and removes the unstated precondition. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | Element-wise comparison (length + every) replaces `join(' ')`. Strictly correct, cheaper, and the unstated 'no id may contain a space' precondition — whose violation silently discarded a real edit as a no-op cancel — is gone. | read
F1018 | mobile-search | utils/format.ts:31-46 + components/render-meta-detail-line.tsx:54-63 | CLIENT RE-DERIVATION OF A SERVER TRUTH, WITH THE WRONG CLOCK — the highest-severity truth-in-2-places in the territory. The server already computes `closesInMinutes` IN THE RESTAURANT'S OWN TIMEZONE (apps/api/src/modules/search/utils/restaurant-status.ts:205-215,224-234). The client fallback `minutesUntilCloseFromDisplay` re-parses the human string `closesAtDisplay` ("9:30 pm") and subtracts the DEVICE-LOCAL clock (`new Date().getHours()*60+getMinutes()`). A null-producing projection genuinely exists: apps/api/src/modules/user-lists/user-list.mappers.ts:425,525 returns `closesInMinutes: null` while still supplying `closesAtDisplay`. Failure: a user in NYC viewing an Austin restaurant closing at 10:00pm CT is evaluated against 11:00pm ET → diff = -60, which is not < -60, so the function returns null and the "Closes at 10:00 PM" amber warning silently never appears; two more timezones and `diff += 24*60` makes it read as closing in 23 hours. Ladder: compensates-for = list/preview projections dropping the field; bedrock = the server owns closing time and is the ONLY party that knows the restaurant's timezone — a device clock can never answer this; rederive = populate `closesInMinutes` in the list/preview mappers from the same `evaluateOperatingStatus`, then delete the client parser; red-team = the fallback is load-bearing TODAY, so deleting it before the server fix removes the closing-soon cue on those surfaces — the order is server-first, then delete, never the reverse. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | CHECKED THE API FIRST, and the finding overstated the server gap: apps/api restaurant-status.ts is the ONLY producer of closesAtDisplay repo-wide, and it sets closesInMinutes on the SAME return in every branch (open -> both present via computeMinutesUntilClose which returns a number, closed -> both null). The named null-producing mappers (user-list.mappers.ts:425,525) emit operatingStatus: null ENTIRELY — no display string either — so they never feed the fallback. NO SERVER CHANGE WAS NEEDED. The client parser was therefore both unreachable-by-contract and wrong: minutesUntilCloseFromDisplay + parseTimeDisplayToMinutes are DELETED and render-meta-detail-line consumes status.closesInMinutes alone. The invariant the client now leans on is LOCKED at the source: two new cases in apps/api restaurant-status.spec.ts (open always carries a numeric closesInMinutes; evaluation happens in the restaurant timezone, not the caller clock). It cannot fire today only because the sole caller renders the component inside `shouldRenderSuggestionPanel ? … : null` AND separately passes `visible={shouldRenderSuggestionPanel}` (SearchSuggestionSurface.tsx:193-195) — so `visible` is always true when mounted. The day someone keeps the component mounted and toggles `visible`, React throws "Rendered fewer hooks than expected" and the entire search surface white-screens. Ladder: compensates-for = a `visible` prop that predates the parent's mount gate; bedrock = hooks are unconditional, and a component must not depend on a caller remembering to gate it twice; rederive = delete the `visible` prop entirely (the parent already gates mounting) or move the early return below the hooks; red-team = deleting the prop touches one call site and carries zero risk — this is the cheapest high-value fix in the pass. | ESCALATED (trivial; NOT landed) | read both files
F1036 | mobile-search | (components layer, assorted) | SMALLER CONFIRMED ITEMS. (a) components/TopFoodPreMeasure.tsx:29-31 — `remainingRef` is seeded once via `useRef(items.length + moreCounts.length)` and `completedRef`/`done` latch permanently, so if the parent keeps the instance mounted and passes a new `items` array the component is already `done`, returns null, and the new widths are NEVER measured — cards silently fall back to `maxInline` forever. Works today only because the parent presumably remounts by key; the fix must key on a CONTENT signature, not object identity, or it loops. (b) components/metric-icons.tsx:26-28 and components/search-bottom-nav-icons.tsx:34-36 hold byte-identical `<Path>` data; the second site's comment names a real layering concern ("nav chrome never imports screen-component internals") but the answer is a shared `components/icons/` primitive, not a copy. (c) components/search-map.tsx — `areCoordinatesEqual` (:361-372) and `areCentersEqual` (:2161-2169) are identical functions IN THE SAME FILE, both live; `DOT_TEXT_SIZE = 17` (:924) exists solely to derive `DOT_TAP_INTENT_RADIUS_PX` (:957) but dots are pre-baked ICON SPRITES now, not text glyphs (:1858-1866 says so) — a tap radius derived from a font size that no longer renders anything; `arePropsEqual` (:2202-2285) is a hand-maintained comparator that already OMITS four props, and `mapCameraAnimation` is declared, passed by the engine, and never destructured by `SearchMap` — a prop threaded three layers to nowhere, i.e. adding a prop silently does nothing unless you also edit the comparator, and four have already fallen through; :616-623 puts a `console.log` SIDE EFFECT inside a memo comparator (dev-only, but a comparator with side effects is a trap); :1973 assigns `nativeViewportChangedHandlerRef.current` during render, closing over a `handleMapIdle` declared 48 lines later. (d) search-perf-debug.ts:72-105 — `PROD_THRESHOLDS` is selected when `!__DEV__` but EVERY consumer flag is `isDevEnvironment && …`, so in production no threshold is ever read and no log is ever emitted; the module looks like production telemetry and emits nothing. (e) Stale `market` vocabulary survives in search-map.tsx:259-263 and :671-673 — comments only (no live market code anywhere), but they are the EXPLANATORY comments for the `isInvisibleResident` filter, so a reader chasing "market" finds an exterminated system and mistrusts a live filter; rewrite to "out-of-searched-bounds sibling locations". (f) Eight `__DEV__` console probes run string-formatting work inside the frame-submit hot path ([BASELEDGER] on every transport build AND every ack with JSON.stringify, [GENREUSE] on every structural frame, [NGAPJS], [CATALOG], [PRESENTER], [NATIVE-SNAP], [FADE-OUT-ACK], [CAMPAD]) — production is clean, but they measure INTENT (which branch ran) rather than composite output, in exactly the environment where you profile feel; gate them as a set behind the existing `searchPerfDebug` module rather than deleting any (several were the levers that solved real defects). F964 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/sheetUtils.ts:65-88 vs apps/mobile/src/navigation/runtime/AppRouteSceneChromeMotionRuntimeProvider.tsx:55-68 | THE SNAP GEOMETRY IS COMPUTED IN TWO PLACES WITH THE SAME UNEXPLAINED LITERALS. `calculateSnapPoints` (the shared sheet snap resolver, 12 consumers) computes `rawMiddle = screenHeight * 0.4`, `middle = Math.max(expanded + 96, rawMiddle)`, `hidden = screenHeight + 80`, `clampedMiddle = Math.min(middle, hidden - 120)`. The chrome-motion provider's `buildExpandedMiddleChromeSnaps` (F949) recomputes the IDENTICAL four expressions with the IDENTICAL four literals. Neither file explains any of them, and neither imports the other. So the sheet and its chrome derive their geometry independently from a copied formula — the classic setup where tuning one drifts the other silently. `hidden - 120` is `screenHeight - 40` written the long way in both. Additional unexplained literals local to `calculateSnapPoints`: `resolvedHeaderHeight = headerHeight > 0 ? headerHeight : 96` (a magic fallback height for a value the repo now computes exactly — see `computeSceneChromeHeight`, F957 — so the fallback should be the computed height, not 96) and `finalCollapsed = Math.max(navAlignedCollapsed, clampedMiddle + 24)`. Ladder: bedrock = one snap-geometry function; rederive = the chrome provider imports `calculateSnapPoints` (it already receives the same inputs), and every literal becomes a named constant with a stated derivation, the way overlay-chrome-metrics.ts already does for the chrome row (F966). | FIXED with F949 (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | ONE formula: `resolveExpandedMiddleSnaps(screenHeight, searchBarTop, insetTop)` in sheetUtils, called by BOTH `calculateSnapPoints` and the chrome provider. Literals named with stated-or-explicitly-lost derivations; `hidden - 120 === screenHeight - 40` recorded as one number written two ways and deliberately left expressed relative to `hidden`. The save-list pair is named but deliberately NOT reconciled with the sheet's (making them equal is a design decision). RECORDED DEBT: the `headerHeight > 0 ? : 96` fallback should be `computeSceneChromeHeight`, left as-is because it moves the collapsed detent on the pre-measure frame and wants a sim look. | read both

F965 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/sheetUtils.ts:6-14,32,39-58 | DEAD EXPORTS in the shared sheet utility module (verified: repo-wide grep for each symbol, excluding the defining file, returns ZERO). `SHEET_STATES`, `snapPointForState`, `SMALL_MOVEMENT_THRESHOLD`, and the `SheetGestureContext` type are all unused. `snapPointForState` in particular is a worklet-safe snap-lookup that the gesture runtimes reimplement by indexing the `SnapPoints` record directly. Also note two springs coexist with no stated relationship: `SHEET_SPRING_CONFIG` here is `{damping: 28, stiffness: 320, overshootClamping: false}` while `DEFAULT_TRANSITION_SPRING_CONFIG` (transition-lane-player.ts:38) is `{damping: 28, stiffness: 220, overshootClamping: true}` — the second documents its near-critical derivation, the first documents nothing. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | `SHEET_STATES`, `snapPointForState`, `SMALL_MOVEMENT_THRESHOLD`, `SheetGestureContext` deleted (banked re-grep: zero hits outside the file). The two springs' relationship is now STATED on `SHEET_SPRING_CONFIG`: they drive different things (a physical sheet that may overshoot vs an invisible settle ramp that must not) and share a damping value by coincidence — written so nobody 'unifies' them. | grep verified per symbol

(1) `overlay-chrome-metrics.ts`: every chrome-row token is a named declared constant and `OVERLAY_TAB_HEADER_HEIGHT` is the literal SUM of them — so the height is a derivation, not a measurement or a guess. The header states why it is RN-free (so the pure geometry module and its jest contracts can consume it) and why the sum is physically true ("OverlaySheetHeaderChrome renders `fixedHeight` — text conforms to the box, never the reverse"). This is exactly what F949/F964's literals are missing. (2) `sheetBodyScrollDefaults.ts`: 30 lines of comment explaining the PHYSICS of the down-handoff, why three separate bounce flags are needed (iOS needs both `bounces` and `alwaysBounceVertical`; Android needs `overScrollMode`), that it is applied STRUCTURALLY after the prop spread so it cannot be overridden per scene, and — best of all — it records the alternative that was tried and REVERTED (a direction-gated bounce) with the two defects that killed it. A doc comment that names the road not taken is worth more than one that only describes the road taken. (3) `scene-chrome-ack-runtime.ts`: a module-scope store with ONE named writer, an explicit test seam, and a closing paragraph declaring what is now DEAD and unrepresentable (the measured-chrome-height cache) so nobody re-adds it. | - | read

F971 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/SearchMountedSceneBody.tsx:197-260 | THE LEAK CLASS, module-global variant. The landing clock's state is seven module globals (`landingSliceBase`, `landingSliceSnapshot`, `landingSliceCount`, `landingSliceTimer`, `landingRampHoldRelease`, `motionFencedListDataSnapshot`, `wasRedrawEpisodeLive`) driven by a self-rescheduling `requestAnimationFrame` chain (:235-236) and a 700ms `setTimeout` (:228) — with NO unmount path. The data authority's subscribe cleanup (:378) only removes the listener. If the search body unmounts mid-landing, the rAF chain keeps re-scheduling itself and keeps mutating globals for a component that no longer exists, and the NEXT mount inherits a half-advanced clock (a partially-sliced list). Every one of those globals also pins the previous episode's `primaryData`/`secondaryData` arrays alive. Same family: `postPhotosPendingAssets.ts:15` is a `Map` keyed by a minted `post-photos-${Date.now()}-${n}` nonce — UNBOUNDED — whose `release` is only called on the funnel's all-done close, so every ABANDONED photo funnel retains full-resolution `ImagePickerAsset` URIs + EXIF for the process lifetime; and `originSceneSegmentRuntime.ts:14` only shrinks on consume, so a staged restore whose scene never re-activates is permanent. Ladder: bedrock = state with a lifetime has an owner that ends it; rederive = move the landing clock onto the component (or give it an explicit `disposeLandingClock()` the body's unmount calls), and give the pending-assets map a bounded retention keyed to the funnel's route entry. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — all three instances, per the "state with a lifetime has an owner that ends it" bedrock. (1) The landing clock gained `disposeLandingClock()`, called when the LAST data-authority subscriber leaves (i.e. the search body unmounting): it cancels the pending rAF, releases the ramp hold, and resets all seven module globals. Previously the self-rescheduling rAF chain kept running and mutating globals for an unmounted component, pinned the previous episode's primaryData/secondaryData alive, and handed the NEXT mount a half-advanced clock — a partially-sliced list, a visible defect rather than merely retained bytes. (2) postPhotosPendingAssets is bounded (cap 4, oldest-first) because `release` is NOT guaranteed — an abandoned funnel retained full-resolution ImagePickerAsset URIs + EXIF for the process lifetime. PROVEN RED: postPhotosPendingAssets.spec.ts (3 cases) — deleting the eviction loop fails "EVICTS abandoned funnels" while stash/peek/release keeps passing. (3) originSceneSegmentRuntime ASSESSED NOT A LEAK and documented as such: keyed by the bounded scene vocabulary, one short string per key, overwritten rather than accumulated — an eviction policy there would be scaffolding without a cost to pay for. | read

OVERLAY_TAB_HEADER_HEIGHT)` arm (:100-102) unreachable; and `grabHandleCutout` is permanently `true`, so both `: null` arms (:204, :212) are dead. F980 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/BottomSheetSceneStackBodyLayer.tsx:69-71 + BottomSheetSceneStackHost.tsx:333-334 (+ 9 hand-written comparators) | THE MEMO LANDMINE — a caller-must-remember convention the codebase names twice, in its own words. "memo landmine: skip fns must compare every render-read prop or a unit change never re-renders the leg body" and "snapshot-equality landmine: render-read fields MUST be compared here or a unit change never republishes". Nine hand-written comparators across this scope (`shouldSkipSceneStackBodyContentLayerUpdate`, `shouldSkipSceneStackBodyContentUpdate`, `areSceneBodySurfaceSelectionsEqual`, `areSearchRouteSceneBodyContentSpecsEqual`, `areSearchRouteSceneBodyTransportSpecsEqual`, plus five in the host) each enumerate 10-20 fields BY HAND. Add one field to `SearchRouteSceneBodyTransportSpec`, forget one comparator, and that scene silently stops updating — a MISSING re-render, so nothing crashes and no test fails. Ladder: bedrock = equality is derived from the shape, not restated; rederive = generate the comparators from the type (a `satisfies { [K in keyof Spec]: (a,b)=>boolean }` map, the pattern app-overlay-route-params-equality.ts already uses successfully — F957 item 4), so a new field is a COMPILE ERROR naming the field. This is the single highest-leverage structural fix in the overlays root. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | New overlays/shape-equality.ts (FieldComparators<T> + createShapeEquality/createNullableShapeEquality) derives every comparator from its shape. Intentional omissions use a named `ignoreField` so they cannot be confused with forgotten ones. RED-proven twice: removing one entry from the transport map and from the surface map each gives "Property '<field>' is missing ... but required in type FieldComparators<T>". 535 tests green (behaviour identical)

F983 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/BottomSheetSceneStackListBodySurface.tsx:255-302 + SearchMountedSceneBody.tsx:796-855 | THE MVCP LAW HAS NO STRUCTURAL ENFORCEMENT AT THE TRANSPORT MERGE SITES. `grep -rn "maintainVisibleContentPosition" apps/mobile/src/overlays` (excluding panels/) returns NOTHING — the sheet machinery never sets it, so every FlashList it renders runs FlashList 2.0.2's default (ON). The risk: that per-scene `{ maintainVisibleContentPosition: { disabled: true } }` is threaded through FOUR spread/merge sites, and at least one of them spreads scene props in an order where a later object can overwrite an earlier one (`sceneSecondaryFlashListProps` at BottomSheetSceneStackListBodySurface.tsx:275 spreads `sceneSecondaryInputFlashListProps` AFTER the defaults; the dead BottomSheetWithFlashList.tsx:239-246 does the same with `activeFlashListProps` after `secondaryList.flashListProps`). There is no sweep and no type-level guard making "disabled on a re-sortable list" structural — it is a per-scene opt-in that the transport can silently drop. Given CLAUDE.md records this as a hard-won law with a specific expensive symptom, it deserves better than a spread order. F984 | mobile-nav-overlays | apps/mobile/src/overlays/bottomSheetSurfaceStyleUtils.ts + page-body-contract.ts + ChromeTitleText.tsx:21-24 + useBottomSheetSharedGestureRuntime.ts (the mount-stable pan memo) + BottomSheetSceneStackBodyActivityContext.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, five more, argued. Silent-strip converted into a compile error at the producer is the single highest-leverage move available in this codebase, and it was made here. (2) `page-body-contract.ts`: a closed, total five-state enum where the query lives in the controller and row components receive RESOLVED items — so a row component cannot express a pending branch because the pending case never renders it. With one interpreter (`PageBodyShell`) and one failure chokepoint, "a page-local retry view has nowhere to exist". Its spec asserts totality directly. That is the difference between a rule and an architecture. (3) `ChromeTitleText`'s `SingleLineText` brand makes a multi-line chrome title unrepresentable AT THE TYPE LEVEL, not merely clipped at render — the correct answer to the same class as F980's memo landmine, and worth citing as the target shape. (5) `BottomSheetSceneStackBodyActivityContext`'s volatility split — separating the transition-STABLE object from a primitive-valued `isActive` context, because bundling them handed every consumer a new context object per transition and re-rendered whole resident bodies (~75ms, measured, `[COMMITDBG]`-attributed). F1037 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/search-open-now-projection.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED on the single most-suspected client-re-derivation in the territory — and the suspicion was WRONG, recorded so the next pass does not re-hunt it. "Open now" is NOT recomputed client-side: the projection FILTERS on the server-provided `operatingStatus.isOpen` (rows) and the API's per-feature `isOpen` (coverage). It never parses an hours string and never consults the device clock. Three things make it exemplary: (1) rows WITHOUT openness data are DROPPED, explicitly "matching the server's open-now post-filter semantics exactly" — the client mirrors server semantics rather than inventing gentler ones; (2) `coverageCarriesOpenness` is a real VERSION-SKEW guard that can fail — an older API omitting `isOpen` would silently empty the coverage, so the projection DECLINES instead of producing a confidently-wrong empty map; (3) the projected totals are marked PROVISIONAL in the metadata and the resolver settles them with the honest server slice as a version update (no second reveal), so the optimistic paint can never harden into a wrong number. This is the correct shape for optimistic client projection of a server truth, and it is the counter-example that isolates F1018/F1019: the re-derivation disease in this territory is NOT in the runtime layer, it is confined to the components/utils layer. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | full read + compared against the server's open-now post-filter
F1038 | mobile-search | runtime/camera/resolve-fit-all-camera.ts + resolve-focus-camera.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED — the territory's best example of the instrument doctrine applied to a CONTROL path, not just a telemetry one. `commitFitAllCamera` "returns false when the arbiter rejects (gesture) — THE CALLER MUST TREAT AN UNEXECUTED INTENT AS A RED BARK, NEVER SILENCE", and use-search-root-submit-control-runtime.ts:99-105 does exactly that. The math is derived, not tuned: the fit solves `z = log2(C / requiredMetersPerPixel)` from mercator metersPerPixel rather than guessing zoom levels, and the focus camera's outlier cut is a stated algorithm (greedy growth admitting ds[k] while ds[k] <= max(D*FLOOR, ALPHA * self-median)) with its own golden specs and injectable tunables — so the constants are parameters of a documented rule, not magic. Owner decrees are named as decrees ("EXACT by owner decree — no exceptions"; lists deliberately do NOT use the outlier cut) rather than smuggled in as defaults. Only blemish: the stale "cross-market" vocabulary in the comments (informal distance language left over from the exterminated markets system) — cosmetic, covered by F1036e. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | read both + their specs

F1040 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-freeze-gate-state-runtime.ts:26-43 | A LIVE DEFECT, NOT A SMELL: THE FREEZE GATE IS SAMPLED ONCE PER SESSION AND NEVER UPDATES. `const sampledRuntimeState = React.useMemo(() => { const state = searchRuntimeBus.getState(); const policyFacts = searchRuntimeBus.getPolicyFactsSnapshot(); ... }, [searchRuntimeBus])` — the ONLY dependency is the bus OBJECT IDENTITY, and the bus is created once per session (use-search-route-results-policy-domain-runtime.ts:42). There is no `useSearchRuntimeBusSelector` and no `subscribe`. `useMemo` with a stable dep does not re-run on re-render — that is the entire point of the dep array — so `isSearchSurfaceRedrawChromeFreezeActive`, `isResponseFrameFreezeActive`, `freezeClassification` and `searchSurfaceRedrawPhase` are FROZEN AT FIRST RENDER FOR THE WHOLE SESSION, while `useSearchResponseFrameFreezeRuntime` — called from the same `useSearchFreezeGateRuntime` — faithfully publishes `isResponseFrameFreezeActive` into the bus on every response commit. The gate reports the boot-time value forever: if the surface renders while a redraw is in flight the gate says "not frozen" and chrome paints through the redraw, or the inverse, a boot-time freeze that never lifts. Contrast the sibling use-search-results-panel-hydration-runtime-state.ts:26-27, which also reads the bus during render but LISTS THE SAMPLED VALUES in its memo deps, so it at least re-samples when the parent re-renders. Ladder: compensates-for = re-render cost, a real concern since freeze state changes at high frequency and a naive subscription would re-render the tree; bedrock = freeze is a POLICY FACT and the bus already maintains a derived snapshot for exactly this (`getPolicyFactsSnapshot`, search-runtime-bus.ts:252); rederive = `useSearchRuntimeBusSelector` with the eight freeze keys and structural equality — key-scoped, so it costs one render per actual freeze EDGE, not per publish; red-team = the tempting objection "the parent re-renders on every bus publish anyway so the memo re-runs" is simply false for a stable dep, which is what makes this a live defect rather than a latent one. Severity is bounded by how many consumers still gate paint on these four booleans — that is the one thing to measure before editing. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | The useMemo sample is GONE: the hook is now `useSearchRuntimeBusSelector` scoped to the six bus keys that can move freeze (the four reported booleans + isChromeDeferred + searchSurfaceRedrawCommitSpanPressureActive) with structural equality, so it costs one render per freeze EDGE. The two returned shapes NO CALLER read (searchSurfaceRedrawRuntimeState, freezeGateRuntimeState) are deleted. PROOF: use-search-freeze-gate-state-runtime.spec.ts — new, and it required a new react-hook-harness (spec-support/react-hook-harness.ts) because the mobile jest project has no react-test-renderer; the harness implements React useMemo/useRef/useCallback/useSyncExternalStore with REAL semantics (a stable dep does not re-run). RED PROVEN: all 4 cases failed against the pre-fix hook (stale sample, no subscription, listenerCount 0)
F1041 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-dismiss-motion-plane-runtime.ts:266-307 | A WATCHDOG THAT OBSERVES A HANG IT CANNOT RECOVER, AND IN A USER SESSION DOES NOT EVEN LOG. `armDismissMotionBoundaryWatchdog` fires 420ms into a dismiss and its entire body is three early returns followed by ONE `logPerfScenarioAttributionEvent` — and the third early return is `if (!isPerfScenarioAttributionActive(...)) return` (:279), so in a real user session it does nothing at all. The hang it watches is real: the boundary commit requires `dismissMotionPollPageReadyForBoundary >= 0.5` (:629); when the sheet reaches the collapsed boundary but the poll page is not ready, the reaction sets a waiting flag and returns 0, deferring the commit to the readiness reaction at :594. If poll readiness never arrives (`pollHeaderReady && pollBodyReady && pollHostReady`, :159-162), `commitDismissBoundary` never runs, so `markDismissBoundaryReached` → `notifyCloseCollapsedBoundaryReached()` + `notifyCloseSheetSettled()` (:262-263) never fire and THE DISMISS NEVER COMPLETES — the sheet sits at the collapsed boundary forever. The instrument that exists to catch precisely this is off. This is the CLAUDE.md map-saga lesson recurring verbatim. OPEN QUESTION RESOLVED: I checked whether an outer deadline in the surface runtime rescues it — `search-surface-runtime.ts` has a tier-1/tier-2 cover watchdog ladder and a join-liveness force-reveal that barks LOUD `[TXN-CONTRACT]` (:884-886, :905, :976), but those govern the REVEAL/cover side; a grep for a dismiss-scoped timeout/watchdog/deadline in that file returns NOTHING. So no outer deadline was found on the dismiss boundary commit and the finding stands at full severity. Ladder: compensates-for = a genuine ordering need — the boundary must not commit before the incoming poll page can paint or you flash blank; bedrock = a readiness gate needs a DEADLINE, not merely an observer, and every other deferred path in this territory has one (search-fresh-bounds-capture.ts:74-79 races every native read against a timeout and fails open); rederive = at 420ms the watchdog should `commitDismissBoundary()` ANYWAY and report a contract violation — fail-open with a loud trace, matching the fresh-bounds policy of "an accuracy upgrade, never a blocker"; the perf-scenario gate should keep guarding the RICH payload only, never the recovery; red-team = fail-open at 420ms could commit the boundary while the poll page genuinely still needs 50ms, reintroducing the blank flash the deferral was built to prevent — so the deadline wants to be measured against real poll-readiness latency rather than reused from the 420ms proof-stage constant, and the recovery should be paired with the violation report so the frequency is visible before anyone tunes it. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) | Real recovery landed: an OUTER deadline (search-dismiss-boundary-recovery.ts, SEARCH*DISMISS_BOUNDARY_RECOVERY_DEADLINE_MS=1200) armed alongside the 420ms proof watchdog in observeDismissMotion. At the deadline a live, uncommitted dismiss is FORCE-COMPLETED (completeDismissBoundaryFromJs — the same effect as the commitDismissBoundary worklet, factored out and now also used by the canCompleteImmediately path) and the degrade is reported UNGATED in **DEV** ([TXN-DEGRADE] search_dismiss_boundary_recovery); the perf-scenario gate now guards only the rich payload, never the recovery. Both timers are cleared through one clearDismissMotionBoundaryTimers() at every teardown. DELIBERATE CHOICE, stated: 1200ms is NOT measured — poll-readiness latency has never been measured — it is a conservative bound paired with the loud report so frequency is visible BEFORE anyone tunes it; the 420ms proof constant was deliberately NOT reused. PROOF: search-dismiss-boundary-recovery.spec.ts reproduces the wedge (at boundary, waiting flag up, readiness never arrives) -> force_commit/poll_page_never_ready; RED PROVEN by returning already_committed instead (2 failures)

The submit owner's`onResolveFailed`handler classifies cancellation with the SAME string match the resolver uses —`reason.includes('canceled') || reason.includes('runSearch returned no response')`— so the fragile classifier is duplicated across the resolver/owner boundary, not merely used once. The offline arm is a documented owner call (pause, no abort, covers stay up) and is fine. This raises F1005's priority: it is not one weak classifier but one weak classifier with two call sites that must agree. Ladder: unchanged from F1005 — the resolver should reject with a typed reason`{kind:'canceled'|'offline'|'failed', message}`and both sites switch on`kind`; red-team = the typed reason must be threaded through EVERY reject site in the resolver, so this is a resolver-file change rather than a local one — low risk but not free, and it must land in one commit or the two string matchers will drift apart. | ESCALATED (merge with F1005) | two independent readers found the same classifier

STILL UNREAD, and nobody should assume these are clean: (a) runtime/profile — ALL 73 FILES, ZERO READ, only a structural inventory exists; this is now the single largest blind spot in the territory; (b) 40 of 53 runtime/controller files — the reader has structural metadata only (line count, presence of useEffect/setTimeout/rAF/catch, subscribe-vs-recompute shape) and one file (…sheet-control-selection-state-controller.ts) was truncated mid-recompute with no verdict; note that the ONE controller bug found (F1052f) turned up in the 13 that WERE read, and its three siblings prove the intended contract — so the unread 40 are a real risk surface, not a formality; (c) most of runtime/{map,read-models,native,reconciler,surface,mutations,adapters} (~65 files) — I read the highest-value ones personally (F1000-F1009, F1015, F1038) but not the rest; (d) runtime/reconciler specifically, which is the named caveat on F1049 — the hooks layer introduces no supersession bypass, but the reconciler's own supersede logic is unread.
