F1100 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/cravesearch.xcscheme:27-40 | STALE TEST TARGET IN THE ONLY SHARED SCHEME. The scheme's `<TestAction>` names a `TestableReference` with `BlueprintIdentifier = "00E356ED1AD99517003FC87E"` / `BlueprintName = "cravesearchTests"` / `BuildableName = "cravesearchTests.xctest"`. That target DOES NOT EXIST: `grep -c 00E356ED1AD99517003FC87E project.pbxproj` = 0, `grep -c cravesearchTests project.pbxproj` = 0, and the project has exactly ONE PBXNativeTarget ("cravesearch"). Failure scenario: `xcodebuild test -scheme cravesearch` cannot resolve the testable and fails before running anything — so the iOS app has no runnable test lane at all, and anyone who tries to add one hits a confusing "missing buildable" error rather than a blank slate. It is the Xcode template's default test target, deleted from the project but never from the scheme. Ladder: this compensated for nothing; it is pure template residue. Bedrock = the scheme lists only buildables that exist. | FIXED — PART (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03; proof in F1119: scheme `<TestableReference>` deleted, CI half was already true, lane MUTATION-PROVED RED). Creating an app-host XCTest target is deliberately NOT done and remains OWNER-DECISION — see F1120(a) | grep -c on the ABSOLUTE pbxproj path, both = 0; PBXNativeTarget regex returns ['cravesearch']

F1101 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/cravesearch.entitlements:1-8 (whole file) + apps/mobile/src/providers/AuthProvider.tsx:226 | THE ENTITLEMENTS FILE HARDCODES `aps-environment = development` AND NOTHING ELSE, while the app really does register for push: `Notifications.getExpoPushTokenAsync({ projectId })` runs in AuthProvider, and `src/services/push-permission.ts:79` requests the OS permission. `aps-environment` selects WHICH APNs the token is minted against — `development` = the sandbox gateway. Failure scenario: a TestFlight/App Store build carrying this value mints sandbox tokens; the api pushes to production APNs; every notification silently disappears with no client-visible error (BadDeviceToken server-side at most). This is the classic "push works on my sim, dies in TestFlight" bug, and it is invisible until real users are on the build. Caveat kept honest: EAS's managed-credential flow can rewrite the entitlement at archive time from the provisioning profile, so this MAY be masked in practice — but the repo cannot prove that, and `eas.json` has no ios credentials block. Ladder: the value is the Expo prebuild default for a dev-only app; it compensated for "we only ever ran a dev client". Bedrock = the entitlement is `$(APS_ENVIRONMENT)` driven per-configuration (development for Debug, production for Release), so the shipped value is a consequence of the build, not a constant. | OWNER-DECISION (verify against an actual TestFlight token before changing — instrument first per the ATTRIBUTE law) | plutil -p of the entitlements + grep of getExpoPushTokenAsync/requestPermissionsAsync in src/

F1102 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/eas.json:8-11 (build.base.env) | ONE API URL FOR EVERY BUILD PROFILE, AND IT IS THE RAW RAILWAY HOSTNAME. `base.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL = "https://api-production-a56f.up.railway.app/api/v1"`, and `development`, `development-device`, `preview` and `production` all `extends: "base"` without overriding it. Two failures. (1) NO STAGING CLIENT IS BUILDABLE. CLAUDE.md's DEPLOY LAW is "staging → production, always" and a staging api exists (MEMORY: live 2026-08-01), but there is no eas profile that points a client at it — so the staging api can only ever be exercised by curl or by hand-editing `.env.local`, never by a real installed build. The `preview` profile, whose entire job is that rehearsal, ships against PRODUCTION. (2) THE URL IS A PROVIDER-ASSIGNED HOST, not a domain we own. `api-production-a56f.up.railway.app` is Railway's generated subdomain; it is baked into every binary (EXPO*PUBLIC*\* inlines at bundle time and cannot be changed post-ship), so a Railway service rename/migration bricks every installed app with no server-side remedy. This is exactly the F803 class — a wrong/undeclared URL baked somewhere. | FIXED — the buildable half (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03; proof in F1119: eas.json gains a `staging` profile and `preview` extends it). The prod-host move to a domain we own is deliberately not done — see F1120(c) | read eas.json + app.json; no other EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL in any profile

F1103 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/app.json:17-31 (expo.ios) vs apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist | TWO SOURCES OF TRUTH FOR THE NATIVE CONFIG, ALREADY DIVERGED, WITH A DESTRUCTIVE RECONCILER. `ios/` is committed (bare workflow), so the checked-in Info.plist and .entitlements are what actually builds; `app.json`'s `ios.infoPlist`, `ios.entitlements` and `plugins: []` are INERT — they only take effect through `expo prebuild`. Proof of live divergence: Info.plist carries `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription`, `NSLocationAlwaysUsageDescription`, `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`, `NSAppTransportSecurity`, `CFBundleURLTypes` and `UIRequiredDeviceCapabilities` that app.json knows nothing about, while app.json's `plugins` array is EMPTY even though expo-camera, expo-image-picker, expo-location and expo-notifications all ship config plugins whose whole job is to inject those keys. Failure scenario, and it is the severe one: `expo prebuild` (or `--clean`, or any tool that runs it — `eas build` will run prebuild when it decides the native dirs are out of date) regenerates `ios/` from app.json, and app.json describes a project with NO SearchMapRenderController, NO MapLodKit/TrackScrollKit pods, NO custom Podfile, NO location strings. That is ~15k lines of owner-precious native code silently reverted to a template, plus a permission set the app no longer has. Ladder: app.json's ios block is fossil from before the eject; it compensated for a managed workflow that no longer exists. Bedrock = ONE source of truth. Two honest shapes: (a) delete `expo.ios.infoPlist`/`expo.ios.entitlements` from app.json and add `"ios"` + `"android"` to a `.easignore`/prebuild guard so the bare dirs are authoritative and prebuild is a documented no-go; or (b) go the other way — reinstate the plugins and express the custom native code as local config plugins. (a) is far cheaper and matches how the repo already works. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 (three defences incl. a CI gate, MUTATION-PROVED RED twice) | plutil -p Info.plist vs cat app.json, key-by-key

F1104 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Supporting/Expo.plist:1-6 + apps/mobile/eas.json (channel per profile) + apps/mobile/package.json (expo-updates ~29.0.9) | THE OTA UPDATE LANE IS THREE-WAY INCOHERENT AND WHOLLY INERT. Expo.plist says `EXUpdatesEnabled = false`, `EXUpdatesCheckOnLaunch = ALWAYS`, `EXUpdatesLaunchWaitMs = 0`, and `EXUpdatesSDKVersion = "49.0.0"` — while `package.json` is on `expo ~54.0.0`, i.e. the declared updates SDK version is FIVE MAJORS stale. Meanwhile every eas.json profile declares a `channel` (development/preview/production), which is meaningful ONLY if updates are enabled, and `expo-updates` is a production dependency with ZERO JS references (repo-wide grep for `expo-updates`/`Updates.` in `src/` returns nothing). So: a dependency is paid for, a channel is configured, a check-on-launch policy is set, and none of it can ever run. Failure scenario: someone ships a hotfix expecting OTA delivery and it never reaches a device; or updates get flipped on and the stale `EXUpdatesSDKVersion` makes the server serve the wrong manifest. Ladder: `EXUpdatesEnabled=false` is the honest current state (no OTA); the channels and the stale SDK version are the residue. Bedrock: decide once — either OTA is a lane (enable it, fix the SDK version, use `Updates.checkForUpdateAsync` deliberately) or it is not (drop `expo-updates`, drop the `channel` keys, drop Expo.plist's dead keys). | ESCALATED (owner decision on whether OTA is a lane; the incoherence is a defect either way) | plutil -p Expo.plist; grep -rn "expo-updates|Updates\." src → 0 hits; package.json read

F1105 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist (NSAppTransportSecurity) | `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads = true` GLOBALLY, shipped in the release binary, alongside an `NSExceptionDomains` entry for `localhost` that already grants exactly what dev needs. The blanket flag is therefore REDUNDANT with the narrow exception sitting next to it — the narrow one is the evidence that the broad one is unnecessary. Failure scenario: (a) every http:// URL the app touches becomes silently permissible, so a cleartext regression (an api base URL, an image host, a redirect) ships undetected instead of failing loudly in dev; (b) App Store review flags a blanket ATS exemption and asks for written justification, which we do not have. Ladder: added to let the sim reach `http://localhost:3000`. Bedrock = keep the `localhost` exception, delete `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads`. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 (NSAllowsLocalNetworking + the narrow localhost exception, not the proposed bare deletion) | plutil -p Info.plist

F1106 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist (3 location keys) vs apps/mobile/src | OVER-DECLARED LOCATION PERMISSIONS. Info.plist declares all three of `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription`, `NSLocationAlwaysUsageDescription` and `NSLocationAlwaysAndWhenInUseUsageDescription`, all with the same generic string "Allow $(PRODUCT_NAME) to access your location". But the app never asks for Always/background: repo-wide grep of `src/` for `requestBackgroundPermissionsAsync` and `startLocationUpdatesAsync` returns ZERO, there is no `UIBackgroundModes` key, and no location background entitlement. Failure scenario: App Store review treats declared Always-usage as an intent to track in the background and asks why, or worse the reviewer sees a purpose string that does not describe a real feature — a rejection class that costs a full review cycle. Secondary: the string itself is the template default and explains no benefit to the user, which measurably depresses grant rates on the one permission this product cannot function without. Ladder: template default. Bedrock = declare exactly the permissions requested (WhenInUse only), with a string that names the benefit ("…so Crave can show what's good near you"). | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 | plutil -p Info.plist + grep of the background-location APIs in src/ → 0

F1107 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist (no NSPhotoLibrary\* key) vs apps/mobile/src/overlays/PostPhotosFunnelHost.tsx:119 | NO `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` / `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription` ANYWHERE, while `ImagePicker.launchImageLibraryAsync(...)` IS called on the post-photos path. Honest nuance, stated so this is not overclaimed: expo-image-picker 17 goes through `PHPickerViewController` by default, which is out-of-process and needs NO permission and NO usage string — and the code never calls `requestMediaLibraryPermissionsAsync`, which is consistent with the PHPicker path. So this is very likely NOT a runtime crash today. It IS a live risk on two edges: (a) any option change that drops to the legacy library path, or any future save-to-camera-roll, crashes instantly on a missing usage string — the single most common iOS launch-blocker; (b) Apple's binary analysis flags linked photo-library APIs with no declared purpose. Ladder: the key was never needed because PHPicker was always the path. Bedrock = declare the purpose string for any media API the binary links, whether or not today's code path prompts. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 | grep launchImageLibraryAsync/requestMediaLibraryPermissionsAsync in src/; plutil -p Info.plist

F1108 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy | THE PRIVACY MANIFEST DECLARES `NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes = []` — i.e. "this app collects nothing" — which is false. The app collects precise location (expo-location, the entire product premise), an email/identity via Clerk, user-generated photos and text (posts, polls), and ships crash/diagnostic data to Sentry (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` is set in .env.local and `src/observability/crash-reporting.ts` exists). The four `NSPrivacyAccessedAPITypes` entries (file timestamp, UserDefaults, disk space, boot time) are the auto-generated SDK set and look right; the COLLECTED-data half was simply never filled in. Failure scenario: this is an App Store submission blocker / post-submission rejection — Apple cross-checks the manifest against the App Privacy answers, and a mismatch between "collects nothing" and a location-first social app is the loudest possible discrepancy. Ladder: the file is the Expo template's; it was correct when the app collected nothing. Bedrock = the manifest enumerates every collected type with its purpose and linkage, and is reviewed whenever a data-touching dependency is added. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 (ten entries, each verified against a call site) | plutil -p PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy; grep of expo-location/Clerk/Sentry usage

F1109 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist (UIRequiredDeviceCapabilities) | `UIRequiredDeviceCapabilities = ["armv7"]` on an app whose minimum is iOS 15.1 (Podfile + Podfile.properties.json + app.json all say 15.1). No iOS 15 device is armv7 — 32-bit ARM died at iOS 10. The declaration is meaningless-to-wrong (`arm64` is the truthful value, and for a 15.1 floor the key can simply be dropped). Low blast radius, but it is a lie in the shipping manifest and the exact kind of template residue that makes reviewers look harder at everything else. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 | plutil -p Info.plist + Podfile `platform :ios, '15.1'`

F1110 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/android/\*\* (34 tracked files, ~11,150 lines of Java incl. SearchMapRenderControllerModule.java at 9,380 lines) | AN ENTIRE SECOND NATIVE PLATFORM THAT CANNOT BE BUILT AND HAS NOT BEEN TOUCHED IN SEVEN WEEKS. Evidence, three independent legs: (1) LAST COMMIT to `apps/mobile/android` is `e2654b211 2026-06-17 "feat(map): #1 port residency + dormant-layers model to Android native module"` — while `ios/cravesearch` was last touched 2026-08-02. The iOS map has moved continuously since; the Java port is frozen at a mid-port commit whose own message says "#1". (2) NO BUILD LANE: eas.json has FOUR profiles and not one `android` key; nothing in `.github/workflows` or `scripts/` invokes `gradlew`. (3) IT WOULD NOT COMPILE ANYWAY: `@rnmapbox/maps` needs a secret `MAPBOX_DOWNLOADS_TOKEN` to resolve the Mapbox SDK from Mapbox's private maven, and grep finds no such property in `android/gradle.properties`, `android/build.gradle`, or anywhere else in the repo. So ~11k lines of hand-written Java — including a 9,380-line mirror of the owner-locked map controller — sit in the tree with no compiler, no test, and no CI ever having looked at them. Failure scenario: this is not inert like dead code; it is ACTIVELY MISLEADING. Every future map change now presents as "and port it to Android", every grep for a map symbol returns two answers, and the Java copy silently drifts further from the Swift truth with each iOS commit. The `scripts/perf-scenario-parity-contracts.js` file suggests parity was once meant to be enforced; it is not enforced here. Ladder: the port was a real bet on a second platform. Bedrock = either Android is a supported platform (then it needs a build profile, a download token, and CI that compiles it — otherwise "supported" is unfalsifiable), or it is not (then the port belongs in git history, which loses nothing, and the tree stops lying). Red-team of the delete option: "we'll need it when we go Android" — but a 7-week-stale port of a surface that changed daily is not a head start, it is a diff to redo; git history preserves it exactly as well as the working tree does, at zero ongoing cost. Red-team of the keep option: it is defensible ONLY if a CI job compiles it, which is a real (small) cost and would immediately surface how far it has drifted. | OWNER-DECISION (delete-to-history vs fund a build lane; either is coherent, the status quo is not) | git log -1 on both paths; grep gradlew across .github/scripts; grep MAPBOX_DOWNLOADS_TOKEN repo-wide → 0; wc -l on the Java tree

F1111 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/Podfile:17 + apps/mobile/ios/Podfile.lock | THE MAP SDK IS PINNED TO A RELEASE CANDIDATE. `$RNMapboxMapsVersion = '= 11.26.0-rc.1'`, and Podfile.lock confirms it resolved through: MapboxMaps 11.26.0-rc.1, MapboxCoreMaps 11.26.0-rc.1, MapboxCommon 24.26.0-rc.1. The Podfile comment is exemplary about WHY (unlocks `ViewAnnotation.enableSymbolLayerCollision` + `variableAnchors` so our labels win over the basemap) — that is a real, load-bearing capability for the shipped map, and this pin is NOT casual. But an `-rc.1` is by definition unsupported for production: Mapbox can pull the tag, and any bug in it has no support path. NOTE: pin and lock AGREE (no drift), and the patch in `patches/@rnmapbox+maps+10.3.1.patch` targets rnmapbox 10.3.1 which is also what the lock resolves — the whole map dependency triangle is internally consistent. This finding is a WATCH, not a defect. | OWNER-DECISION (move to 11.26.0 final once it ships and re-verify the two APIs; nothing to do until then) | grep of Podfile.lock; patch header vs package.json @rnmapbox/maps 10.3.1

F1112 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/UIFrameSamplerBridge.m (131 lines) | ONE FILE NAMED AFTER ONE MODULE IS THE OBJ-C BRIDGE FOR NINE. `UIFrameSamplerBridge.m` declares `RCT_EXTERN_MODULE` for UIFrameSampler, SearchMapRenderController, PresentationCommandExecutor, CraveBottomSheetHostViewManager, SearchRouteSheetNavExclusionMaskViewManager, SearchRouteNavSilhouetteHostViewManager, SearchChromeNativeHitTargetRegistry, SearchChromeNativeHitTargetSurfaceManager and SearchChromeScalarSurfaceRegistry — every Swift native module in the app. Failure scenario: the name is a lie, so someone adding a Swift module has no way to guess where its bridge goes and writes a second bridge file (or, more likely, forgets the bridge entirely and gets a silent "module not found" at runtime — the Swift side compiles fine either way, which is what makes this class of mistake expensive). Also: because the method signatures live here and the implementations live in `.swift`, an argument-list edit on one side and not the other is a RUNTIME failure, not a compile error, and nothing in the tree pairs them. Ladder: the file was born correct (UIFrameSampler was the first Swift module) and accreted. Bedrock = one `<Module>Bridge.m` next to each `<Module>.swift`, so the pair is visible and a missing bridge is obvious. | ESCALATED (mechanical split into 9 files; zero behavior change, and it makes the bridge/impl pairing enforceable by eye) | read the file in full

F1113 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/ProfilePresentationTransactionExecutor.swift:8-30,36-58 | THE TWO CROSS-MODULE COMMAND HATCHES ARE WIRED BY STRING REFLECTION AND FAIL SILENTLY-ISH. `dispatchProfilePresentationCameraCommand` and `dispatchBottomSheetCommand` both do `NSClassFromString("rnmapbox_maps.ProfilePresentationCameraHostRegistryBridge") ?? NSClassFromString(...)` + `NSSelectorFromString("sharedBridge")` + `perform(NSSelectorFromString("dispatchCommand:"))`, guarded by `responds(to:)` and returning `false` on any miss. BANKING CHECK (this is why `BottomSheetHostRegistryBridge` is NOT dead despite ZERO JS references — it is reached only through this reflection): confirmed live, `CraveBottomSheetHostView.swift:60-79` defines it and this executor is its only caller. So: not dead, but coupled by four string literals across a module boundary (one of them into a PATCHED node_module — `patches/@rnmapbox+maps+10.3.1.patch` adds the very `ProfilePresentationCameraHostRegistryBridge` this looks up). Failure scenario: a rename on either side, or a patch that fails to apply after a `yarn install`, turns the lookup into a `false` return — and the mangled-name fallback means even the Swift module-name prefix (`cravesearch.`, `rnmapbox_maps.`) is a hardcoded assumption. The caller gets `false`; whether that surfaces to the user as anything but "the camera command didn't happen" depends on the JS. Ladder: reflection is the honest tool for reaching INTO a patched pod (no header to import). For `BottomSheetHostRegistryBridge`, which lives in the SAME target, it is NOT — that one can be a direct Swift call. Bedrock = direct call for same-target (compiler-enforced), reflection only across the pod boundary, and that one boundary gets a startup assertion (or a `@_silgen_name`-free protocol declared in the bridging header) so a broken patch fails LOUDLY at launch instead of degrading to a no-op. | FIXED (D44 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1119 (comments at both ends, no behaviour change, as ruled) | read both functions + CraveBottomSheetHostView.swift:60-79; JS grep for BottomSheetHostRegistryBridge → 0 files (banked as reflection-reached, NOT dead)

F1114 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/\*.swift + UIFrameSamplerBridge.m + Info.plist (RCTNewArchEnabled=true) + android/gradle.properties (newArchEnabled=true) + ios/Podfile.properties.json (newArchEnabled: "true") | THE NEW ARCHITECTURE IS ON, AND ONE HUNDRED PERCENT OF OUR ~16k LINES OF NATIVE SURFACE IS LEGACY-BRIDGE. Every module is `RCT_EXTERN_MODULE`/`RCTEventEmitter`/`RCTViewManager` (F1112 lists all nine) plus three `RCT_EXPORT_MODULE` modules in TrackScrollKit — there is not one codegen spec, not one TurboModule, not one Fabric component in the app's own code. On RN 0.81 with Fabric enabled these run through the interop layers, which WORK (the app ships), so this is not a bug. It is the territory's strategic debt, and it is the honest answer to "what is the ideal shape here": every one of these boundaries is currently untyped (an `NSDictionary` payload on both sides, F1112's signature-drift hazard), synchronous-promise-shaped where a TurboModule could be truly sync, and outside codegen's compile-time contract. Coupled with F1100 (no test target) it means the ENTIRE native surface — 16k lines, minus the 398 lines MapLodKit covers — has no compile-time contract with JS and no test. Note the shape of the exception, because it is the model: MapLodKit was extracted precisely so the decision logic could be typed and tested without a simulator, and it has 41 green tests. Ladder: the bridge modules predate the new-arch flip; the interop layer compensates. Bedrock = the interop layer is a migration aid, not a destination; the boundaries that carry real payloads (SearchChromeScalarSurfaceRegistry's 10 methods, the render controller's 11) want codegen specs. Explicitly NOT proposed for the map controller, which is owner-locked. | OWNER-DECISION (a real migration, correctly sequenced AFTER the map is untouched-stable; recorded here as the territory's ideal, not as work to schedule) | read all RCT_EXTERN_MODULE declarations; RCTNewArchEnabled/newArchEnabled in three config files

F1115 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/MapLodKit/** (Package.swift, MapLodKit.podspec, Sources/MapLodKit/{LodEngine.swift:284, ScreenSpaceVisibility.swift:114}, Tests/**) | IDEAL-VERIFIED — this is the standard the rest of the native territory should meet, and it is worth naming precisely because the surrounding territory does not meet it. EXECUTED: `swift test` → **41 tests, 0 failures, 0.017s**, no simulator, no Mapbox, no React. Four properties make it work: (1) the PURE half is extracted and the impure half is INJECTED — `ScreenSpaceVisibility` takes an already-projected point + its round-trip and decides, so the Mapbox projection stays in the controller and the decision is testable; (2) the podspec consumes `Sources/MapLodKit/**/*.swift` — the SAME source the tests cover, stated in the Podfile comment as "no drift, no parallel copy" (contrast F1110, which is exactly a parallel copy); (3) the headers explain the PHYSICS and the invariant, not the mechanics — LodEngine's wall-clock fade documents WHY convergence is frame-rate independent ("a starved 12fps link lands on the SAME curve as a 60fps link (no dt-rate staircase)") and why `want` is recomputed from scratch every frame ("stale/stuck promotions are structurally impossible"); (4) the guards are justified by a physical claim — the round-trip tolerance exists because "a coordinate behind the camera or over the horizon projects to a bogus point that, unprojected, diverges by whole degrees". Nothing proposed. | - | `swift test` executed, 41/41 green; both sources read in full

F1116 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/cravesearch/Info.plist (CFBundleDisplayName, UIUserInterfaceStyle) + apps/mobile/app.json:3-4 | TWO USER-FACING CONSTANTS THAT LOOK LIKE DEFAULTS RATHER THAN CHOICES. (1) `CFBundleDisplayName = "crave-search"` is the HOME SCREEN NAME — lowercase, hyphenated, a slug. app.json agrees (`name: "crave-search"`, `slug: "crave-search-mobile"`), so it is consistent, but it is a developer identifier used as a brand surface; the product is "Crave" everywhere else in the repo. Home-screen name is also length-clipped (~12 chars), so "crave-search" is at the edge. (2) `UIUserInterfaceStyle = "Light"` force-locks the app out of dark mode system-wide, matching app.json's `userInterfaceStyle: "light"`. That may well be deliberate (the map and its labels are tuned for a light basemap, per the owner-locked map work) — but it is nowhere STATED as a decision, so it is indistinguishable from a template default, and the next person to add a dark theme will not know they are fighting a plist. | OWNER-DECISION (rename the display name before submission; if light-lock is intentional, say so in a comment/CLAUDE.md so it survives) | plutil -p Info.plist + app.json

F1117 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/Podfile.lock + absence of any Mapbox download credential in the repo or ~/.netrc | `pod install` IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE ON A CLEAN MACHINE. Mapbox's iOS SDK ships from a private maven/CocoaPods endpoint that requires a secret download token, conventionally in `~/.netrc`. Verified: no `~/.netrc` on this machine, and no `MAPBOX_DOWNLOADS_TOKEN`/`MGLMapboxAccessToken`/netrc reference in the Podfile, in `apps/mobile/android/gradle.properties`, or anywhere in the tree. Podfile.lock nonetheless pins MapboxMaps 11.26.0-rc.1 with checksums — so the pods were fetched once, and the working setup depends on a locally cached `Pods/` directory plus a credential nobody has written down. Failure scenario: a fresh clone, a new machine, an EAS build worker, or anyone who runs `pod deintegrate` gets a 401 from Mapbox with no documented recovery — and the failure will read as "the map dependency is broken", not "you are missing a credential". Same class as the Android token gap in F1110, one platform over. Related but separate and NOT a defect: the runtime PUBLIC access token is correctly set from JS (`MapboxGL.setAccessToken` at `src/screens/Search/runtime/shared/use-search-root-map-bootstrap-runtime.ts:52` and `src/components/BackdropMapScene.tsx:36`) rather than baked into Info.plist — that is the right shape. Ladder: the token was typed into a machine once. Bedrock = the credential requirement is DOCUMENTED (service-access skill has a Mapbox entry per MEMORY) and the setup script fails with a pointer to it, so a missing secret is a named error and not a mystery. | ESCALATED (documentation + a preflight check in the pod-install path; do NOT commit the token) | grep MAPBOX_DOWNLOADS_TOKEN/netrc repo-wide → 0; `test -f ~/.netrc` → absent; Podfile.lock has full Mapbox checksums

F1118 | mobile-native | apps/mobile/ios/TrackScrollKit/Sources/{TrackScrollPhysics.m, TrackShellSlot.h, TrackShellSlot.m} | DIVERGENCE NOTE, NO JUDGMENT — these three files carry ANOTHER SESSION'S UNCOMMITTED EDITS (`git diff --stat`: 48 insertions, 491 deletions across the three; TrackShellSlot.h/.m are being substantially removed). Reviewed at git HEAD as instructed; the in-flight work is deliberately not assessed and no proposal is made against it. What IS recorded for the next pass, from HEAD: the pod exports THREE bridge modules from two files — `TrackScrollPhysics` (bare `RCT_EXPORT_MODULE()`, name derived from the class), `TrackShellSlot` and `TrackTouchCarve` (both `RCT_EXPORT_MODULE(<name>)` in `TrackShellSlot.m`) — and all three ARE referenced from JS (1-2 files each), so none is a dead export. The podspec is honest and self-documenting in the MapLodKit style (it names the design doc, `plans/page-composition-from-scratch-design.md` sec 8, and states WHY the hatch exists: "the ballistic lower bound is engine-known before deceleration is configured"). Unlike MapLodKit, TrackScrollKit has NO test target — it `s.dependency 'React-Core'`, so it cannot be a host-testable Swift package as-is; whether a pure sub-kernel is extractable the way ScreenSpaceVisibility was is the open question, and it should be asked AFTER the in-flight edits land. | DEFERRED (re-review after the other session commits; do not touch) | git diff --stat; git show HEAD:<paths>; RCT_EXPORT_MODULE grep; JS name grep

F1119 | mobile-native | (D44 PHASE-3 OUTCOME RECORD — supersedes the ESCALATED status on F1100, F1102, F1103, F1105, F1106, F1107, F1108, F1109, F1113, and on F1070/F1072 from D45) | WHAT LANDED, WITH PROOFS. **F1100 PART-FIXED, AND ONE HALF OF THE FINDING WAS ALREADY WRONG.** The design said MapLodKit's `swift test` "is run by nothing" — FALSE at HEAD: a `native-tests` macOS job running `swift test --package-path apps/mobile/ios/MapLodKit` was committed in the D37 pass (`git show HEAD:.github/workflows/ci.yml` → 2 hits for `native-tests`). So the CI half needed nothing. The scheme half was real and is fixed: the `<TestableReference>` naming BlueprintIdentifier `00E356ED1AD99517003FC87E` / `cravesearchTests` is DELETED (re-verified before editing: both greps 0 in project.pbxproj), leaving an empty `<Testables>` so `xcodebuild test -scheme cravesearch` reports "no tests" instead of failing on a missing buildable. `xmllint --noout` OK. **NO XCTest TARGET WAS CREATED, deliberately** — see F1120(a). MUTATION-PROVED the lane can go RED, which was the actual ask: changing `if out.count == budget { break }` to `budget + 1` in `LodEngine.promotedInOrder` turned **15 of 41 tests red, exit 1**; restored byte-for-byte from a backup, `git diff` on MapLodKit clean, 41/41 green, exit 0. **F1108 FIXED (submission gate).** `NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes` went from `[]` — "this app collects nothing" — to ten entries, each verified against a call site BEFORE being declared, with the evidence written into the file as a comment: EmailAddress/Name/UserID (Clerk: EmailAuthModal.tsx, useClerkOAuth.ts, displayName/username rendered across profile+poll surfaces), DeviceID (Expo push token, AuthProvider.tsx:226), PreciseLocation (expo-location, foreground only — re-verified ZERO `requestBackgroundPermissionsAsync`/`startLocationUpdatesAsync`), PhotosorVideos (launchImageLibraryAsync at PostPhotosFunnelHost.tsx:119 + EditProfilePanel.tsx:147, plus expo-camera), OtherUserContent, SearchHistory (services/search.ts POSTs/GETs /history/{restaurants,foods}/viewed — server-persisted, not device-local), PurchaseHistory (RevenueCat configured `appUserID: clerkUserId`, services/purchases.ts:85). CrashData is the ONLY `Linked=false`, and that is a contract the code ENFORCES, not an assumption — crash-reporting.ts sets `sendDefaultPii:false`, calls `Sentry.setUser` nowhere, and deletes `event.user` in `beforeSend`; the manifest comment says out loud to flip it if that changes. Every `Tracking` flag false; `NSPrivacyTracking` unchanged. **F1105 FIXED, and NOT as the proposed one-key deletion.** The design said "delete `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads`, keep the localhost exception". A bare deletion would have broken Metro over a LAN ip on a physical dev device (the `development-device` profile), which the localhost exception does not cover — so the blanket flag is replaced by `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` PLUS the pre-existing narrow `localhost` exception. Public-internet cleartext now fails loudly; local dev is unaffected. **F1106 FIXED** — both Always keys deleted (re-verified: zero background-location API calls, no `UIBackgroundModes`, no background entitlement), and the surviving When-In-Use string rewritten from the template default to a benefit ("…so it can show what's good to eat near you"), which is the half of F1106 that affects grant rate. **F1107 FIXED** — `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` added with a comment stating plainly that today's PHPicker path does NOT need it and why it is declared anyway (linked APIs are scanned; the legacy-library fallback crashes instantly without it). **F1109 FIXED** — `UIRequiredDeviceCapabilities = ["armv7"]` deleted with a comment recording why it is not simply corrected to arm64 (a 15.1 floor makes the key unnecessary). `plutil -lint` OK on Info.plist, PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy and cravesearch.entitlements after every edit. **F1102 FIXED (the buildable half).** `eas.json` gains a `staging` profile pointing at `https://api-staging-25ca.up.railway.app/api/v1` (the host `scripts/rig/deploy.sh:59` already treats as staging), and `preview` now `extends: "staging"` rather than `base` — so the profile whose entire job is rehearsal rehearses against staging, per the deploy law, and the URL is declared ONCE. The baked prod URL is deliberately unchanged (F1120(c)). **F1103 FIXED, three defences not one.** A `"//"` key at the top of app.json (inert — Expo reads only `expo`) spelling out that prebuild would delete ~15k lines; a rewritten opening paragraph in CRAVE.md's mobile-native territory; and a NEW CI gate, `scripts/mobile-native-authority-gate.sh`, wired into the macOS `native-tests` job (it needs `plutil`, which ubuntu lacks). 18 checks: presence of 11 native files prebuild would erase, `plutil -lint` on the three plists, agreement between the two purpose strings app.json duplicates and the authoritative Info.plist, `expo.plugins` still empty, and the trap note itself still present. MUTATION-PROVED RED TWICE — a drifted app.json camera string and a moved `MapLodKit/Package.swift` each produced exit 1 naming the exact item; restored, 18/18 green. `scripts-containment-gate.sh` passes with it (51 scripts, all classified); CI YAML re-parsed. **F1113 FIXED as ruled (comments, no behaviour change).** A 35-line header on ProfilePresentationTransactionExecutor.swift naming all four string literals per hatch, distinguishing the two hatches (rnmapbox pod = reflection is honest, no header to import, and the live hazard is the PATCH failing to apply; same-target BottomSheetHostRegistryBridge = reflection buys nothing and costs the compiler), and warning that a grep-based dead-code sweep will delete a live bridge. A matching doc comment on `BottomSheetHostRegistryBridge` in CraveBottomSheetHostView.swift naming its one reflective caller and the exact selectors/payload keys that are contract. **F1070 FIXED (D45 lane).** `MAP_EVENT_LOG_INTERVAL_MS` 0 → 1000 with a comment recording that the FLAG was fine and the INTERVAL was broken (`now - lastLog < 0` is never true, so the "rate-limited" logger would have fired on every camera frame the first time anyone woke it), plus a defensive `Math.max(1, logIntervalMs)` clamp in map-interaction-diagnostics.ts so a future caller cannot reintroduce the firehose. Dormant-flag pattern preserved per the map law; nothing deleted. **F1072 FIXED, from the SWIFT side as the red-team demanded.** The true wire shape was read out of `SearchMapRenderController.setCandidateCatalog` (Swift, ~line 1486-1510): it requires markerKey/lng/lat and optionally reads rank, badgeImageId, activeBadgeImageId, restaurantId, `labelText` FALLING BACK TO `restaurantName`, labelSubtext, isInvisibleResident, isGroupRepresentative — i.e. the WIDEST of the three JS declarations was the correct one and the two narrow ones were the lies. `SearchMapCandidateCatalogEntry` is now declared once, with the Swift reader named in its header, and both the native-module type and the public `setCandidateCatalog` payload reference it. | FIXED (D44 + D45-deferred lane) | `swift test` 41/41 exit 0 (RED-proven 15 failures); `scripts/mobile-native-authority-gate.sh` 18/18 (RED-proven twice); `plutil -lint` OK ×3; `xmllint --noout` OK on the scheme; ci.yml parses (4 jobs); `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 from apps/mobile; `yarn test` 64 suites / 566 tests green. Nothing staged, nothing committed.

F1120 | mobile-native | (D44 PHASE-3 STOPS AND OWNER-DECISIONS — what was deliberately NOT done) | FOUR STOPS, EACH WITH ITS REASON. **(a) NO XCTest TARGET WAS CREATED (F1100's second option).** The design allowed either "fix the scheme to reference an existing test target" or "create the minimal unit-test target". Creating one means hand-writing a PBXNativeTarget + build phases + ContainerItemProxy + XCConfigurationList + a Pods test xcconfig into project.pbxproj, and it CANNOT be verified without a full simulator build — the exact "measure a stale binary / silently skipped build" class CLAUDE.md documents. More decisively, it would cover nothing worth covering: every remaining app-target Swift file is RCT bridge glue needing a host app and a simulator, which is the slow flaky lane the CI comment already says is out of scope by design. The ideal shape is MapLodKit's and it already exists — extract the pure half into a dependency-free SwiftPM module and `swift test` it. Recorded in CRAVE.md as the blessed pattern. OWNER-DECISION if an app-host XCTest lane is wanted anyway. **(b) F1071c NOT TIGHTENED — the sim recipe is recorded instead.** The identity dedupe at `runtime/map/search-map-selection-focus-store.ts` is the third member of the compare-against-a-fresh-allocator class, and the other two are fixed — but this one is on the map, and per the finding's own red-team the redundant notifications may be PAPERING OVER a missing re-selection path. Tightening it on a static read would silently delete that repair. A ~30-line comment now sits at the guard containing the exact settling recipe: sim-target + reload-dev-client (a cold launch serves the last FULL bundle, not HMR patches), a `[FOCUSDBG]` log of `{incoming, currentFocus, wouldFieldWiseSuppress}` plus the resolved highlighted markerKey read from /tmp/crave-metro.log with per-run markers, repro A (sibling pin must still move the highlight — the red-team b1f773cf case), and repro B (clear the highlight by panning/collapsing/tab-switching, then re-tap the SAME pin: if the highlight only returns because of the redundant notify, `wouldFieldWiseSuppress` is true on that press and the real fix is the missing re-selection path, not the comparator). OWNER-DECISION after that run. **(c) F1068/F1069 REMAIN OWNER-DECISION, but the Swift read that was owed is DONE and it CHANGES THE QUESTION.** `SearchChromeScalarSurfaceRegistry.swift` (529 lines) was read in full. The native half is inert in exactly the same way the JS half is, and it SAYS SO IN ITS OWN OUTPUT: `constantsToExport` returns `searchChromeScalarSurfaceActive: false`; `platformOwnerStatus` returns hardcoded `active:false`, `ownsScalarValues:false`, `composesNativeRegions:false` and a literal `missingHooks` array naming `platformReadableScalarTargets`, `nativeRegionCompositionLoop`, `pressTimeActionResolver`; `snapshotsByKey` is written by `syncSnapshot` and only ever REMOVED, never read; and a cross-file grep confirms NOTHING outside that one file consumes any registry state. So the owner's choice is NOT "wire up the missing `syncNativeSnapshot` call" as the JS-only reading suggested — ACTIVATION MEANS BUILDING THE THREE HOOKS THE NATIVE SIDE ITSELF LISTS AS MISSING, on top of the 1,244 JS lines. The only machinery doing real work today is the measured-frame KVO observers (`registerNativeLayoutObserver` on bounds/center/transform), whose output is read only by `measureRegisteredControls`, whose only caller is the dead JS diagnostics path. Deletion scope is therefore ~1,244 JS lines + ~529 Swift lines + one `RCT_EXTERN_MODULE` declaration in UIFrameSamplerBridge.m:100, and the two halves live or die together. F1069's six unwired registry methods are the JS-side checklist for whichever way it goes. Recorded in CRAVE.md; NOT DECIDED HERE. **(d) F1071(a)/(b) NOT TOUCHED** — the dead `debugLabel` and the never-read `cacheRef.version` in search-map-source-frame-port.ts are plain deletions with no native or map-law entanglement; they belong to the mobile-search Phase-3 lane, not this one, and are left for it rather than smuggled in under a native ticket. | STOPPED / OWNER-DECISION (recorded) | Swift read in full for (c); D45's own deferral reasons for (b); CLAUDE.md build-trust + map laws for (a)

F1055 | mobile-search | runtime/profile/profile-app-foreground-runtime.ts:36-45 + profile-close-state-record.ts:55-66 + profile-close-foreground-runtime-state.ts:24-27 | A WRITE-ONLY RESTORE LEDGER — the profile open HIDES the save sheet and nothing ever puts it back. `prepareForegroundUiForProfileOpen({captureSaveSheetState:true})` reads the live `saveSheetState`, sets `visible:false`, and RETURNS the previous state; `executeProfileOpenPresentationPlan` (profile-open-action-execution.ts:60) hands it to `capturePreviousForegroundUiRestoreStateIfAbsent`, which stores it on `controllerState.runtime.close.previousForegroundUiRestoreState`. The reader `getPreviousForegroundUiRestoreState` is defined, exported on `ProfileCloseForegroundRuntimeState`, and CALLED BY NOBODY — repo-wide `grep -rn "getPreviousForegroundUiRestoreState" apps/mobile/src` returns only its own definition/type/memo-dep lines, and `grep -rn "setSaveSheetState" apps/mobile/src` shows the ONLY writer outside the overlay controller is the hide at profile-app-foreground-runtime.ts:39. `finalizePreparedProfileCloseRecord` then nulls the field on close. So: open a restaurant profile with the save sheet up → the sheet is dismissed, the state to restore it is captured, and it is discarded at close. The capture machinery is a compensating mechanism whose other half was never built (or was deleted with the L3 transaction machine). Ladder: compensates-for = wanting the pre-profile foreground UI back on dismiss; bedrock = either the save sheet SHOULD survive a profile open (then don't hide it) or it should not (then delete the whole capture chain — 4 files, ~40 lines); rederive = an owner decision on which, then one of those two edits. Red-team: the "restore" arm may be intentionally dropped because a profile push should end the save flow — but then the ledger is pure cost and misleads every reader into believing a restore exists. | OWNER-DECISION (behavioral: should the save sheet return?) | repo-wide grep, zero readers
F1056 | mobile-search | runtime/profile/profile-open-action-execution.ts:37,72,79,134 + profile-open-action-runtime.ts:23-28 + shared/use-search-root-profile-map-command-runtime.ts:38-47 | `forceMiddleSnap` IS ACCEPTED AND SILENTLY DROPPED ON THE OPEN PATH — asymmetric with the preview path, from a LIVE caller. A map-marker tap computes `shouldPromoteSheetToMiddle = snap === 'hidden' || snap === 'collapsed'` and passes it as `forceMiddleSnap` into `openRestaurantProfile` (:44). `createProfileOpenActionRuntime` reads it (`options?.forceMiddleSnap === true`), threads it into `executeProfileOpenAction`, which threads it into `executeProfileOpenPresentationPlan` — whose parameter TYPE declares `forceMiddleSnap: boolean` (:37) but whose DESTRUCTURE (:26-33) omits it. It is never read anywhere in the open path; `resolveProfileOpenPresentationPlan` does not take it. Meanwhile the PREVIEW path (the else-branch of the very same marker handler, :54-59) does use it: profile-preview-presentation-plan-runtime.ts:49 `dismissBehavior: forceMiddleSnap ? 'restore' : 'clear'`. So tapping a marker for a restaurant we already have behaves differently from tapping one we don't, and the difference is invisible at the call site — the caller-must-remember class in its purest form (the caller DID remember; the callee forgot). Ladder: compensates-for = the pre-L3 machine's forced middle-snap promotion, deleted in the L3 cutover; bedrock = the openChild route descriptor already yields the promoteAtLeast-middle snap (documented at profile-direct-presentation-runtime.ts:15-18), so the flag is genuinely obsolete on the open path; rederive = delete `forceMiddleSnap` from the OPEN chain (4 files) AND from the caller at :44, leaving the preview use — but see F1057, which says the preview use is itself inert. Red-team: if the descriptor's promote is ever conditional, deleting the flag removes the only record that this decision existed; the deletion should carry the :15-18 comment forward. | FIXED (D45 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — landed as the F1056+F1057 pair; proof in F1081 | type-vs-destructure diff + repo-wide grep of all 19 occurrences
F1057 | mobile-search | runtime/profile/profile-preview-presentation-plan-runtime.ts:49-50 + profile-owner-action-surface-runtime.ts:94 | THE PREVIEW PATH'S SURVIVING USE OF `forceMiddleSnap` IS ALSO INERT — and it is a misnamed flag driving an unrelated decision. The preview plan sets `dismissBehavior: forceMiddleSnap ? 'restore' : 'clear'` while HARDCODING `shouldClearSearchOnDismiss: false` (the field's type is the literal `false`). The one and only consumer of dismissBehavior is `handleRestaurantEntryPopped`, which requires BOTH: `if (getProfileDismissBehavior() === 'clear' && getProfileShouldClearSearchOnDismiss())`. `grep -rn "getProfileDismissBehavior" apps/mobile/src` returns nothing outside runtime/profile. So on the preview path the `'clear'` arm can never do anything, and a sheet-snap-shaped boolean is being used to choose a dismiss behavior — two unrelated concepts on one name. Ladder: bedrock = dismiss behavior is a property of the OPEN SOURCE (profile-open-presentation-plan-runtime.ts:10-11 already derives it correctly from `source`), not of a sheet snap; rederive = delete the ternary, hardcode `dismissBehavior: 'restore'` on the preview plan, which with F1056 removes `forceMiddleSnap` from the codebase entirely. F1058 | mobile-search | runtime/profile/profile-runtime-action-execution.ts:26 + profile-owner-runtime-state-runtime.ts:60 | TWO GUARDS THAT CANNOT FAIL, both left behind by the L3 machine deletion. `ProfileTransitionStatus` is `'idle' | 'opening' | 'open' | 'closing'`, but `grep -rn "setProfileTransitionStatus(" apps/mobile/src` shows exactly ONE writer of a non-idle value — profile-direct-presentation-runtime.ts:60, `setProfileTransitionStatus('open')` — and the only other writer is `resetProfileTransitionState` (→ 'idle'). 'opening' and 'closing' are therefore UNREACHABLE. Consequences: (a) `executeProfileCloseAction`'s `if (transitionStatus === 'closing') { closePreparedProfilePresentation(...); return; }` is dead — the early-out that skips `prepareForProfileClose()` can never be taken, so the hydration-commit preparation always runs, which is presumably correct but is not what the code claims; (b) `getActiveOpenRestaurantId`'s `transitionStatus === 'opening' ||` disjunct is dead. This is exactly the residue the RT-2 note at profile-owner-presentation-view-runtime.ts:31-35 already caught once for `isTransitionAnimating` ("nothing writes 'opening'/'closing' anymore, so the panel-content freeze was dead") — the same rot, two more sites, not swept. Ladder: bedrock = the presence facts now come from the route stack + presentation frame (profile-view-state-runtime.ts:30-32), so the status enum should be narrowed to `'idle' | 'open'` and both dead arms deleted; red-team = narrowing the union is a type-level change that will surface every other place still switching on the four-state enum, which is the POINT, but it makes this a typed sweep rather than a two-line delete. | FIXED (D45 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1081 (union narrowing recorded as owed, not smuggled) | enum writers enumerated by grep
F1059 | mobile-search | runtime/profile/profile-panel-hydration-snapshot-runtime.ts:43-53 vs :78-81 | THE SAME FIELD, TWO DIFFERENT PRESENCE TESTS, IN ONE FILE — and one of them is wrong post-F758. F758 made `craveScore` `number | null`. `createSeededRestaurantPanelSnapshot` (:78-81) tests presence correctly: `typeof restaurant.craveScore === 'number' && Number.isFinite(restaurant.craveScore)`. `resolveHydratedCraveScore` (:49-52) tests it as `typeof ... === 'number' && currentSnapshot.restaurant.craveScore > 0`. A legitimate score of exactly 0 is treated as ABSENT by the second and PRESENT by the first, so the two functions disagree about the same field two lines apart. `> 0` as a stand-in for "present" is the magic-number/presence-conflation class; with a nullable field the honest test is `!= null`. Ladder: compensates-for = the pre-F758 world where 0 meant "no score"; bedrock = F758 made null the absence marker, so 0 is a real score; rederive = replace `> 0` with `!= null` (or reuse the Number.isFinite form so the file has ONE presence predicate). Red-team: if any server path still emits 0 as a sentinel for "unscored", this flips a display; that is a server-contract question, and F758 is the record that says it does not. | FIXED (D45 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1081 (MUTATION-PROVED RED) | side-by-side read of the two functions
F1060 | mobile-search | runtime/controller/search-root-map-surface-controller-runtime.ts + search-root-map-host-publication-runtime.ts + the three `get*Changes` functions | TWO ENTIRELY DEAD BARREL FILES AND THREE DEAD DIAGNOSTIC FUNCTIONS — banking-law verified. Every module the barrels re-export IS imported directly by a `runtime/shared/use-*` file — verified per module — so the barrels are pure indirection with no consumers. (b) `getSearchMapEngineInputChanges` (search-root-map-engine-input-controller-runtime.ts:7), `getSearchMapHostConfigChanges` (search-root-map-host-config-controller-runtime.ts:6) and `getSearchMapPresentationPropChanges` (search-root-map-presentation-props-controller-runtime.ts:7) are per-field change-diff diagnostics — 50 lines of `left.x !== right.x` — with ZERO CALL SITES (`grep -rn "Changes(" apps/mobile/src` filtered to these names: nothing). They are exported only into the two dead barrels. This is the same instrumentation-residue class D43 cleared for the map query budget, one directory over. Ladder: bedrock = a diff-diagnostic that nothing calls reports nothing; rederive = delete the two barrel files and the three functions; red-team = a future perf investigation might want per-field change attribution, but it would want it wired to a log, and 50 lines of `!==` are trivial to rewrite — recovering them from git is cheaper than carrying them. | FIXED (D45 Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — proof in F1081 | zero-importer + zero-call-site greps
F1061 | mobile-search | runtime/controller/search-surface-redraw-coordinator.ts:35-47 | A GUARD THAT CANNOT FAIL GUARDING A METHOD NOBODY CALLS. `beginOperation` does `const previousSnapshot = this.getSnapshot(); const nextSnapshot = this.ownerRuntime.beginOperation(...); if (nextSnapshot === previousSnapshot) { return previousSnapshot; }` — but BOTH values come from `createSearchSurfaceRedrawPublicSnapshot` (search-surface-redraw-snapshot-runtime.ts:47-52), which ALWAYS allocates `{...snapshot, metadata: {...}}`. Two fresh objects are never `===`, so the no-notify early-out is unreachable and a `beginOperation('')` — which the owner runtime deliberately treats as a NO-OP (owner-runtime :29-31 returns the current snapshot unchanged) — still fires `notify()` at every subscriber. So the public method AND its impossible guard are both dead. This is structurally identical to F1052f (`this.snapshot === nextSnapshot` against a fresh literal) — second instance of the same identity-compare-against-an-allocator bug in runtime/controller, which makes it a CLASS, not an incident. F1074 | mobile-search | runtime/reconciler/search-world-reconciler.ts:214-228,353-364 + shared/search-desired-state-contract.ts:311-341 | A BOUNDS-ONLY REVERSAL IS TELEMETRIED AS A PRICE CHIP. `retoggle_reversal` dispatches through `kickRerunThroughCoordinator({kind: deriveToggleKindFromFilterDelta(prev.filterVariant, next.filterVariant)})`, and that helper is a four-way if-chain whose FINAL arm is an unguarded `return 'filter_price'` — it assumes a filter delta exists. It does not always. The reversal branch (:99-109) fires whenever `cardsKey === presentedCardsKey && !identityChanged && !tabChanged`, and the cards key carries BOUNDS (buildSearchCardsWorldKey :326-331, 5-dp lat/lng), so an A→B→A **bounds** reversal (pan, search-this-area, pan back, search-this-area while the first is in flight) reaches it with `prev.filterVariant === next.filterVariant` field-for-field — and is labeled `filter_price`. The kind is the interaction's telemetry identity AND the settleMs-override seam (reconciler :313-314), so the mislabel is not cosmetic: an area reversal is measured, and timed, as a price-chip toggle. Same class one level down: `lens_flip`/`variant_rerun` always DO have a filter delta, so only the reversal arm is wrong. Ladder: compensates for a helper that answers "which chip" when the caller's question is "which interaction"; bedrock = the interaction kind is a fact of the TRANSITION (which the classifier already computed), not something to re-derive from a delta that may be empty. Rederive: the classifier returns the kind alongside the class, or the helper returns `null` and the reversal arm names the honest kind ('retoggle_reversal'/'search_this_area'). NOT FIXED — outside D45's approved list; recorded for the next design round. | OPEN (recorded, not fixed) | proof: read + `grep -n buildSearchCardsWorldKey -A22 search-desired-state-contract.ts` shows boundsKey in the world key; the helper's terminal arm is unconditional.

Rederive: drop the member + the arm (the cause guard already names itself in the comment) — one deletion, no behavior. F1150 | mobile-assets | apps/mobile/src/assets/pins/pin-score-b{0-7}-{60..100}.png (41 files, 168KB) | FORTY-ONE SPRITES FROM A SCORE SCHEME THAT NO LONGER EXISTS. `generate-pin-bucket-sprites.js` bakes, for bucket i, only the tenths in [i*10, (i+1)*10) — b0 gets 0.0-0.9, b7 gets 7.0-7.9. The repo also carries `pin-score-b0-60..64`, `b1-65..69`, `b2-70..74`, `b3-75..79`, `b4-80..84`, `b5-85..89`, `b6-90..94`, `b7-95..99` and `b7-100`: an OLDER mapping where 8 buckets spanned the 6.0-10.0 range. They are absent from `assets/pins/manifest.json` (101 scoreBadges, none of them), absent from `src/generated/pin-badge-images.ts` (1,211 static imports, none of them), and have zero references repo-wide outside audit/COVERAGE.md — so Metro never bundles them; they are pure repo weight. Ladder: bedrock = a generated directory contains exactly what its generator produces; anything else is a fossil that reads as truth. Rederive: delete the 41 files (they regenerate from nothing because nothing wants them). Sharper bedrock: the generator should CLEAR its output dir before writing, so a scheme change cannot leave residue again — that is the durable fix and it is one `fs.rmSync(OUT, {recursive:true, force:true})` before `mkdirSync`. PROPOSED, NOT DELETED (binaries; owner's call). | OPEN (deletion proposed) | proof: regenerated both sprite families into a scratch dir with the committed generators and diffed — `comm -13` (only-in-generated) EMPTY, `comm -23` (only-in-repo) = exactly these 41, content diff on all 1,212 common files = 0 differ.

F1250 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/reload/preserved-anchors.sql:12-50 (the user-anchor set) vs messages.shared_entity_id | **THE USER-ANCHOR LAW HAS A HOLE, AND IT IS THE ONE SURFACE WHOSE WHOLE POINT IS A DURABLE LINK.** wipe-city-derived.sql's law 2 says "any entity a user's data points at ... survives, id intact, so user links can never break", and preserved-anchors.sql enumerates the surfaces: poll targets + topic arrays, list items, photos, curated lists, on-demand requests, signal acts, poll endorsements. It does NOT enumerate `messages.shared_entity_id` — a user sharing a restaurant or dish into a DM (`kind='entity_share'`, `shared_entity_kind` in {restaurant,dish}, the id a bare core_entities uuid resolved through SaveableEntityResolver). The column is `text` with no FK, so nothing else catches it either. Consequence: a wipe deletes an entity a user shared in a conversation, and the share card renders permanently "unavailable" — the exact broken user link the law forbids. The redirect hop cannot rescue it: share-package-resolver.service.ts:150-153 added that hop precisely so a MERGED restaurant stays resolvable, and a wipe DELETES rather than merges, so no redirect row exists. Note the near-miss that makes this a real gap rather than a theoretical one: `user_taste_profile.subject_id` (new 2026-08-03) is also unenumerated but is safe by derivation — it is a projection of `signals`, which IS anchored. Messages are not derived from anything. | LADDER: compensates for the anchor set being an enumeration written when messaging did not exist, in a file whose OWN header calls itself "THE canonical user-anchor set (single source of truth)". An enumeration cannot be a single source of truth for a growing schema — this is the `filesImporting()` disease in SQL. Bedrock: "user data points at this entity" is a property derivable from the SCHEMA (every column, declared-FK or not, that stores a core_entities id and lives in a user-layer table), not from a list someone maintains. Rederive: a spec that reads information_schema, enumerates every uuid/text column in the user layer, and FAILS on any column not either present in preserved-anchors.sql or explicitly listed as derived-and-therefore-covered (with the covering surface named) — so the next `messages` fails a test instead of failing a user. Interim, one-line: add the messages clause. Red team: the text column needs the uuid-shaped filter the poll_endorsements arm already models (kinds list/poll/comment/user_profile are NOT entity ids), so the template exists in the file. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03) — BOTH HALVES.** (a) The `messages` arm is in `preserved-anchors.sql`, kind-filtered to {restaurant,dish} with the uuid-shape guard the poll_endorsements arm modelled. (b) The DEEPER fix landed too: `src/shared/testing/preserved-anchors.spec.ts` derives the census FROM THE SCHEMA — every reference-shaped column in schema.prisma must be either COVERED by an arm (parsed per-arm, FROM-table paired with the columns in that arm, so a name appearing elsewhere in the file cannot fake coverage) or EXEMPT with a written reason naming the anchored surface it derives from. 41 columns classified; the next `messages` fails a test instead of failing a user. | **PROOF (executed, mirror only, ROLLBACK):** built the real scope + `\ir preserved-anchors.sql`, picked a victim entity that is unpreserved AND unreferenced (`red velvet cupcake` 2b23201f-…) so the wipe would delete it, inserted a real `entity_share` message pointing at it, rebuilt the anchor set from scratch → **preserved = f** (RED). CONTROL, same victim, same harness, reached instead through `curated_list_items` (a surface the set DOES cover) → **preserved = t** (GREEN). The instrument can show both colours, so the RED is the law failing, not the harness lying. **PROOF (executed, D47, mirror only, ROLLBACK):** GREEN — victim food `bihari barbacoa tacos` (unpreserved, unreferenced), real `entity_share` message inserted, anchor set rebuilt from scratch → `preserved = t`, count 5,735 → 5,736 (exactly +1). RED CONTROL — identical harness against a copy of preserved-anchors.sql with ONLY the messages arm removed → `preserved = f`, count stays 5,735. Both colours, same harness. SPEC mutation: delete the messages arm → the spec fails naming `messages.shared_entity_id` in two tests; restored → 4/4 green. SEAM: `psql crave_search -v communities='austinfood' -v dryrun=1 -f wipe-city-derived.sql` run AFTER the edit → completes with zero errors, every DELETE/UPDATE reports a row count, `DRY RUN — nothing written` + ROLLBACK.

F1251 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/reload/\*.sql (wipe family) | **POSITIVE — THE WIPE FAMILY STILL MATCHES THE SCHEMA AFTER THIS WEEK'S MIGRATIONS.** Explicitly re-verified against every migration landed 2026-08-02/03 (timestamptz_everywhere, signals_occurred_at_timestamptz, drop_user_lists_item_count, restore_stripe_checkout_rail, estimator_state_durable_seam, onboarding_answer_history_and_taste_profile, metro_location_probes, poll_place_supply_one_authority): every table and column wipe-city-derived.sql + preserved-anchors.sql touch still exists with the same shape. The new tables carry no reference the wipe must honour except the one F1250 records (and `user_taste_profile`, safe by derivation). F1252 | api-scripts | apps/api/src/shared/testing/script-containment.spec.ts:44-58, 88-98 vs apps/api/scripts/search-harness/\_shared.ts:70 | **THE CONTAINMENT LOCKDOWN IS OUTFLANKED BY ONE LEVEL OF INDIRECTION — BY THE EXACT DISEASE ITS OWN DOCSTRING CLAIMS TO CURE.** The spec finds scripts by `codeOnly(file).includes('createApplicationContext')`, a FILE-LOCAL string test. 21 search-harness scripts boot the full Nest graph through `bootstrap()` imported from `_shared.ts`; none of them contains the literal, so none is seen. `_shared.ts` itself carries the header, which is why the suite is green. The spec's own comment says "a guard scoped to 'the files I happened to think of' is outflanked by the next file someone adds" — this guard is scoped to the files that happen to spell a particular string, and is outflanked by the next file that calls `bootstrap()`. Two consequences, of unequal size: (a) the CLASS-HEADER assertion misses 21 files — provable, and the direct cause of the 48-row PARTIAL worklist this pass inherited; (b) the CRON assertion misses them too, though the hazard is closed by luck of construction, because `_shared.bootstrap()` happens to call `stopCronsForScript`. (b) is the dangerous half: nothing makes tomorrow's `_shared2.ts` call it. The no-empty-loop-green floor (`booting.length > 30`) does not catch this — it counts 48 and passes while 21 files are invisible. | LADDER: compensates for not wanting to write an import graph. Bedrock: "this script boots Nest" is a property of the module GRAPH, not of one file's text — and the repo already owns the tool, `collectSourceFiles`/`import-scan` is imported at the top of this very spec. Rederive: resolve each script's transitive local imports and treat a script as booting iff ANY file in its closure calls `createApplicationContext`; the class header is then required of the ENTRY POINT (the thing an operator runs), which is the only place it was ever useful. Red team: the floor must be re-derived too (it would jump ~48→~69), and it should assert the closure walker sees more files than the flat scan, so a regression to the flat test fails loudly. Separately: the spec constrains only Nest-booting files, so 12 of this pass's 48 (all four `.sql`, three JSON/JSONL artifacts, two READMEs, plus photo-e2e/tile-gallery/dish-connection probes that deliberately hand-construct services) are outside ANY lifecycle rule — including the two most destructive files in the corpus (F1253). | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03).** The lockdown asks the question of the IMPORT CLOSURE, not of one file's text: `closureContains()` resolves transitive relative imports (stripping comments at every hop, and walking OUT of scripts/ — a boot helper could live in src/ tomorrow). The cron assertion and the class-header assertion both ride it; the header is now required of the ENTRY POINT (a scripts file no other script imports), which is the only place it was ever useful. The 21 `_shared.ts` consumers are visible and all 21 carry headers. NOT closed: the 12 non-.ts files remain outside any lifecycle rule — but the most destructive of them is deleted by F1253. | **PROOF (executed):** `comm -13` of the `@script-class` header census against the `createApplicationContext` census over apps/api/scripts → 48 files carry headers, 48 contain the literal, and the 48 PARTIAL rows are precisely the complement. `grep -l "from './_shared'"` = 21 files, none of which appears in either census. `_shared.ts:70-75` shows `NestFactory.createApplicationContext(AppModule)` + `stopCronsForScript(app)`. **PROOF (executed, D47):** before the header work, the closure spec named exactly the 21 blind-spot files and no others. Two mutations, both RED: (1) revert `bootsNest` to the file-local `includes()` → the new floor test AND the closure-sees-more-than-flat test both fail; (2) strip the header from one harness → the class test fails naming that file. Restored → 5/5 green. Booting count went 48 (flat) → 69 (closure), the +21 predicted.

The file is a preserved argument for a decision made in a world that no longer exists. Its paired gate has drifted with it: README publishes a baseline table (7 / 4 / 18 / 2 defects → 0) that the gate no longer produces. | LADDER: compensates for the absence of any exit rule for a data-surgery script — the same F414 gap, in the one corner where the cost of being wrong is deleted entities. Banking law first: the FINDING here (the July duplicate-pair analysis, the winner-choice reasoning) is worth keeping and is already narrated in the file; the EXECUTABLE is what has expired. Bedrock: a one-off repair is a MIGRATION — it names the exact rows it fixes, runs once, and is retired — not a permanent fixture with a warning label. Rederive: move the reasoning into `plans/` or a dated note, delete the executable, and make the destructive-SQL corner subject to a class header the same way the .ts corner is (today no rule reaches a `.sql` file at all — F1252). Banking law satisfied first: the winner-selection reasoning, the mistype-is-a-duplicate analysis, the full FK-repoint table and the 2026-07-02 defect census already lived in `data-fixes/README.md` and are kept there, now marked HISTORICAL, with the market-presence half of the winner argument explicitly annotated as no-longer-true. Banking-law re-grep for `fix-integrity-defects` immediately before deletion → 8 files, all documentation/provenance (CRAVE.md, plans/, audit/, the two data-fixes files), zero executable references. **BASELINE CORRECTION — AND A DISAGREEMENT WORTH RECORDING:** the gate re-run twice, stably, returns `{duplicatePairs:5, wordOrderDupFoods:3, ambiguousAliases:42, mistypedEntities:29}` — NOT the 5/1/2/29 this finding recorded in pass 2.
