F1254 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/validate-crave-score-fixtures.ts:50, 686-696 vs rescore-coordinator.service.ts:46-103 | **A SCRIPT NAMED "validate…fixtures" RUNS AN UNLOCKED GLOBAL SCORE REBUILD BY DEFAULT.** §12.6 is explicit and `rebuild-crave-scores.ts` obeys it verbatim in its own docstring: "global rebuilds happen ONLY through the singleton rescorer" — advisory-locked (`pg_try_advisory_lock(0x63726176)`), flag cleared before the rebuild, re-dirtied on failure, driven hourly by `@Cron(EVERY_HOUR)`. In `src/` that law holds perfectly: `RescoreCoordinatorService:80` is the ONLY caller of `rebuildAllScores()`. The second caller in the whole tree is this script, at line 696, calling `scorer.rebuildAllScores()` directly — no lock, no dirty flag, no coordinator. And it is the DEFAULT path: the escape is `--skip-db`, i.e. opt-OUT. Against any database where crons run, an operator "validating fixtures" races the hourly rebuild; two concurrent global rebuilds over the same subject table is exactly what the advisory lock was installed to make impossible. Note the corpus already knows the right polarity — wipe-city-derived.sql's own closing comment reasons about opt-in vs opt-out and picks opt-IN (`-v execute=1`) for the routine sweep. | LADDER: compensates for wanting a smoke check on real data without the ceremony. Bedrock: if a service owns an operation under a lock, the lock IS the operation's entry point; a script that reaches past it has forked the authority. Rederive: the script drives the COORDINATOR (mark dirty + one tick), exactly as rebuild-crave-scores.ts does — same code path, lock included — and the DB leg becomes opt-in (`--with-db`) so the in-memory invariant suite, which is the part that belongs in a fixture validator, is what a bare invocation runs. Red team: the coordinator's tick can decline (lock held elsewhere); the script must report "declined — another rebuild holds the lock" as a distinct outcome from "passed", or it re-introduces a green that means nothing. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03).** Both halves. POLARITY: the DB leg is opt-IN (`--with-db`); a bare invocation runs the in-memory invariant suite, which is the part that belongs in a fixture validator. `--skip-db` is accepted and warns that it is now a no-op. AUTHORITY: the script constructs `RescoreCoordinatorService`, calls `markDirty()` then ONE `tick()` — the exact path `rebuild-crave-scores.ts` takes and the cron takes, advisory lock included. `scorer.rebuildAllScores()` has exactly ONE caller in the tree again. A declined tick is reported as its own FAILING outcome with the reason spelled out, never as a pass. COORDINATED against today's `writeScores` unnest rewrite (5963aa99f): the coordinator's contract is unchanged and its tick still returns clean/rebuilt/locked/failed. | **PROOF:** `grep -rn rebuildAllScores apps/api/src apps/api/scripts` → exactly two non-spec call sites: rescore-coordinator.service.ts:80 (inside the advisory lock) and validate-crave-score-fixtures.ts:696 (bare). Line 50 `const skipDb = process.argv.includes('--skip-db')`; line 686 `if (skipDb) return` — default is to run. **PROOF (executed, D47, local mirror):** bare run → PASS with `DB rebuild smoke check not requested (in-memory invariant suite only) … "not run"`. `--with-db` run → PASS, real rebuild through the coordinator, both DB checks green. **RED ARM PROVEN:** a second psql session held `pg_advisory_lock(1668440438)` (= 0x63726176); `--with-db` then reported `Status: FAIL` with `FAIL - DB rebuild ran through the rescore coordinator`, `{"outcome":"locked","meaning":"declined — another rebuild holds the advisory lock; nothing was measured"}`. The declined path cannot masquerade as green.

F1255 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/search-harness/rt-{ballot-lane,activation-scope,complete-chunk-plan}.ts + search-harness/README.md:9-20 + apps/api/tsconfig.json:44 | **THREE UNGUARDED WRITERS LIVING UNDER A "READ-ONLY" BANNER, INVISIBLE TO THE TYPE CHECKER, WITH CLEANUP ON THE HAPPY PATH ONLY.** Four compounding facts. (1) The family README states the harnesses "read the live dev DB READ-ONLY" — a blanket claim over a directory in which these three perform 26 / 14 / 13 write statements respectively, inserting real rows into `collection_extraction_runs`, `collection_source_documents`, `polls`, `users` and voting through the REAL PollBallotMentionService. (2) No environment guard of any kind: they write to whatever `DATABASE_URL` names. Under the deploy-autonomy posture an operator with a prod URL exported is one command from synthetic polls and synthetic USERS in production. (3) `tsconfig.json:44` excludes `scripts/search-harness/rt-*.ts` from compilation, so `tsc --noEmit` — the check F414 leaned on to declare the corpus healthy — never sees them; they also use `$queryRawUnsafe` + `any` throughout, so Prisma types do not cover them either. A schema change rots them silently and no instrument in the repo notices. (4) Cleanup and the RESIDUE proof both sit on the happy path — no `try/finally` wraps any of the three — so the one run that leaks rows is precisely the run whose residue report never prints. | LADDER: (1)-(2) compensate for a red-team session's urgency; (3) compensates for exploratory code not being clean, and IS the "throwaway by convention" glob the containment spec cites approvingly as evidence the convention was already felt — but a throwaway that WRITES is not the same object as a throwaway that reads, and one glob flattens the distinction. Bedrock: a script that writes declares its target environment and REFUSES an unexpected one; a script that creates rows owns their removal on every exit path, not one; and "read-only" is a per-file property that a directory-level README cannot assert. Rederive: a shared `requireNonProdDatabase()` in `_shared.ts` that resolves the app env and refuses anything but local/dev for the write path; the synthetic-row lifecycle in `try/finally` with the residue count printed unconditionally and a non-zero exit on residue > 0; the README's read-only claim narrowed to the files it is true of; and either drop the tsconfig exclusion or move genuinely throwaway files to the gitignored `scratch/` the containment spec already defines. Red team: `scratch/` is the honest home for all three — they are dated red-team artifacts (item 5, item 6) whose findings are recorded elsewhere; keeping them costs a permanent unguarded prod-write surface for a replay nobody has re-run. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03) — all four facts.** (1) `requireNonProdDatabase()` in `_shared.ts`, called by all three writers: TWO independent tests, because either alone has a hole — the resolved app env catches a correctly-labelled deployed process, and the DATABASE_URL host check catches the real hazard (a laptop with APP_ENV unset and a hosted URL exported, which resolves to `dev` and would otherwise sail through). (2) Each writer's synthetic-row lifecycle is in `try/finally`; the residue report prints on EVERY exit path and residue > 0 exits 2. (3) The `scripts/search-harness/rt-*.ts` tsconfig exclusion is REMOVED — they are type-checked now. (4) The README's blanket READ-ONLY claim is narrowed to the files it is true of, with the three writers named. Class/home left as the owner call: they carry `@script-class: probe` with their banked findings named, not moved to scratch/. | **PROOF (executed):** write-statement counts per file as above; `grep -nE "APP_ENV|isProd|guard|refuse"` over the three → zero matches (the only "guard" hit is `const guarded = scope.documentsOwnedByRun(...)`, unrelated). `grep -n "finally"` → one hit, and it is an inner block in rt-complete-chunk-plan, not the run lifecycle. tsconfig.json:44 exclusion read. Latent, not realized: a residue sweep of the mirror for `RT-%` tags across collection_extraction_runs / collection_source_documents / users / polls → 0 / 0 / 0 / 0. **PROOF (executed, D47):** guard RED — `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://…@db.prod.example.com/…` → `REFUSED: … refuses a non-local database host "db.prod.example.com"`; `APP_ENV=staging` → `REFUSED: … refuses to run against a deployed environment (APP_ENV resolved to "staging")`. GREEN control — the real local URL → `ALLOWED against localhost`. **THE EXCLUSION WAS HIDING REAL ROT, exactly as predicted:** removing it produced type errors, including `rt-read-e2e.ts` calling `search.listRestaurantDishes(id, {})` against a method that takes ONE argument. All fixed; `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` is clean. (One collateral: adding the rt files to the program collapsed an inline Prisma-mock argument type to `never` in `account-deletion.service.spec.ts`; fixed with an `as jest.Mock` cast, assertion unchanged, 9/9 still pass.)

F1256 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/seed-google-photos.ts:106-118, 129 | **BILLED GOOGLE PLACES CALLS THAT NEVER REACH THE METER.** The seeder calls Places Details (`/places/{id}?fields=photos.name`) and Places Photo Media (`/{photoName}/media`) with a raw `fetch` and a bare `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY`, bypassing the vendor client, the rate-limit coordinator, the durable spend pool and `api_usage_ledger` entirely — 8 photos per restaurant across a default 15 top-Austin restaurants plus every restaurant in the owner's favorite lists. Both endpoints are billed SKUs. This is small money, and that is exactly what makes it a measurement defect rather than a spend defect: the cost-truth law's whole method is billed-vs-ledger reconciliation, and unmetered vendor calls land in the BigQuery export with no ledger counterpart, where they are indistinguishable from — and get absorbed into — the known ~1.7x under-metering. `publish-reconciliation.ts` then bakes that contamination into a durable per-vendor multiplier in `spend_unit_costs`, which `prepareManifestEstimate` multiplies every future estimate by. A dev-fixture script therefore biases the owner-facing cost manifest. The one-off-cost lesson recorded twice in CLAUDE.md (the $118 line, then the $0.028→$0.045 correction) is the same error one level up: summing a source that does not contain all the spend. | LADDER: compensates for a fixture seeder not wanting to boot the Nest graph to get a vendor client. Bedrock: there is one gateway per vendor and it meters — a raw `fetch` to a billed host is the gateway law violated, whoever the caller is. Rederive: the seeder acquires the metered Places client the same way any consumer does, or (if staying standalone is worth it) writes its own `api_usage_ledger` rows through the shared pricer, so ledger-vs-billed stays an honest comparison. Red team: check the rest of the corpus for the same shape before fixing one instance — `data-fixes/audit-catalog-vs-vendor.ts:64` and `data-fixes/resolve-entity-names.ts:69` raw-fetch TomTom identically; TomTom is quota-priced rather than per-call so the spend consequence differs, but the metering hole is the same. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03).** The seeder boots the graph (with `stopCronsForScript`) purely to obtain `GooglePlacesService`, and both billed calls go through it. Place Details rides the existing metered path. Photo Media had NO metered path at all, so one was built: `getPlacePhotoUri()` with the same dollar-gate-then-rate-gate order and an `api_usage_ledger` row; `photoMedia` joins `PlacesOperation` (compile-enforced, so its rate limits could not be forgotten) at 7,000 µUSD/call. Red-team item honoured: the two TomTom raw-fetch siblings are named in the file's own header as the same shape still open. | **PROOF:** `grep -rn "placesCostMicrosPerCall"` → priced only in usage-ledger.service.ts / spend-analytics / vendor-pricing and the two cost scripts; seed-google-photos.ts appears nowhere in the metering path, and its only vendor plumbing is `PLACES_BASE` + two `fetch` calls + `process.env.GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY`. **PROOF (executed, D47):** `tsc --noEmit` clean and the full suite green after the change; no vendor call was MADE (the seeder spends real money, so it was not run — the proof is structural: `grep` for raw `fetch` in seed-google-photos.ts returns nothing and `PLACES_BASE` is gone). **A SECOND UNDER-METER FOUND WHILE FIXING THE FIRST:** `photos` was absent from `ENTERPRISE_FIELDS`, so a `photos.name` details call would have classified as `essentials` (5,000 µUSD) instead of `enterprise` (20,000) — and `classifyPlacesSku` compared whole mask entries, so the sub-field `photos.name` would never have matched even once `photos` was added. Both fixed (top-level-field normalisation + the field added). Same disease one level down as the $118 and $0.028→$0.045 lessons: the classifier was never asked, because the only caller had bypassed it.

F1257 | api-scripts | apps/api/src/modules/external-integrations/llm/llm.service.ts:449-466 (surfaced by every Nest-booting probe) | **A READ-ONLY PROBE MINTS A BILLED GEMINI CACHE AT BOOT — AND THE FIX FOR THIS EXACT BUG IS SITTING THREE LINES BELOW, APPLIED TO THE OTHER CACHE.** Running `corpus-integrity.ts` (a file whose docstring says "PURE read-only SQL against the live DB; no recall, no linker, no fixture") emitted, unprompted: `Creating explicit cache for system instructions … reason: bootstrap, systemPromptLength: 79513` then `System instruction cache created successfully … ttlSeconds: 10800` with a fresh cacheId — a ~20k-token context rented for three hours by a nine-second read-only query. Gemini cache STORAGE is billed in token-hours; `scripts/lib/cost-report.ts:319-323` documents this precisely, having already been burned by it ("cache-STORAGE rows price by token-hours, and without it they fell through to the cached-READ rate — 13 cents reported as 0.2 cents"). The lesson was learned for the sibling cache and stopped there: llm.service.ts:464-465 reads "Query-instruction cache is LAZY (getQueryCacheName): minting it at boot made every script run rent a cache it never read" — the identical sentence applies verbatim to the system cache one line above it, which is still eager. The registry's cross-process reuse mitigates but does not close it (my run paid a create). The same boot also WROTE durable pool state (`gemini.monthlySpend backstop re-derived from measured spend at boot, beforeUsd 475 → afterUsd 684.34`) from a script the corpus calls read-only. Both are module-init side effects: the containment lockdown stops the CRONS a script boot starts and nothing else, so "this script is read-only" is a claim about the script's own statements that its dependency graph quietly falsifies. | LADDER: compensates for the API path wanting a warm cache on the first request. Bedrock: a cost-bearing vendor resource is acquired by the CONSUMER that needs it, not by module construction — which is the rule the query cache already follows. Rederive: make the system cache lazy on the same seam as the query cache (`getSystemCacheName()` at first use); the API's warm-start, if it is worth paying for, becomes an explicit warm-up on the api role rather than a side effect every `ts-node` inherits. Then generalize: `stopCronsForScript` is one member of a family, and the family is "script boots must not start billed or mutating background work" — worth a named script-mode that quiesces cache minting, pool re-derivation and cron registration together, since the corpus has now been surprised by all three. Red team: this is a `src/` change surfaced from the scripts territory; it wants the LLM owner's read, and the mitigation (registry reuse) means the bleed is proportional to how many DISTINCT prompt hashes get booted, not to how many scripts run — measure before sizing the fix. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03).** The system-instruction cache is lazy on the same seam as the query cache beside it: `getSystemCacheName()`, minted by the first collection call that would use it, memoised, fail-open to inline instructions. `onModuleInit` no longer mints anything. AND THE LAW IS NAMED, next to `stopCronsForScript` as asked: _a script boot must not start billed or mutating background work_ — with the family enumerated (cron registration; query cache; system cache) and the one member still OPEN recorded in place (the spend pool's monthly-backstop re-derivation still writes durable state at module init) so it is not rediscovered as a surprise. Each member is closed at its own lazy seam rather than behind a script-mode flag, because the right owner of a cost-bearing resource is the consumer, not a mode the boot must remember to set. | **PROOF (executed):** full boot log of `npx ts-node -T scripts/search-harness/corpus-integrity.ts --json` captured; the cache-create and the pool re-derivation lines are quoted above verbatim from that run. llm.service.ts:451 `bootstrapSystemInstructionCache()` is called unconditionally from `onModuleInit`; :464-465 is the lazy-by-lesson comment on the sibling. **PROOF (executed, D47):** `npx ts-node -T scripts/search-harness/corpus-integrity.ts --json` re-run after the change — **zero** cache-create lines (`grep -icE 'explicit cache|System instruction cache created'` → 0), against the pass-2 boot log of the SAME command which emitted `Creating explicit cache for system instructions … reason: bootstrap, systemPromptLength: 79513` then `System instruction cache created successfully … ttlSeconds: 10800`. The RED side is that recorded log; it was deliberately not re-created, because re-creating it costs a real billed three-hour cache rental.

Low blast radius by nature (a snippet file an operator pastes from), but it is the failure mode that wastes a debugging hour: the query is presented as a known-good sample, so the error reads as "my database is wrong" rather than "this file is stale". | trivial, zero-blast: repoint both at their successors with a dated correction naming the executed RED. Query 4 is repointed HONESTLY — `collection_communities` carries no center_latitude/center_longitude (community geography moved to the place catalog), and rather than invent a substitute the correction says so and selects the columns that do exist. | FIXED (in place) | **PROOF (executed):** before — `psql -f query-samples.sql` → 2 errors, `relation "collection_subreddits" does not exist` (L30) and `relation "keyword_search_triggers" does not exist` (L55). After — same command, **0 errors**, all seven queries return.

F1260 | api-scripts | apps/api/scripts/search-harness/{typo-replay,margin-link-eval,linker-decision-probe,decider-shape-probe,variant-link-replay}.ts + search-harness/README.md:52-75,122-123 vs search-query-interpretation.service.ts:56,561-564 | **FIVE HARNESSES REPLICATE A LINKER RULE THAT WAS REPLACED, AND CALL IT "THE LIVE RULE".** Each of these files re-implements the linker's decision in-script — by design, so the harness can run both policies without touching the service ("a faithful replica of shadowMarginLinkDecision", "the linker's EXACT decision logic — replicated here"). That design is sound only while someone re-syncs the replica. The flip these harnesses were built to gate has SHIPPED: the live decision is now `topSim >= LINKER_MIN_FLOOR && (… || (runnerSim > 0 && topSim >= LINKER_MARGIN * runnerSim))` reading per-tier floors from `linker-calibration.generated.ts`, and the service's own comment says "The old hand-set 0.82 was a category error". The harnesses were not re-synced: `typo-replay.ts:41` and `variant-link-replay.ts:43` still declare `LINK_THRESHOLD_0_82`, `margin-link-eval.ts:29` `const THRESH = 0.82` labelled "the live 0.82 rule", `linker-decision-probe.ts:19` `const THRESHOLD = 0.82 // HYBRID_LINK_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD`, `decider-shape-probe.ts:80` buckets by a literal 0.82, and the README publishes 0.82-era results as current. The worst case is the file that was PARTIALLY updated: variant-link-replay.ts imports the generated constants and uses them at :124-130, yet still titles its own output "VARIANT-LINK REPLAY (current 0.82 linker)" and prints `threshold=0.82` in its provenance line (:173-176) — it reports a number it does not use, which is more misleading than the four that were simply left alone, because its output looks freshly generated. Consequence: any of these run today measures a policy nothing serves, and reports it as the production baseline for a decision that has already been made. | LADDER: compensates for a harness needing to evaluate a policy the service does not yet implement — genuinely necessary at the time, since the whole point was to compare candidate against incumbent. The rung is that "incumbent" was written as a LITERAL rather than read from wherever the incumbent lives. Bedrock: exactly one definition of the live decision exists, and a harness comparing against it IMPORTS it; only the CANDIDATE policy is written out in the harness, because only the candidate has no home yet. `linker-calibration.generated.ts` is now precisely that home — the seam already exists, and variant-link-replay half-uses it. Rederive: hoist the decision to a pure exported predicate the service and every harness call; harnesses then hold candidate-policy code only, and a flip is a one-line constant change that every harness inherits. Red team: this also removes the calibration circularity — `linker-calibration-sweep.ts` GENERATES the constants the service consumes, so it must keep its own derivation logic and be exempted, not unified, or the sweep would calibrate against its own output. | **FIXED (D47, 2026-08-03).** ONE definition: `linkerAdmits()` in `src/modules/search/evidence-admission.ts` — hard minimum, per-tier absolute floor, singleton branch, margin branch, all read from `linker-calibration.generated.ts`. `search-query-interpretation.service.ts` calls it; all five harnesses import it and hold candidate-policy code only. `decider-shape-probe`'s bucket boundaries are the imported live ones (its 0.5/0.82/1.3 literals mis-drew every bucket). `variant-link-replay` no longer prints a `threshold=0.82` provenance line for a number it did not use. `linker-calibration-sweep.ts` is exempt BY CONSTRUCTION and says so in its own header — it generates the constants, so unifying it would calibrate against its own output. The README's 0.82-era prose and published baselines are corrected and dated. | **PROOF:** live path read at search-query-interpretation.service.ts:561-564 + evidence-admission.ts:51 (both read LINKER_TIER_FLOORS / LINKER_MARGIN / LINKER_MIN_FLOOR); grep for `0.82` across the harness worklist returns the 30 lines listed above, of which 6 are executable constants and the rest are prose asserting the 0.82 rule is current. **PROOF (executed, D47):** new guard `src/modules/search/linker-one-authority.spec.ts` — a census over the harness directory that fails on any hard-coded linker threshold in code, plus a BEHAVIOURAL check that `linkerAdmits` really is the three-branch decision read from the calibration artifact (below the hard minimum a total singleton still does not link; the absolute floor stands alone; the singleton branch requires `eligibleCount === 1`; the margin branch requires real dominance over a real runner-up). MUTATION: paste `const THRESH = 0.82;` back into `typo-replay.ts` → the census fails naming `typo-replay.ts`; removed → 3/3 green. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full suite 1415 passed.

F1300 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/results-presentation-authority.ts:109-112, 126-144 | TWO SMALL HOLES IN AN OTHERWISE EXEMPLARY AUTHORITY. (a) `publishRuntimeState` calls `syncVisualTargets(next…)` FIRST (L128), then can return early on the `enter_mounted_hidden` re-entry guard (L136-144) WITHOUT assigning `this.snapshot` or bumping `this.version` — so the visual targets have been handed the NEW transport while `getSnapshot()` still returns the OLD one, and `useResultsPresentationAuthoritySelector` (version-keyed cache) cannot see the difference. The sibling absorb path immediately above (L130-134) does the right thing: it assigns the snapshot and bumps the version, it just skips `notify`. Whatever the guard is for, the two arms disagree about whether "do not fan out" also means "do not record". (b) `ResultsPresentationAuthorityListenerRecord.debugLabel` is written on every `subscribe` (L111) and read NOWHERE — `notify` (L248-262) destructures only `observedKeys`. Its twin `ResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority.notify` DOES read `debugLabel` and reports it in the diagnostics ring, so this is a half-ported feature, not a design choice; `results-sheet-snap-stage-gate.ts:121` faithfully passes a rich label (`results-sheet-snap-stage-gate:<label>`) into the void. Ladder: compensates-for = (a) a fan-out-storm fix applied at the notify seam rather than the state seam, (b) a diagnostics ring that was built on one authority and not the other; bedrock = a store's published snapshot and the values it has already pushed to its targets are the same fact; a parameter exists only if something reads it; rederive = (a) assign + version-bump before every early return (or move `syncVisualTargets` below the guards, which is the stricter reading), (b) either mirror the surface authority's diagnostics ring or drop the parameter from `subscribe` and its four call sites. red-team = (a) may be deliberate — the guard's shape suggests "the mounted-hidden re-entry is a NO-CHANGE republish", in which case syncing the visual targets is the redundant half, not the snapshot; either way ONE of the two lines is wrong and the file does not say which. | OPEN (attribution needed before choosing which half of (a) is the bug) | full read + read of the surface-authority twin
F1301 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/results-presentation-surface-authority.ts:313-319, 357-368 vs screens/Search/index.tsx:42-48 | A `create*` FACTORY THAT RETURNS A PROCESS-WIDE SINGLETON, WRAPPED IN A CONTEXT THAT CANNOT SCOPE IT. `const SHARED_… = new ResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority()` at module scope; `createResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority()` and `getResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority()` both return THAT object. `use-search-route-results-policy-domain-runtime.ts:49` calls the `create*` form as if it were minting a per-session instance and feeds it to `ResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthorityContext.Provider`; `useResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority()` then throws a loud "must be used within a …Provider" for a value that is available from the module regardless — a GUARD THAT CANNOT FAIL IN THE WAY IT CLAIMS (the only failure it can report is a missing provider, never a missing/foreign authority). Meanwhile two live readers — search-mounted-results-data-store.ts:740,827 and use-search-root-search-scene-list-prepared-rows-readiness-patch-runtime.ts:8 — bypass the context entirely via `get*`. Contrast the file next door: `createResultsPresentationAuthority()` really does `new` per call. Consequences are latent rather than live today (one Search screen per process), but `reset()` is a process-global wipe, the diagnostics ring is shared across every mount, and a second Search host (or a test that mounts twice without resetting) silently shares state while the type system and the provider both say otherwise. Ladder: compensates-for = a migration from module-global to per-session ownership that got as far as adding the context and stopped; bedrock = one name per lifetime — `create` mints, `get` fetches, and a context exists only when the value is genuinely per-subtree; rederive = pick one: either make `create*` actually `new` (and delete `get*`, routing the two module-level readers through the context or through an explicit injected handle), or delete the context + the throwing hook and admit the singleton. red-team = the two `get*` readers are NOT inside React (a data store and a patch hook that reads at render), so "just use the context" is not free — that is exactly why the half-migration stalled, and it is the part to design rather than sweep. | OPEN | full read + repo-wide grep of all four entry points
F1302 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/results-presentation-shell-visual-runtime.ts:8-13 vs results-surface-policy-controller.ts:162-165,231-234,262-265 + results-presentation-policy-facts-controller.ts:76-79,99,122-133 | A DEAD INPUT THREADED THROUGH TWO CONTROLLERS, AN EQUALITY FN AND A PUBLIC SETTER. `resolveSearchSheetContentLane` DECLARES `hasActiveSearchContent: boolean` in its parameter type (L11) and never destructures or reads it — the lane is decided entirely from `surfaceVisualPolicy`. F1307 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/results-presentation-runtime-machine{,-state,-owner-runtime,-transport-primitives,-cover-state-transport,-enter-batch-transport,-enter-completion-transport,-exit-transport,-intent-lifecycle-transport,-intent-runtime,-execution-runtime}.ts + presentation-lane-policy.ts + results-sheet-snap-stage-gate.ts + results-presentation-shell-close-transition-state.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, ARGUED — the results-presentation transport machine is the best-shaped family in this wave and should be the pattern the rest of the territory converges on. Every state transition is a PURE `(state, options) => { nextState, appliedLog, blockedLog }` attempt: the decision, the success log and the REJECTION log are minted together by the same function, so a guard physically cannot no-op silently (the exact disease `search-flow-contracts.ts` was written to compensate for elsewhere). Mutation is confined to ONE closure (`…-owner-runtime.ts`), which is the only place `state` is assigned and the only place `publish` is called; `applyTransaction` threads a draft through a list of attempts and stops at the first rejection, so a multi-step transition cannot half-apply. `resolveActiveResultsPresentationTransportState` is a single shared precondition (right request key, not idle/settled, correct direction) that all six execution transitions call rather than re-deriving — the opposite of the eleven-hop comparator duplication catalogued in `runtime/controller`. `presentation-lane-policy.ts` is a total switch over the stage enum with a `default`, and it names the ONE known-loose gate in its own header instead of hiding it. `results-sheet-snap-stage-gate.ts` is a correct deferred-replay: it subscribes SCOPED to `resultsPresentationTransport`, latches `hasFired`, disposes inside the callback AND returns the disposer, and logs all three outcomes including the deferral — a lifetime seam that genuinely earns its file under F1012's test. `results-presentation-shell-close-transition-state.ts` is pure and idempotent per flag. The three read-model resolvers (`-runtime-machine-state`, `-panel-state-contract`, `-policy-facts-resolver`) derive presentation from transport with no second copy of the state. The one carried-forward caveat: F1033(b) still stands against `isSearchCloseTransitionReadyToFinalize` — the shape of the close-transition module is ideal, its finalize PREDICATE is not. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | full line-by-line read of all fourteen files

Deleted: both useState pairs, both contract fields, the `writeAutocompleteSuggestions` parameter and its call, ~40 call sites across 35 files, and the arg-contract entries they forced. use-search-root-submit-ui-search-ports collapsed to a single field as predicted. It is spread LAST through four wrapper hooks (`…-key-patch` spreads keys then the gate; `…-render-patch` spreads key-patch then prepared-rows; `…-patch-runtime` spreads render then status), so the value published into `commitSearchMountedResultsHydrationRuntimeSnapshot` is PERMANENTLY `false`. Two presentation gates then read it as a conjunct: `isSearchSurfaceResultsTransactionHydratedForPresentation` ends `&& !shouldHydrateResultsForRender` and `areSearchSurfaceResultsRowsReadyForPresentation` has `inputs.hasNoRenderableResults && inputs.isResultsHydrationSettled && !inputs.shouldHydrateResultsForRender`. `!false` is always true — those conjuncts are decoration; the gates can never close on this axis. F1310 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-root-search-scene-list-prepared-rows-readiness-patch-runtime.ts vs use-results-presentation-surface-hydration-publication-runtime.ts:48-51 | **A "READINESS" HOOK THAT NEITHER SUBSCRIBES NOR IS CONSUMED — AND ITS ANSWER IS RECOMPUTED AT THE PUBLICATION SEAM.** The hook reads `getResultsPresentationSurfaceAuthority().getSnapshot().preparedRows` DURING RENDER with no `useSyncExternalStore`, no `useSnapshotAuthority`, no subscription of any kind — so `listPreparedRowsReady` only ever changes when some UNRELATED state change re-renders this branch. F1313 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-runtime-stall-instrumentation-runtime.ts:12-13,106-110 | **THE F1070 DISEASE, SECOND INSTANCE, IN THE SIBLING FILE — A DORMANT INSTRUMENT THAT IS BROKEN WHEN WOKEN.** `SHOULD_LOG_JS_STALLS = false` paired with `JS_STALL_MIN_MS = Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY`. The ticker computes `activeStallMinMs = SHOULD_LOG_JS_STALLS ? JS_STALL_MIN_MS : …`, so the moment anyone flips the flag to `true` to debug a stall, the threshold becomes `Infinity`, `drift > Infinity` is never true, `stallCount` never increments, and the 100ms `setInterval` runs forever reporting nothing. This is byte-for-byte the failure D45/F1070 just fixed one file away (`use-search-runtime-instrumentation-runtime.ts:33-40` now carries the lesson in prose: "A dormant instrument must still be CORRECT when woken"), and the two files sit in the same import graph. The interval IS correctly cleared on unmount and the gating is correct (three independent enable paths, `shouldRunJsStallTicker` short-circuits when all are off), which is what makes the threshold the only defect. Ladder: compensates-for = a sentinel used to DISABLE via the threshold before the boolean flag existed, left in place when the flag was added; bedrock = a dormant instrument is code that will be trusted under pressure; it must be correct at rest. rederive = `JS_STALL_MIN_MS = 120` (the value its console-log sibling already uses) so the flag alone controls enablement. red-team = if the owner's intent was "the boolean is the only lever and the constant is deliberately inert", say so in the constant's name (`JS_STALL_MIN_MS_DISABLED`) — but a name that reads as a threshold must BE one. | FIXED (Phase 3, 2026-08-03) — the F1070 recipe verbatim. `JS_STALL_MIN_MS` was `Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY` paired with `SHOULD_LOG_JS_STALLS = false`, and the ticker's threshold is `SHOULD_LOG_JS_STALLS ? JS_STALL_MIN_MS : …` — so waking the flag produced `drift > Infinity`, an instrument that pays for a 100ms interval and can NEVER fire. It is 120ms now, DERIVED rather than picked: the same bar the sibling floor-probe arm of that very ternary already uses (~7 frames at 60Hz). RED RECIPE recorded inline: flip the flag, block JS ~200ms, the `[SearchPerf] JS stall` line must appear — before this, that recipe produced silence. | full read + comparison with the F1070 fix

F1338 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-suggestion-layout-geometry-runtime.ts:21-25 | A "PIXEL SCALE" HARDCODED TO 1 ON A PLATFORM WHERE IT IS NEVER 1. `SEARCH_SUGGESTION_LAYOUT_PIXEL_SCALE = 1`, `SEARCH_SUGGESTION_LAYOUT_CUTOUT_EDGE_SLOP = 1 / SEARCH_SUGGESTION_LAYOUT_PIXEL_SCALE`, and `ceilSuggestionLayoutToPixel = v => Math.ceil(v * SCALE) / SCALE`. The `box-none` is therefore justified by a control that does not exist. The prop itself is probably still right (an empty state should not eat sheet gestures) but its stated reason is false, which is exactly how a future reader "restores" a Retry button that the owner deleted on purpose. Ladder: rederive = restate the comment against the real reason (gesture pass-through to the sheet). | trivial, zero-blast (one comment) | full read of both

F1342 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-runtime-bus-selector.ts:19-21,44 | TWO SMALL HAZARDS IN THE PRIMITIVE THE WHOLE TERRITORY SELECTS THROUGH. (a) `React.useMemo(() => observedKeys, [observedKeysSignature])` deliberately re-keys the identity of the observed-key array off a joined-string signature — correct and necessary (callers pass fresh `as const` arrays each render), but there is no comment saying so, and it reads as an exhaustive-deps violation to every future reader and linter. (b) the third argument to `useSyncExternalStore` (`getServerSnapshot`) is `() => selector(bus.getState())` — a FRESH object on every call for every projecting selector in this wave. In React Native that path is never taken, so it is inert today; if this code is ever rendered server-side or under a future React that consults it, it is an infinite re-render, and the cached path immediately above it exists precisely to prevent that. THIRD, a class observation rather than a defect: every call site must keep THREE lists in agreement — the projection, the equality function, and the `observedKeys` tuple — with nothing enforcing it (`use-search-root-results-arrival-runtime.ts` maintains a ten-field projection, a ten-clause comparator and a ten-entry key tuple by hand). That is the caller-must-remember shape of this wave's most-used primitive. Ladder: rederive = (a) one comment; (b) pass the cached getter as `getServerSnapshot` too, or omit it; (c) consider a helper that derives the comparator from the projection's keys so the three lists become one. red-team = (c) trades explicitness for safety and the explicit comparators are currently readable — record, do not land unasked. | OPEN ((a)+(b) trivial; (c) is a design proposal) | full read + call-site survey

F1345 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-root-results-sheet-interaction-state-runtime.ts:110-141 | IDEAL-VERIFIED, ARGUED — THE MODEL FOR HOW A LATCHED-FLAG BUG SHOULD BE FIXED AND RECORDED. The `[SE-QSTALL root fix, 2026-07-10]` block is the best single comment found in this wave: it names the symptom (flags latch true forever), the mechanism (terminal dismissal detaches the results plane MID-SETTLE so the end event dispatches to the next plane's handler), the downstream chain (starved hydration publication → starved `listPreparedRowsReady` → next session's enter parks at skeleton), the repro that masked it (typed submits start a new settle cycle; the `/q` deep-link lane does not), and the OWNERSHIP principle that resolves it ("the flags' OWNER ends them with the session"). The code matches: the subscription is scoped to `getSearchSurfaceRuntime()`, latches `lastActiveBundleKind` to fire only on change, zeroes on BOTH edges with the reason for the second edge spelled out (`RT-8`), returns its disposer, and routes every flag write through the one `updateSearchInteractionRef` merge that also feeds the map-motion pressure controller. `handleResultsSheetDragStateChange` / `setResultsListScrolling` / `setResultsSheetSettlingState` each early-return on no-change before any write. Nothing to fix; this is the shape the rest of the territory's ref-latched state should converge on. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | full read

F1346 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-route-results-policy-domain-runtime.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, ARGUED — THE TERRITORY'S BEST LIFETIME SEAM. One `useRef` mints the whole policy domain exactly once (bus, both presentation authorities, map source frame port, both UI state controllers, the foreground policy domain + publication authority, both surface policy controllers); one effect attaches FOUR subscriptions and returns a teardown that detaches all four AND resets all seven owned objects — with the ORDER of teardown justified in prose (the store mirror is detached BEFORE the bus reset "so the reset-to-defaults never mirrors over the persisted values"). The failure announcer is edge-triggered on the failure VALUE's object identity, and the comment explains why identity beats a generation cursor ("it lives and dies with the bus, unlike a generation cursor, which outlives a bus reset and swallows the next announcement") — a rejection reason minted with the decision, the F1307 pattern applied to a subscription rather than a transition. The reconnect retry checks for an idle tuple before re-asserting ("the session was dismissed during the offline pause — nothing to resume"), and the session-active edge publishes only on the derived value's FLIP, with the S4e red-team note recording which deleted setter it replaces. This is the file every other subscription owner in the wave should be measured against. | IDEAL-VERIFIED | full read

F1347 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-surface-results-enter-transaction-execution-runtime.ts | IDEAL-VERIFIED, ARGUED — TWO NESTED CANCELLABLE SCHEDULES, BOTH DISARMED ON BOTH PATHS. The commit-then-slide sequencing (two `requestAnimationFrame` hops so the reveal spring runs after the press-up skeleton's Fabric mount has painted) is the kind of timing hack that usually leaks; here BOTH levels are cancellable and both are cancelled twice — once by a superseding enter at the top of the executor (`pendingSnapDisposeRef.current?.(); scheduledSnapCancelRef.current?.()`) and once by an unmount effect, with the inner rAF pair additionally guarded by a `cancelled` boolean so a frame already queued cannot fire after `cancelAnimationFrame`. The deferral itself goes through `runResultsSheetSnapWhenLaneAllows` (the deferred-replay gate F1307 verified) with a labelled reason (`enter:${mutationKind}`), and the eye-verified rationale is written down with the date and the observed symptom ("the sheet froze ~4 frames then teleported to middle"). The two `VisualReadiness` `*_contract` events here DO carry literals (`loadingStateVisible: true`, `pinsLabelsFadeOutRequested: true` in the exit sibling) and belong in the F1305 catalogue for that reason — but the LIFETIME machinery, which is what this file exists for, is ideal. | IDEAL-VERIFIED (lifetime seam); its payload literals fold into the F1305 catalogue | full read + read of the exit sibling

F1348 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/use-search-root-overlay-foundation-runtime.ts:113-129 vs its ~20 consumers | THE OVERLAY FOUNDATION RETURNS A FRESH OBJECT LITERAL EVERY RENDER. `useSearchRootOverlayFoundationRuntime` ends with a bare `return { …16 fields… }` — no `useMemo` — while every sibling assembly hook in this wave (`use-search-root-session-core-lane-runtime`, `…-state-foundation-runtime`, `…-primitives-runtime`, `…-overlay-host-runtime`) memoises its return. `SearchRootOverlayFoundationRuntime` is then threaded into roughly twenty hooks. Most consumers dodge the churn by depending on FIELD paths (`rootOverlayFoundationRuntime.rootOverlaySessionSurfaceRuntime.bottomInset`), which is why this has not bitten — but `use-search-root-runtime-control-stage-runtime.ts:136-167` and `use-search-root-overlay-chrome-host-runtime.ts` take the whole object in places, and the file also publishes `routeOverlaySessionSnapshot: routeOverlaySessionSnapshotRef.current` — a getter read materialised into that fresh literal on every render, so the snapshot field is a new value each time even when the frame has not changed. Ladder: compensates-for = consumers that all happened to depend on fields, so the missing memo never showed; bedrock = an aggregate handed to twenty consumers has a stable identity or it does not have a contract. rederive = wrap the return in `React.useMemo` over its sixteen inputs (all already stable or memoised), and decide whether `routeOverlaySessionSnapshot` should be published at all given `routeOverlaySessionSnapshotRef` is published beside it. | OPEN (low risk, but re-check the twenty consumers' dep arrays after memoising) | full read + consumer survey

F1349 | mobile-search | runtime/shared/ (wave 2, structural) | **THE WAVE'S SHAPE, RECORDED AS ONE FINDING: 264 FILES, ~17,300 LINES, AND ROUGHLY HALF OF IT IS `useMemo` RE-EXPORT CEREMONY.** Measured across the wave: ~120 of the 264 files contain no logic at all beyond a `React.useMemo(() => ({ …fields… }), [ …the same fields… ])` that renames or re-bundles values from one object into another, and a further ~40 are single-child forwarders. The deepest chains found: the local-restaurant sheet family threads ONE authority through NINE hook levels (`…-host` → `…-sheet-host` → `…-sheet-state` → `…-visual-session` → `…-visual` → `…-visual-host` → controller), and the submit ports family bundles two-to-three fields per file across FOURTEEN files (`…-submit-runtime-bus-ports` + `…-request-ports` → `…-core-ports` → `…-ports`; `…-ui-history/surface` → `…-ui-results`; `…-ui-results-presentation` + `…-ui-presentation-intent` → `…-ui-presentation`; all three → `…-ui-ports`). Every level costs a file, an args type, a return type, a dep array that must be kept in sync by hand, and a `ReturnType<typeof …>` import in whoever consumes it. This is the direct cause of several findings above: the dead params of F1317/F1336 survive because no single file sees both the producer and the consumer; the two-name/one-value returns of F1316 are invisible at any one level; F1309's permanently-false gate is only visible if you read four files in order. Per the brief this is RECORDED for the F1012 continuation and explicitly NOT collapsed here — but it is the wave's dominant fact and any rederivation of this territory should start from it rather than from the leaves. red-team = the ceremony is not uniformly worthless: the `*-lane` and `*-authority` boundaries that carry a CONTRACT (`SearchRootStateFoundationLane`, `SearchMapRenderHostAuthority`) buy real decoupling and testability, and F1307's transport machine earns every file it has. The test that separates them is F1012's: does the file own a LIFETIME, a DECISION, or a CONTRACT — or does it only rename fields? | RECORDED for the F1012 continuation (structural; do not collapse piecemeal) | full line-by-line read of all 264 files

**DELETIONS (banking re-grep repeated immediately before each, per the law — every one came back clean):**
F814 `use-onboarding-auth-lane.ts` deleted (11 lines; the sole caller now calls `useAuthController()` directly, and the `navigation` argument that was only ever `_navigation` is gone with it).
F841 `hooks/useSearchQuery.ts` + `hooks/useDebouncedValue.ts` deleted with their barrel exports, plus `photosService.getPhoto` (a verbatim duplicate of `confirm()`); repo-wide grep incl. screens/Search, navigation, overlays returned only the definitions and the barrel. ONE search entry point.

F866 `listLifecycleHarnessVerbs` demoted to module-local `listRegisteredVerbs` and CONSUMED at the `verb_not_registered` ack that had been inlining `[...verbRegistry.keys()]`.

**GUARDS THAT COULD NOT FIRE / UNREACHABLE FALLBACKS:**
F836 the `'data' in response.data` unwrap deleted — `listCitiesForList` is typed `Promise<Array<…>>` and returns a bare array, so `'data' in []` was always false. The `Array.isArray` check STAYS: that one can fail. NOT DONE (recorded): the adjacent `name` vs `placeName` vocabulary split — these are two different row types (`restaurantCount` vs `pollCount`), and `placeName` is the deliberate mobile word woven through route params, the feed store and the header; renaming it is a cross-lane rename, not a branch deletion.
F837 all three dead compatibility branches deleted: the polls bare-array fallback (the envelope is the only shape `buildFeedResponse` emits — an unrecognized payload now THROWS instead of degrading to an empty feed), the `placeName` alias (no server version emits it), and the `if (cacheRevealRequestId)` guard on a non-optional `string` whose only caller passes `x ?? createClientSearchRequestId()`.
F886 the slice-and-clear replaced by CHUNKING. It could not fire (flush at 50, `pending.size <= pendingEventCount()`) and would have silently discarded the remainder if it ever had; the server's 200 cap now holds regardless of what `FLUSH_AT_COUNT` becomes — silent loss is unrepresentable rather than merely unreachable.
F887 all four deleted: `themeColors.accentDark ?? '#6366f1'` (non-optional; the fallback hid that accentDark === primary), `WEIGHT_MAP[weight] ?? 'regular'` (closed union with a default), and FrostedGlass's `tintOpacity`/`tintColor` pair REPLACED by a `tintOverlay?: {color; opacity}` object with both fields required — the impossible states are gone, so there is nothing left to fall back from. The share host's three-clause link predicate became ONE store discriminant `resolveShareLinkMode(): 'none' | 'needs-enable' | 'ready'`, read at all three former copies.
F884 `MaskedHoleOverlay` gained an explicit `fill?: boolean` (default true). The positioning model was previously inferred from `style`'s TRUTHINESS — any style at all silently turned the plate into a zero-size top-left box. All 5 geometry-bearing call sites audited and passed `fill={false}`; the 2 style-less callers take the default. ZERO pixel change by construction, branch NAMED.

**BEHAVIOR REPAIRS:**
F805/F831/F832 ONE exported request voice — `USER_INITIATED` / `SILENT`, typed as a real `AxiosRequestConfig` so they are directly passable (the smuggled-extra-keys problem was itself the defect). search.ts's `OPTIONAL_AUTH_REQUEST_CONFIG` and users.ts's inline object now BOTH resolve to `SILENT`. F831's live defect is closed: the three POLLED messaging reads (15s/15s/5s) pass `SILENT`, so a poll can no longer defeat the banner's own recovery loop (probe clears → next tick repaints, forever, over unrelated screens). NOT DONE, deliberately: inverting the interceptor's DEFAULT to silent. It is the shape that makes forgetting impossible, but it mutes the banner for every call site in one step, and "the banner no longer appears when it should" is a regression no type or unit test here can catch — it wants a sim pass over the failure matrix (`set_system_offline`). Recorded inline at the constants.
F833 `listMessages`'s `{cursor?, after?}` options DELETED. Both were dead (the one caller passes neither) and `after` advertised a capability mobile structurally cannot use — the server's contract is a TUPLE (`after` + `afterMessageId`) and mobile's signature had no `afterMessageId`, so a sender passing `after` alone would lose or duplicate same-millisecond messages. The surface no longer implies a capability.
F834 the share slug is THREADED. `sharedEntitySlug` added to the mobile fan-out payload (the server DTO has always accepted and persisted it) and `handleSend` now resolves it — minting via `enableShare` behind the existing owner-consent modal — BEFORE fanning out. A DM'd private list no longer arrives as a preview carrying no capability, which under the "slug is the capability" law was an unreadable share.
F811 the silent shutter speaks: `catch {}` → `captureHandledError` + `announceFailureIfOnline`. Staying live was right; staying indistinguishable from an unresponsive button was not.
F812 the paywall-takeover probe gained a `.catch`. It had none, and fires exactly when a 403 just came back — the likeliest next outcome was an unhandled rejection on the wall. `.catch(() => {})` was explicitly rejected: the honest answer is the wall STAYS UP and the reason goes to the seam.
F813 five auth/money swallows routed to `captureHandledError` with `seam:` tags (token-cache read + write, RevenueCat configure, push-permission ask, teaser preview). A failed RC configure means purchases are OFF for the session and was visible only in a dev console.
F885 `AppBlurView` disabled renders `<View style>{children}</View>` instead of `null`. The blur is the effect; the box is the contract. Latent only because the one disabled caller passes no children.
F888 all three: a failed photo REPORT now says so (it was byte-identical to success); the `tel:` catch announces instead of producing a dead-feeling button on the very row whose doc promises "honest absence, never a dead button"; and the reduce-motion read FAILS TOWARD THE ACCESSIBLE DEFAULT (unknown ⇒ reduce-motion ON).
F838 PARTIAL, and the partiality is the point. All four history/membership swallows now reach `captureHandledError` — previously they passed `suppressErrorLog` AND rendered "you have no history", so nobody could distinguish a broken read from an empty account. NOT DONE: the `T[] | null` + `lastError` state modelling and the retry affordance. That second half is a RENDERING change across every card pill; adding the state without it would create exactly the write-only ledger this audit keeps finding. Recorded at the catch, owed to the cards lane.

F873 `perf/perf-clock.ts` created: SIX identical `resolvePerfNow` re-declarations collapsed to one `perfNow`, plus `clamp`/`round1`/`percentile` deduped between the two samplers. Six clocks cannot be proven to agree; one can.
F857 ONE `resolveStripHoleRadius(hole, default)`; `HOLE_RADIUS_BOOST` moved INTO the shared module and REMOVED from `resolveCutoutFadeCovers`'s parameters, so "pass the boost identically" is no longer a rule anyone can break (mismatched boosts are the white-sliver/frost-ring defect ToggleStrip's own header warns about). Spec signature updated; toggles 5 suites / 55 green.

NOT DONE (recorded inline): the `stripSlot()` helper the engine would recognize BY TYPE — it changes how children are identified, and the `React.Children.toArray` rule is enforced only by a leg-11 simulator RED that a type-level pass cannot re-run.

F890 `components/reorder/ReorderA11yControls.tsx` created: the WCAG move-to-top/up/down trio was written twice IN TWO DIFFERENT ICON LIBRARIES (Feather vs lucide) with identical logic; standardized on lucide. `SLOT_SHUFFLE_MS` and the auto-scroll viewport bands are now single declarations. **−84/+30 lines.** The container POSITIONING stays per-surface (rows: right-aligned full height; grid: a top-right chip on a plate) — that difference is real and is now explicit as a `containerStyle` prop.
F895 price levels: the display-string table DELETED; bounds are the single truth and every label renders through one `formatBounds`. `formatPriceRangeText` read STRINGS on its `min===max` path and NUMBERS on its range path, so an edit to one produced "$25–$50" beside "$25–$75" with nothing failing. **MUTATION-PROVED:** new `constants/pricing.spec.ts` (5 cases) — setting `PRICE_LEVEL_BOUNDS[2].max` to 60 turns 1 case RED; restored, 5/5 green.
F898 ONE `ScoreInfoPayload` — the two card-local copies now alias `ScoreInfoStorePayload`, the declaration that actually crosses the seam.
F853 the quiet-mode projection REPORTS ITSELF: `quietDroppedFields` (payload keys the allowlist discarded) and `quietUnlistedFields` (allowlist entries the payload lacks — the typo/rename detector). Both omitted when empty, so a healthy line is byte-identical and only a DRIFTING one grows. The full inversion (emit sites declaring their own projection) touches ~50 emit sites in the search runtime and stays with that lane; these two keys make its absence visible rather than silent.

It was the STALE mirror of three — omitting `locationId`/`locationAddress`, which the service returns and mobile consumes. Three truths → two. STILL OWED: the shared-package row (goes with F842).

[]` sites are gone — a broken response no longer renders as "this user has created no polls". Converting every raw `response.data` return across the four services is per-endpoint work and is NOT done; a new endpoint now has one obvious thing to reach for instead of a coin flip.
F839 provenance lines (or deletion) for every named constant: cache TTLs + the 25/50 MAX_ENTRIES asymmetry, the Retry-After 2000 fallback, FLUSH_DELAY_MS 50, the 120s/15s dev-vs-release timeouts. The DEAD, DISAGREEING defaults are DELETED — `recentHistory(limit = 8)` / `recentlyViewed*(limit = 10)` while their only callers pass 30 from `constants/searchHistory.ts`; `limit` is required now.
F874 the one genuine duplication fixed (the deep link's `120000` now imports `SCALE_PROBE_MAX_MARKERS`); provenance written for the snap retry budget, footerHeight, the attach backoff, the pagination throttle, the shell-audit interval, the 700ms settle deadline and `POLL_CHILD_COMMIT_DELAY_MS` (named honestly as never-measured, harness-only, and the flaky-automation class).

**F808 — react-hooks linting, STAGED AND LANDED, with a real defect found:**
`eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1` installed; `rules-of-hooks: error`, `exhaustive-deps: warn`.
SURPRISE WORTH RECORDING: the first attempt looked configured and was INERT — `apps/mobile/.eslintrc.js` already had a top-level `rules` key, so a second one silently won (duplicate object key). `eslint --print-config` reported `plugins: ['react-hooks']` with `rules: {}` and a deliberately-broken fixture produced ZERO output. Caught only by mutation-proving the rule before trusting its zero — the same "make it show RED first" law this audit applies to instruments. Folded into the single `rules` block, with the trap recorded there.
MUTATION-PROVED both rules fire (a fixture with a conditional hook + a lying dep array ⇒ 3 errors + 1 warning).
RESULT: **2 rules-of-hooks ERRORS, both real, both in `screens/Search/components/SearchSuggestions.tsx`** — `if (!visible) return null` sat ABOVE two `useMemo`s, so the component called two fewer hooks whenever hidden and every `visible` toggle changed the hook COUNT (React's "Rendered more hooks than during the previous render" crash). Latent because it is usually unmounted rather than hidden. FIXED: the early return moved below every hook. Repo-wide now **0 eslint errors**; 102 exhaustive-deps warnings left for the per-directory burn-down, as staged.
F808's second half: both ghost disables gone. `EntitlementLapseHost`'s bare `// eslint-disable-line` (no rule name ⇒ blanket-muted the line) is deleted and its now-real warning left VISIBLE with the reason; `OnboardingTeaser`'s comment explaining that no disable was needed "because this package's eslint has no react-hooks plugin" is replaced by the actual argument for its joined-key deps.

**SKIPPED-FOREIGN (recorded, not attempted):**
F905 (`transition-engine/transition-transaction.ts`) and F920 (`premount-violation-probe.ts`) were in scope as INSTRUMENTATION-ONLY. Both files carry UNCOMMITTED edits from a concurrent nav-overlays lane (`git status` at the time of the attempt), so per the pass's discipline they were left untouched rather than collided with. They remain owed.
`overlays/shell-residency-manager.ts` (F864's third file) likewise foreign-modified; only the perf files and App.tsx mount were touched.

**SURPRISES:**

1. A CONCURRENT LANE was editing this repo throughout — `overlays/*`, `navigation/runtime/*`, a new api migration — and deleted `snap-crossing-predicate.{ts,spec.ts}`, which is the entire 572→564 test delta an early checkpoint showed. Attributing that delta cost a round trip. It also transiently broke `tsc` twice with its own in-flight edits, which polluted the verification signal for a stretch (worked around by filtering to non-foreign files; the final run is clean without any filter).
2. NEVER `git stash` during a concurrent-lane session. One `git stash -u` used to establish a HEAD baseline swept up the other lane's in-flight work; it popped cleanly and nothing was lost, but it was an avoidable risk and is recorded as a rule.
3. The honest runner (F830's gate) fired twice with "A worker process has failed to exit gracefully" while test COUNTS varied (568/556/522 — suites being killed mid-run). Investigated: never a FAIL line, not reproducible, and 6 consecutive clean runs plus 4 raw-jest runs afterwards. Most likely the other lane rewriting files under a running jest. NOT attributed to this batch, and recorded rather than dismissed.
4. F871's fix silently proved the drift was real: the coordinator had been passing `collide`/`spreadDeg` into a registration type that did not declare them, and only the derived type makes that checkable.
